# MyNclex Sessions — 2026-07

Archived session log for 2026-07. Newest first.

---

## Session — 2026-07-30/31 (later still — the **review scoring strip**) — **RELEASED TO PROD**

Branch `claude/local-host-setup-f0d082`. **10 commits**, **one migration**
(`20260903120000_item_response_stats.sql`), dev-applied; vitest **784 → 855**;
tsc at the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip` errors. **Released to prod.** Doc:
`docs/product-plan/scoring-strip.md`. Built from a Claude Design prototype
Sam had already made — *"Runner Review Scoring Line"*.

**⭐ THE STRIP'S REAL JOB TURNED OUT NOT TO BE THE NEW FIGURES.** The brief
was a line above the rationale carrying score, rule, time and a cohort
percentage. But the rationale header read `Rationale · wrong` off
`is_correct`, which is **full-credit-only by design** (§5.5) — so a student
who scored 2 of 3 was told **"wrong"** with **"2 / 3"** beside it, the header
contradicting the number on one line. **199 of 715 dev answers (28%) are
partial**, so more than one review screen in four was doing this. That fix
landed first and alone, and reached the tutor-library embeds for free since
they render the same block.

**DERIVED, NOT STORED — and the distinction became the arc's rule of thumb.**
Sam proposed making `is_correct` three-state. Pushed back: `score_awarded` and
`marks_snapshot` already determine the answer, so a third column would restate
the first two and be free to drift — the shape of the difficulty *word* beside
the difficulty *number* that cost slice 10d. Blast radius measured, not
guessed: **57 TS files and 24 SQL files** read `is_correct`, and they want the
strict flag. Sam's underlying rule — three states *everywhere*, not just the
strip — was adopted in full, as a shared function. Later, the opposite call for
item statistics: **derive when the source is one row away; materialise when the
source is the whole table.**

**⭐ THE SCORING RULES ARE THE FIRST TIME THE PRODUCT TELLS A STUDENT HOW THEIR
MARKS WERE WORKED OUT** — until now those rules existed only as code comments.
Sam reworded all seven. **His Matrix-multi-response is better than mine and
shows why:** it is literally the SATA sentence with `Per row — ` in front, so
the copy does not *describe* the relationship between the two rules, it **is**
that relationship — 19 chars shorter than my version, which buried the same
idea in nine extra words. Pinned by a test asserting the exact prefix. ⚠ The
copy is **not free text**: each rule carries a `penalisesWrongPicks` claim and
the test runs the **real** `scoreAttempt` for every type to check the score
moves the way the sentence says. **Proven to bite** — falsifying the +/− claim
fails 3 tests. ⚠ Also **corrected a description of my own that was wrong**: the
pairing check is *not* "same scoring function ⇒ same sentence" — Cloze and
Drag-cloze read alike across two *different* functions, while Drag-cloze and
Drag-to-order share *one* and read differently. **The maths fixes the claim;
what the student is looking at fixes the noun.** ⚠ Rejected alternative
recorded: the notation the canonical doc itself uses (`0/1 per row`, `+/− with
floor 0`) — precise, shorter, **our own house vocabulary** — but it needs a key,
the only place for a key is a hover, and our students are on phones.

**⭐ SAM KILLED THE NAME.** "Cohort" was the prototype's word and I carried it
without questioning it. It is already taken — a cohort here is a group of
students enrolled together in a programme run, ~219 files. **Worse than a
clash, it points at the wrong set:** a tutor would read *my class*, when this
is every student who ever answered the question. The proof: **the word is
accurate for the one view where we don't use it** (a tutor looking at their own
students) **and inaccurate for the one where we did.** Same shape as "mark"
meaning two things — caught before shipping this time. Now **item statistics**.

**⭐ THEN SAM ASKED TWO ARCHITECTURE QUESTIONS THAT BOTH CHANGED THE ANSWER.**
① *"should it be derived or stored?"* — stored, because five surfaces will want
it and each would otherwise re-implement three rules, one of which (the
threshold) is a **safety** rule: below it, a percentage on a rarely-answered
question is the reader's own result handed back. ② *"why a table, can't it go on
the question?"* — checked rather than assumed, and three things ruled it out:
`nclex_bank_items` is readable by **every signed-in user** (`USING is_published
= true`), an **AFTER UPDATE audit trigger** would fill the "who edited this
question" log with nightly machine writes, and **three BEFORE UPDATE triggers**
fire on any update including the CAT seal trigger, which can raise. ③ *"are we
extending the recalibration action?"* — no, and `recalibrate.yml`'s own header
states the rule: the enrolment sweep is pg_cron because *"it applies rules, each
row decided on its own"*. Counting is that kind, and counting fails loudly. ⚠ My
"it rides free on a pass we already make" was **retracted** — that job reads
BANK only, deliberately, so tutor questions could never have joined it.

**⭐ SAM ADDED TUTOR QUESTIONS AND CORRECTED A MISTAKE OF MINE.** I had argued
the figure was structurally bank-only because a tutor never reaches 30 students
— **conflating the data with the display gate**. The threshold stops a *student*
seeing a meaningless percentage; a tutor looking at their own class is a
different situation entirely. Resolved as **two audiences, two gates**: students
get a percentage gated at 30, tutors get their own questions with **no
threshold**, because *a percentage needs a large n to mean anything and a
fraction does not* — "3 of 11 of your students" is honest at n=11, "27%" is not.

**THE SWITCH GATES THE DISPLAY, not just the job** — unlike the other two
scheduled-job switches. Stopping this job alone would **freeze** the last
numbers on screen rather than remove them, which is the wrong outcome for the
reason an admin would most likely reach for it. **The threshold deliberately
did NOT become an admin knob**: it defends against a statistical problem, not a
business preference, and an admin lowering it to 3 would produce exactly the
misleading percentages it exists to prevent.

**THE GRID GOT A SIXTH FILL**, because the strip saying "Partial credit" beside
a dark-red "Wrong" cell would have fixed the contradiction in one place and made
it louder in the other. **⭐ Amber moved from Skipped to Partial** (Sam's option
A): matching the strip was the point and amber was taken — by Skipped, which is
close to partial's *opposite*. Skipped is **not a score**, it is the absence of
an answer, so it became a dashed neutral cell. ⚠ **The tutorial teaches the
colour key in words** — *"amber skipped"* — so this made that step **wrong, not
dated**, on a public walkthrough. ⚠ The **spoken label** was built from the raw
CSS fill token, so a screen reader would have said *"Question 10, partial"*,
which does not say partial **what**. ⚠ `deriveCellFill` — which decides the
colour of **every** cell — had **no tests at all**; 16 added. `gridCounts` was
**dead code** (exported, re-exported, called by nothing) and deleted rather than
taught a sixth state it would never show.

**⚠ FOUR DEFECTS, ALL FOUND BY READING THE RENDERED PAGE, none by tsc, tests or
lint:** ① the tooltip **dropped 41% of students** — *"41% got it right, 18% did
not"* — because the single-mark branch read "did not" as the **zero** share when
it means *not full marks*, which is zero **and** partial; looks impossible on a
1-mark question, except `marks_snapshot` is frozen per attempt while the counts
aggregate across attempts ② the tooltip **totalled 101%**; the residual now
absorbs the rounding ③ the tooltip was **as wide as the screen** — a native
`title` renders as one unbroken line and no CSS can constrain it, so the line
breaks **are** the layout (185 chars → longest line 39, with a test capping it)
④ my draft copy said **"not a verdict on you"** — and `verdict` is this
feature's own word for the chip two inches to the left. One word, two meanings,
one screen: exactly what "cohort" had just taught us.

**VERIFICATION.** The migration's SQL and `verdict.ts` bucket answers in two
languages that cannot see each other, so **the test reads the migration off
disk, extracts its FILTER conditions and EVALUATES them** against `verdictFor`
over a grid of scores — it cannot be satisfied by a stale copy. **Proven to
bite**: `>=` → `>` fails with *"score 1 of 1: expected 'NONE' to be 'CORRECT'"*.
The refresh was reconciled **600/600 against an independently written
recomputation**, tutor rows included. The RPC was proven **under rollback on
real rows** — over-threshold returned, 29 withheld, another student's sitting
refused with the same message so it cannot probe for existence, zero rows with
the switch off — the exception rolling it all back, residue checked after. ⚠
Both function bodies **md5-identical to the file**, which they had **not** been:
I first applied a comment-stripped version with different dollar-quoting, the
exact trap a prior session caught only by md5.

**⚠ THE FIGURE CANNOT BE SEEN ON DEV NATURALLY** — the most-answered question has
**3** answers against a threshold of 30. Rendered by staging figures and then
rebuilding from the answers. Sam tested the admin switch against staged data and
was happy; **the staged rows were left in place on his call** and will clear
themselves at the next 03:00 cron run.

**⭐ THEN SLICE 3b, AND SAM'S CUT WAS SHARPER THAN EITHER OF MINE.** It closes a
gap slice 3 opened: once partial had its own fill **no filter could reach it**
— "Wrong" used to catch partial answers because partial *was* painted wrong,
and that count visibly dropped 7 → 3 when it was fixed. I proposed a fifth tab,
then a merged block; Sam asked **why "Wrong" is in the top rail at all** — you
do not filter for wrong answers mid-sitting. That splits the two controls by
what they are FOR: the rail is **progress** (All · Flagged · To do — three
buttons, no gating, identical in every mode), the key is **outcome**. "Wrong"
was the single correctness idea in a progress control, which is exactly why it
alone needed `revealCorrectness`. ⚠ **His suggested replacement, "Answered",
was checked against the data and dropped** — across every finished sitting on
dev that fill occurs **ZERO** times, because in review everything submitted
resolves to correct/partial/wrong; it would have been a button reading 0 on
every review screen. So Wrong **leaves** the rail rather than being replaced.
**"Dropped marks" (wrong + partial)** exists because single-select cannot union
two states and that union is what a student wants after a sitting — ⚠ *not* a
way to call partial wrong again: both keep their own row, colour and count, and
the row carries **two swatches** so it stays a colour key. **Counts are now
DERIVED from `isVisibleUnderFilter`** over one list rather than tallied per tab,
so a count cannot disagree with what clicking it shows. ⚠ **The key was
`aria-hidden`, being decoration — a control cannot be:** real buttons,
`aria-pressed`, focus rings, and **explicit** labels rather than
name-from-content, which would have computed `"Correct3"` from two adjacent
spans. "Current" stays a plain line — a *position*, not a result. ⚠ **Two stale
strings naming controls that no longer exist**: the runner footer and a
tutorial step both listed "Wrong / Flagged / Unanswered". ⚠ **I over-warned
about the coach steps** — I said two would have to merge; keeping the rail and
key as separate elements meant **neither anchor was orphaned**. Verified by
clicking every filter against the database (partial → 2,3,5,10 · wrong →
1,4,7 · correct → 6,8,9 · dropped → the exact union · clearing → all 10);
union guard proven to bite. vitest **848 → 855**.

⬜ **Not built:** a skipped question still reads **WRONG** in the strip
(pre-existing, and already settled the other way in the session report:
*"Not answered"*, grey not red) · tap-to-reveal on phones, where there is no
hover at all · multi-select filtering · the session report, readiness report
and a curator "hardest questions" view, all cheap now that the table exists ·
cohort-scoped tutor statistics. ⚠ **The runner is badly broken at phone
widths** — `.rn-grid` is a fixed 240px with no rule below 768px, squeezing the
question to ~135px. **Pre-existing, confirmed by runtime experiment**, and left
for its own session on Sam's call.

## Session — 2026-07-30 (later — **marking, built as TWO features: flag + bookmark**) — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-af47f7`. **6 commits**, **one migration**
(`20260902120000_attempt_item_flag.sql`), dev-applied; vitest **739 → 784**; tsc
at the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip` errors. **On `main`, NOT prod** — the
migration ships with the next release. Doc:
`docs/product-plan/flag-and-bookmark.md` (written first, built after).

**⭐ THE WHOLE SESSION TURNS ON ONE OBSERVATION, AND IT WAS SAM'S QUESTION THAT
FORCED IT.** BUILD_LIST said "build marking — every consumer is wired, only the
write path is missing". Sam asked *"do we have a documented plan on the mark?"*
The honest answer was: the plumbing is planned in detail, the experience is not
— `bank-consumption-runner.html` §14 is four sentences tagged *skeleton* with
"TBD: marking UI placement, mark-icon design, behaviour across re-attempts".
Which turned out to matter, because **"mark" was two features sharing one
icon**, and that — not a missing writer — is why it had sat half-built since
2026-05. **Sam named them:** *flag* = "flag this during a live attempt so I can
come back and maybe change my selection"; *bookmark* = "save this so I can see
it again later". Then: *"if the current idea can do both at the same time then
fine, else we can separate them."*

It cannot, and the reason is one line: `nclex_question_marks`'s unique index is
`(student_id, target_kind, target_id)` **with no `attempt_id` anywhere**. The
same question flagged in two sittings is refused by that index, and unflagging
in one would delete the row an older sitting's report depends on. It is a
**bookmark** table; its decision log is a good spec for one of the two
features. The flag went on `nclex_attempt_items`, already exactly one row per
`(attempt, question)`. **Separating was LESS work than merging** — merging
would have meant a new column, a reworked index, and an unanswerable question
(if Q400 was flagged in three sittings and unflagged in one, is it in the
Builder pool?).

**⭐ "MARK" IS RETIRED AS A USER-FACING WORD** — it already means POINTS in this
product (`marks_snapshot`, `computeMarksFromKey`, "a SATA question is worth 5
marks"), so "your marked questions" sat beside "the marks you scored" meaning
something unrelated. Grid chip + legend → **Flagged**; Builder chip →
**Bookmarked**. Internal ids (`MARKED`, `marks_q`, the table name) deliberately
unchanged — renaming them is churn with migration risk and no user benefit.

**BOOKMARK WENT FIRST**, against the order the topbar suggests, because it
needed **no migration and no RPC** and closed a filter that had **never once
matched a question**; the flag needed schema work and fixed nothing that was
broken. Verified rather than assumed: the pool helper already reads bookmarks
correctly — questions directly, cases via `any_child_marked` — so **the read
side needed no SQL change at all**.

**⭐ SAM SHAPED THE SCOPE THREE TIMES, EACH TIME NARROWING IT CORRECTLY.**
① *"shall we not show it for CAT and readiness in both live and review?"* —
right, and for a better reason than exam authenticity: every CAT and readiness
question is reserved stock, `_nclex_eligible_unit_pool` excludes it, and the
bookmark filter is a **CTE inside that helper**, so a bookmarked CAT question
**can never be served back**. The control would have made a promise the
architecture is committed to breaking. ⚠ Writing it found a **third** exclusion
the doc had not noticed — **tutor quizzes**, since the pool reads
`nclex_bank_items` only, so a bookmarked TUTOR question is exactly as
unservable. ② *"bookmarking is for questions only, we are not bookmarking an
entire case"* — and he predicted the mechanic before I checked it: bookmarking
one case child pulls the whole case, because a case is one selection unit.
Already the built behaviour (`case_marked = case bookmarked OR any child`), and
identical to every other filter — the difference is that it is arguably
**correct** here, since you need the scenario to answer the child. ③ *"a
question already bookmarked must not be bookmarkable again, right?"* — right,
but it is **not a rule to enforce, it is a LOAD to perform**: the runner has to
read existing bookmarks at sitting start or the control renders "off" for a
bookmarked question and the student's tap hits the unique index.

**⭐ THAT LAST ONE EXPOSED THE TRAP THAT DEFINES THE ARC.** The grid's existing
set is keyed by **`attempt_item_id`** — flag grain — so the grid was built for
the flag all along. The two features need **two different keys**
(`flag → attempt_item_id`, starts empty every sitting; `bookmark → item_id`,
arrives pre-set), both are `Set<string>`, and **passing one where the other
belongs compiles cleanly**. Guarded by naming (`flaggedAttemptItemIds` /
`bookmarkedItemIds`) and by a test asserting the two predicates **disagree**
where they should: Timed Sequential is bookmark-yes/flag-no, a readiness pack
is flag-yes/bookmark-no, CAT is the only place both are off.

**⭐ THEN SAM KILLED THE FLAG CARD IN THE REPORT, AND CAUGHT AN OVER-CLAIM OF
MINE.** His question: *"what if I flagged questions during the attempt but
unflagged them all before submitting?"* Answer: the report shows nothing —
correct, but it exposes that a flag is a **weak signal** there, failing both
ways (flagged-but-resolved-and-never-tidied; unflagged-but-still-lost). And it
exposed that **"you flagged 6 questions during this sitting" is a claim the
data cannot support** — unflagging *deletes* the state, so the report only ever
sees the position at submit. I had written that sentence in the doc, a column
comment and two commit messages. **His replacement: show what is STILL
BOOKMARKED at the time the report is viewed** — deliberate, durable, and the
only signal on that page that is **actionable**, since it feeds the Builder's
Bookmarked pool. Ranked **first** in the fix list, above the measured
weaknesses: the student's own stated intent outranks our inference about them.
⚠ The wording rule is now **a test**, because bookmarks have no attempt scoping
and one may have been set months ago: copy must say *"still bookmarked"*, never
*"you bookmarked these here"*. He also approved the inline star toggle, making
the report a second home for the pruning loop. **One rule bent to make it
work:** Builder deep links force the pool to UNSEEN so practice serves fresh
questions, but a bookmark is the student saying the opposite in as many words,
so `pool=marked` is honoured — narrowly, that value only, INCORRECT still
off-limits.

**⚠ SLICE 5 WAS BIGGER THAN THE PLAN SAID, and I created the problem.** I added
`data-coach` anchors for flag and bookmark in slice 3 — but **the sandbox hid
both controls**, so the anchors pointed at elements that never mounted and the
walkthrough could not teach them. Fixed by making the sandbox **render both for
real** and skip only the write, which is the tutorial's founding principle (one
runner in a sandbox mode, never a copy) rather than a shortcut. CD's single
"Mark for review" step became **two**; the flag step is **gated** (you must
press it), the bookmark step deliberately is **not** — a bookmark persists, so
forcing one would leave real state behind in a sandbox whose badge promises
nothing is recorded.

**⚠ TWO SILENT BREAKAGES, BOTH MINE, BOTH CAUGHT, BOTH NOW GUARDED.**
① **`COACH_SECTIONS` holds RAW INDICES into `COACH_STEPS`** — inserting two
steps at index 6 misaligned every section from Calculator down, so the jump
menu would have landed one topic off with **tsc clean and every test passing**.
Renumbered; `steps.test.ts` now pins each section label to the step **title**
it must open on, and it is **proven to bite** (it caught two wrong expectations
on its first run). ② **The coach's gate check was
`s.gate === 'calc' ? calcOpen : currentSubmitted`** — anything not `'calc'` fell
through to the submit gate, so adding `'flag'` would have gated the flag step
on **submitting** the question: types fine, tests fine, wrong lesson taught.
Now an exhaustive switch.

**PROVING THE RPC.** 12 checks on real rows: set / idempotent re-set / exactly
one row touched / unflag; another student, a fabricated id, a finished sitting
and an anonymous caller all refused; dev left at 0 flagged rows. ⚠ **The CAT
check first passed for the WRONG REASON** — dev has zero in-progress CATs, so
the status guard fired before the mode guard and the message read "this sitting
has ended". Re-proved by borrowing a finished CAT into `IN_PROGRESS` inside one
transaction, asserting the real message, and restoring its status. ⚠ **The
deployed body and the migration file had DIVERGED** — the applied version was
missing two inline comments dropped while pasting. Logic identical, text not,
and **only the md5 comparison caught it**.

**VOCABULARY SWEEP — one string was WRONG, not merely dated.** The public
`bank-access` builder demo described its MARKED pool as *"Flagged for review
mid-quiz"*, which describes the **flag**; it is the bookmark pool. A public
page teaching the exact conflation this arc removes, and the first thing a
prospective student reads. Also fixed: the grid **legend** still said "Marked
for review" (the very thing the colours step describes), the review-mode footer
brief, and the readiness demo's frame tag. Left alone as different features:
"Marked by your tutor" (attendance), "Mark as done" (library), Tiptap's
`node.marks`.

**⚠ THE DEV SERVER KEPT DYING, AND THE CAUSE IS WORTH KNOWING: running vitest
breaks it.** The worktree has no real `node_modules` — the 488 packages live in
the parent checkout and resolve by walking up — so vitest's `node_modules/.vite`
cache creates a near-empty `node_modules/` that makes Turbopack infer the wrong
workspace root. `next dev` prints `✓ Ready` then dies on first compile, and
`preview_start` reports success for a process already gone. Fix: delete
`node_modules`, restart. **Start the dev server BEFORE running tests.** The
2026-07-28 log records the same symptom and its one-off fix but never
identified vitest as what creates it.

⚠ **Verification is uneven and should be read carefully.** Sam tested slices
1–3 live and approved them. Slice 4 (report) and slice 5's **coach copy** have
**not been clicked** — the report is behind a login and the step copy lives in
a client chunk, not the SSR HTML. What *was* verified on the rendered public
tutorial: both controls present (`rn-flag-btn`, `rn-bookmark-btn`), the legend
reading "Flagged for review", zero "Marked for review", and the sandbox payload
carrying the new fields. ⚠ **PowerShell mangled `actions.ts` mid-session** —
re-encoded every `§`, `—` and `⚠` into mojibake; caught immediately, restored
from the last commit, redone with the proper tool. Nothing corrupt committed.

⚠ **A small correction to carry forward:** the "2 known tsc errors" is now
**one file**. The `validator.ts` error was a stale `.next` artefact pointing at
the deleted `journey` page and is gone after a clean build.

⬜ **Not built, with reasons:** a **"My bookmarks"** surface — the
Builder→runner loop already closes the pruning cycle, so it is a convenience,
not a gap · a **Bookmarked filter on the case bank**, which would let a case met
in a CAT be saved for re-sitting (the table already supports `CASE`) · the
**topbar overflow menu** — raised by Sam and deferred; flag would have to stay
visible regardless · **case-level bookmarking** — schema capacity kept, UI
declined · **mark history** — the marks migration is explicit that a future
analytic gets its own audit table rather than bending that one.

## Session — 2026-07-30 (History becomes a directory, and practice gets a **Session Report**) — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-628d74`. **9 commits**, **one migration**
(`20260901120000_discard_attempt.sql`), dev-applied; vitest **648 → 739**; tsc at
the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint clean; production webpack build
run before each merge. **On `main`, NOT prod.** Full write-up:
`docs/product-plan/session-report.md`.

**⭐ SAM RESHAPED THE WHOLE THING WITH ONE OBJECTION.** The brief was "the
History page is a placeholder, build it for real". I proposed putting practice
detail *inside* History. Sam: *"then we would have to make another page
specially for practice quizzes… the history page becomes just like a log of your
attempt table"* — and that is the right shape. A pack and a CAT each had a
permanent report; a practice quiz had only the end-of-quiz popup, a **moment**
rather than a destination. So practice gets its own report, History stays the
**directory**, and all three kinds become symmetrical. It also made History
*smaller*: a directory needs no per-sitting detail. My version would have given
one row shape detail and another a summary-plus-link — two things to keep in
step — and detail buried in an expanding row has no URL, so the dashboard and
the results popup could never point at it.

**HISTORY, REBUILT (4 commits).** It was an MVP with two chip groups and a
search box rendered permanently disabled, and its one live control — a
show-discarded checkbox — was itself always disabled because nothing writes
`ABANDONED`. Now: paged instead of capped at 50 (the heaviest dev student has
**71** sittings, so 21 were unreachable with nothing on screen to say so), real
filters and search living in the **URL** (so the back button works and the page
needs no client state), a **phone layout** that did not exist at all on one of
four student bottom tabs, and honest counts. **⚠ A CAT was displaying a
percentage** — §13.5 forbids it outright, and one dev CAT read "40%" while two
read "—"; the dashboard rail was already doing this correctly, so the rules moved
into one shared file rather than being copied.

**DISCARD (1 commit + the migration).** ⚠ **A student has no UPDATE policy on
`nclex_attempts` at all**, so this is a SECURITY DEFINER function, like every
other attempt write. **Practice only, measured not assumed**: all 36 stale rows
are practice, zero unfinished CATs, and the only two unfinished packs are exactly
what must be protected (a pack is a paid one-shot). **No answer row is touched** —
otherwise discarding becomes a way to tidy up a bad score. Proven under
rollback 7/7, including another student's sitting and a fabricated id refused
with the **same message** so the function cannot probe for existence; migration
file **md5-identical** to the deployed body.

**THE REPORT, IN THREE SLICES (3 commits).** Built from CD's "1c" direction
across three rounds of review — the first two revisions kept a marked-questions
card that cannot be built, put the no-banding line in a page footnote, and had no
phone design at all; the third fixed all three. `pointsDetail()` from
`lib/scoring` was already the found/missed/wrong-picked splitter, so the most
expensive-looking card was a reuse. **Verified reconciling live: 19 found − 3
wrong picks = 16 = the stored score, and 19 + 9 missed = 28 max.**

**⭐ SEVEN FINDINGS, EVERY ONE FROM LOOKING AT THE RENDERED PAGE OR THE DATA —
none from tests, types or lint:**

1. **The first report contradicted itself** — "Unseen · 9 Q" in the heading,
   "2 of 6" in the body. `actual_question_count` disagrees with the real item
   rows on **7 of 69** practice attempts. Fixed in the report *and* History; on a
   finished sitting the answer rows equal the items exactly (26/26 completed,
   7/7 timed out).
2. **`answer_changes_json` cannot be counted by its length.** It is append-only
   and includes the first answer, and *building* a multi-slot answer appends one
   entry per slot — a 4-row matrix answered straightforwardly logs four entries
   and zero changes of mind. Its saving grace: it stores the answer **before and
   after**, which makes *"1 of those moved away from the right answer"*
   genuinely derivable.
3. **139 of 1,853 stem snapshots (7.5%) are Tiptap JSON**, so the table would
   print `{"type":"doc",…}` on one row in thirteen. `richTextToPlain()` already
   existed for this; verified on a sitting whose stems are 7 of 7 JSON.
4. **`nursing_subject` appears in ZERO stored filter payloads** — a
   subject-only search would have been correct code that could never match.
5. **The confirm dialog inherited `white-space: nowrap`** from the table cell it
   was mounted in. `position: fixed` leaves the FLOW but not the CASCADE, so its
   body copy became one unwrappable 840px line. ⚠ **My first fix addressed a real
   line-height omission but not what was on screen** — my harness rendered the
   dialog standalone and never reproduced the cell it lives in.
6. **The History table was clipped at 1066px** — 872px of content in a 791px
   card with `overflow: hidden`, so part of every row was unreachable on a
   laptop. A priority inversion: every other column was nowrap, so the only one
   that could give was **Session**, squeezed to 92px.
7. **The fix list said "in that order" and then numbered a 75% subject above a
   0% category.** The numbering *is* the recommendation.

**⭐ THEN SAM MADE IT CONSISTENT.** He noticed the practice report was the only
one you had to *discover*: packs and CATs advertised their report in the action
column while practice advertised the runner. Now every finished row offers the
same pair, **Report** and **Review**. ⚠ **Packs are asymmetric** — answers expire
after 21 days, so Review shows only while the window is open and otherwise the
row states *"Review closed"*. **And a defect in my own first cut, found in the
data:** I defaulted an unknown window to OPEN, but the runner treats a *missing*
credit row as expired and on dev only **2** pack attempts have one — that default
would have offered Review on nearly every pack row and bounced every one. Now it
fails closed, proven against the runner: a withheld row's `/session/[id]` lands
on the pack report, an offered row's lands in the runner.

**⭐ AND HE CAUGHT A CARD I DROPPED.** The rail's "On this page" jump list was
missing — not a decision, an omission: I recorded CD's *phone* instruction for it
and never built the desktop version it qualified. ⚠ Its "Where you slipped"
figure could have lied, since the switcher opens on the first axis *with rows*
chosen client-side; `AXIS_ORDER` is now shared so both read the same rule.

**⚠ MARKING IS A HALF-BUILT FEATURE, and this is the session that pinned it
down.** The Builder offers a Marked pool chip, the runner has a ⚑ Mark button,
the grid has a Marked filter, `nclex_question_marks` exists and the SQL reads
it — **and nothing writes to it.** The button is `disabled` with the tooltip
"Marking questions for review isn't available yet". So the Builder's Marked pool
has never been able to match a question. CD's design had three marked-based
elements; all are deliberately absent. **Sam's call: build marking later, so it's
built well** — and when it is, only the write path is missing.

**ALSO FIXED IN PASSING:** the dashboard's recent rail offered **two links that
bounce** — an abandoned practice row → a report that redirects away (mine), and
an in-progress pack → the pack report (pre-existing). It routed by kind before
checking status.

**⚠ Not built, all recorded in the doc:** marking · "re-quiz what you got wrong"
(the Builder *has* an INCORRECT pool chip, but its prefill deliberately forces
UNSEEN — overriding that from one call site is a decision) · per-question deep
links, so a long CAT's Review opens near its end · History sorting and date
filters. **Renaming History was reviewed and dropped** — "test history" is this
market's convention; the nav reads "All history", the heading stays "History".

**⭐ RELEASED TO PROD the same session** (PR
[#44](https://github.com/QAcademy-Nurses/mynclex/pull/44), `b8c7e82`) — this
arc AND the case-bank arc, eleven commits and four migrations, none of which
had been on prod. Both workflows green; prod tracker 145 → **149**, newest

positive here before): **zero rows without a matching file**. Prod probed —

## Session — 2026-07-29/30 (the student **Case Study bank**) — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-a82427`. **6 commits**, **three migrations**
(`20260829120000`, `20260830120000`, `20260831120000`), all dev-applied; vitest
**611 → 648**; tsc at the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint clean on
every new file. **On `main`, NOT prod.** Full write-up:
`docs/product-plan/case-bank.md`.

⚠ **First, a correction to every recent entry in this log:** they say "prod
still `1a6717d`". False since 2026-07-29 — `origin/prod` carries `main`'s tip
via release PR #43 (`e636665`), so the whole CAT arc is live. Fixed in
BUILD_LIST too. Check `git log origin/prod` at session start; a pinned SHA in a
log is only true the day it is written.

### What Sam asked for

A new student page listing every case study in the bank, takeable directly, max
2 at a time, in the **existing runner**. Reserved cases (CAT / readiness) shown
locked — **and once actually seen, unlocked**. A CD prototype existed (two
variants); Sam's instruction was to build it properly first and let CD upgrade
later. Variant 2 (dense rows + a "Your run" rail) chosen.

### ⭐ The unlock rule is the best idea in the feature, and it is Sam's

Reserved content is protected so it isn't pre-studied. Once a student has met a
case in a CAT or a readiness pack, that protection is **spent for them** — so it
unlocks. Most products lock forever (wasteful: you can never redrill the case you
just struggled with in a paid pack) or don't lock (leaks the exam). This is a
third answer, and it has a side effect worth keeping: **packs and CAT become a
source of practice material**.

"Seen" = the case was in one of their attempts AND (that attempt finished OR they
submitted an answer to one of its questions). **Both halves load-bearing** — a
readiness pack snapshots every question the moment the attempt row exists, so
"a row exists" would unlock a pack never opened; an abandoned CAT genuinely
showed what it served. ⚠ Confirmed live that merely *visiting* a question writes
a `DRAFT` answer row, so a rule keyed on "an answer row exists" would have been
wrong — and proven to discriminate **within one in-progress attempt**: of two
cases in the same run, only the one with a submitted answer read as seen.

### ⭐ Sam's correction: the student must never learn WHY

Mid-design Sam stopped a copy suggestion — *"the student must not know that the
locked is because its reserved for readiness and cat. thats telling them our
system."* That has a technical consequence beyond wording: **the reason must not
be in the payload**, or it is readable in the network response while merely
hidden in the UI. So the RPC returns a bare `locked` and never `cat_pool` or pack
membership. The same reasoning killed something planned: **a locked case ships no
scenario text at all**, since that is exam content.

⚠ **Verified to bite, not merely to pass:** against the live 100KB page response,
a locked case's scenario is absent **while an unlocked case's is present**. A
guard only ever seen passing is not a guard. (An earlier check of mine reported
a leak — false positive from my own too-loose regex matching "**Cat**heter" and
"compli**cat**ions".)

### Architecture — own SQL, nothing shared touched

One eligibility helper read by **both** the list and the launch RPC, so what the
page offers and what the launch accepts cannot disagree (10b3's one-gateway
property). Deliberately **not** `_nclex_eligible_unit_pool`: that hard-excludes
reserved stock, and teaching it the unlock rule would leak reserved cases into
the ordinary practice builder and move its counts — exactly the guarantee 10b3
exists to make. The helper is granted to **nobody**, not even `authenticated`,
because it takes a student id.

The launch RPC re-validates every case against that same helper (a stale tab
can't start a locked case) and snapshots the picked cases the way
`nclex_create_attempt`'s CASE branch does. **14/14 checks passed under rollback**
— locked refused alone and when paired, >2 refused, duplicates refused, CAT mode
refused, 1 case = 6q/1 unit, 2 = 12q with contiguous positions in the picked
order, `cjmm_step` and `case_position` on every child, `difficulty_irt` frozen
(10d) not the word, untimed clock NULL, timed = 12 × 90.

### Row content is not the prototype's — three things dropped, each measured

- **"6 questions"** — the schema fixes every case at six, so it was the same
  string on all 93 rows.
- **The difficulty word** — the average case spans **1.90 logits** between its
  easiest and hardest question. One label per case re-introduces exactly the
  drift 10d had just removed.
- **Raw `tags`** — curator vocabulary (`synthetic`, `CATPREP`, `Maryland`), and
  ⚠ one value is literally **`readiness`**, which would have told the student
  which cases are pack members.

Replaced by the **dominant** subject + body system — not every distinct one,
because a case's six children legitimately span subjects (one step may be a
delegation question), which dragged "Leadership and Management" onto a
peritoneal-dialysis case and produced a **140-character** line that wrapped and
was longer than its title. Dominant = most children carry it, ties to the value
on case position 1 ("Recognise cues"). 140 → 42 chars, and *more* accurate.

⚠ `scenario_summary` holds **two formats in one column** — Tiptap JSON (71 of 93)
and plain text (22). The extractor handles both; naively joining Tiptap text
nodes with spaces corrupts prose, because marks split sentences mid-phrase.

### ⭐ A real bug only clicking found

Selecting a third case was correctly refused but showed **no toast**. `setError`
was being called inside the `setSelected` updater — returning state unchanged
makes React bail out of the re-render, and an updater must be pure anyway.
**tsc clean, all tests green.** Same class as the 1,000-row cap and the blank
readiness pills: the failure is invisible to every automated check.

### Then Sam asked how the page is guarded — and it found two holes

1. **The list RPC checked only that you were signed in, not entitled.** A
   non-subscriber calling it directly got case titles and scenario text. Matches
   the existing read RPCs (`nclex_count_eligible_items` is auth-only; the gate
   went on write paths in `20260605120000`) — but mine returns **content**, not
   counts. Now gated, mirroring `requireActiveBankSubscription()` exactly so the
   page and the RPC cannot disagree and strand a real student.
2. **`is_builder_visible` was described in the design and then not built** —
   mine. ⚠ It cannot be a plain `AND is_builder_visible`: reserving a case
   **forces that flag off**, so the naive fix would re-lock all 13 cases this
   student had earned. Rule is `reserved OR is_builder_visible`. Zero rows
   affected today — a latent fix.

Both **proven to bite** under rollback: an unentitled user refused while the
entitled student still gets all 93 rows; hiding a real unreserved case takes the
pool 93 → 92; the reserved-and-seen case stays offered with its flag FALSE.

### ⭐ Then Sam found the Review defect, and redesigned around it

Sam asked what Review does for cases unlocked from a CAT or readiness sitting.
Measured: it opened **the whole originating sitting** — a **100-question CAT,
landing on question 100**, with the case at Q10–15. **7 of 16** attempted cases
behaved that way. Not an access leak (CAT review is already reachable from
History; readiness keeps its 21-day window gate) but a button that lied.

Sam's fix reshaped it: **"Already attempted" means SAT HERE**, so Review can only
ever point at this page's own 1- or 2-case runs. A case met only in an exam waits
in Ready to sit. Then — *"when a student has reattempted several times we must
see the other result right"* — which exposed that the page kept only the **latest**
sitting, discarding the 22% → 45% → 85% progression that is the whole point of
reattempting. And then: show the exam sitting too, as the first attempt, with its
score.

All three converge on **the expanded row becoming the case's own history**, each
entry linking to its own home — readiness to its pack report, CAT to its result
page — so **no entry can open a 100-question runner**. Added `answered`/`served`
per entry: a bare percentage conflated "did badly" with "didn't finish" (a real
row: 50% that was 75% on the four of six answered; another with 0 of 6 answered
rendering identically to a genuine zero). Now `Not answered`, grey not red.

### Also this session

- The **top pane** was widened to span the page, with the run rail beneath it.
- The runner's **exit resolver** learned this surface — a case run is stored as
  `CUSTOM_BUILT` so it was exiting to the practice builder, the same trap the
  file already documented for CAT. `filters_json` is now **required** on its
  input type so a new call site cannot silently inherit the wrong destination.
- Nav relabelled **NGN Case Studies** and **Adaptive Testing (CAT)**.
- **Journey Tracker placeholders removed** — route, nav entry, dashboard door.
  Zero `journey_*` tables ever existed; the design and CLAUDE.md rule #1's naming
  exception are untouched. Its dashboard door was replaced by NGN Case Studies
  with a real count (`nclex_case_bank_count`, reading the same helper — verified
  37 = 37 against the list). ⭐ **Every dashboard door now goes somewhere**, with
  a test asserting none can be dead.

### Verified

A real 12-question sitting played end to end (Case 1 of 2 → Case 2 of 2, each
with its own chart panel); groups reconciling exactly against SQL at every stage;
**13 reserved cases takeable for a student who met them and zero takeable
without**; the 3-sitting case showing CAT 43% → pack 47% (5 of 6) → pack "Not
answered", each to its own destination; mobile clean at 375px throughout; console
clean. All four migration function bodies **md5-identical to their files**.

⚠ **Left on dev deliberately:** a completed test sitting, so the "Already
attempted" group is populated (two cases at 11% and "Not answered").

## Session — 2026-07-29 (later still — Slice 10d + the CAT-reservation guard) — **RELEASED TO PROD** (PR [#43](https://github.com/QAcademy-Nurses/mynclex/pull/43), `e636665`)

Branch `claude/work-session-7bf5e9`. **2 code commits + docs**, **two migrations**
(`20260826120000`, `20260827120000`), both dev-applied; tsc clean bar the 2 known
`scoring-roundtrip` errors; vitest **606 → 611**; eslint clean.

**⚠ First, a correction to every recent log entry: they all say "prod still
`1a6717d`". That has been false since the CAT difficulty arc was released.**
`origin/prod` is at `885ecc1` and carries `main`'s tip, so 10a / 10b1–3 / 10c are
all live on prod, including the Sunday 02:00 UTC recalibration job. Checked
`git log origin/prod` at session start rather than trusting the logs.

### What 10d was

A question's difficulty exists twice: the curator's word and the measured number
(`difficulty_irt`). The engine reads the number; humans see a word. 10c made the
number real, and §5.2 forbids recalibration from rewriting the word — so the word
goes stale *on purpose*. `displayBand()` shipped in 10a to close that drift by
deriving the shown band from the number, **and had never once fired**: every
attempt freezes a snapshot of each question's classification, and the snapshot
carried the word. The number never reached the pill.

10d changes what is frozen. `classification_snapshot` now carries `difficulty_irt`
in place of `difficulty`.

### How the function bodies were produced

Not retyped — there is no local SQL client (no `psql`, no Supabase CLI, no `pg`
module, and `npm install` is a known way to break the dev server's CSS). Each live
definition was read with `pg_get_functiondef()`, the exact target strings replaced
**under an assertion on the occurrence count**, executed, then dumped back to disk
through a temporary RPC called from Node with `fetch` — so the migration file is
**byte-identical by md5** to what is deployed, with zero transcription risk. Both
migrations were built this way and both verified.

### ⭐ Three findings, none caught by any automated check

**1. "Six functions, one substitution each" was ten lines.** A standalone question
and a case child are frozen by *two separate builders* inside `nclex_create_attempt`
and `nclex_create_readiness_attempt`, and the two tutor-quiz paths need their record
`SELECT` list widened as well. Missing the case-child branch would have blanked the
pill on every case question — six per case study.

**2. "Nothing else reads that word — no SQL, no report, no analytics; the pill is
the only reader" was wrong.** §5.5.2b used that to justify replacing rather than
duplicating. Two more readers existed: the **readiness-pack report** froze the word
into every report row and fed it to a breakdown table, the per-question detail, and
a filter *and* grouping — on the surface students pay for; and the **runner
tutorial's** in-code fixtures carried the word, so every pill in the public
walkthrough would have gone blank. **`tsc` was clean and all 607 tests passed** —
both are optional-field runtime shapes, so both would have shipped and been found by
a human opening the page. Same class as the 1,000-row cap last session.

**3. ⭐ The drift relocates to the filter — Sam's "step 2", and he was right that it
existed even though he thought it was already done.** He recapped that the surfaces
were fixed; they weren't. The practice builder's difficulty axis is *chosen and
counted in the database* on `bi.difficulty`. So a student picks by the word and is
then shown a band derived from the number — and that is **more** visible than the
bug it replaced, because before part 1 both ends read the stale word and agreed with
each other while both were wrong. A filter that disagrees with the label on the
question it just returned reads as a defect, not as calibration. Closed with a SQL
`nclex_difficulty_band()` driving the filter predicate and the per-band counts in
both branches of `_nclex_eligible_unit_pool` + `nclex_filter_breakdown`; count and
attempt creation follow through the one gateway (the 10b3 property).

⚠ **The cost, named not hidden: the cut-offs now exist in two languages.** A
generated column was rejected (relocates the duplication rather than removing it,
and adds a column part 1 avoided) and TS-side numeric ranges were rejected (cleanest
for the filter, but the live per-band counts then need five ranges passed in and
grouped by hand). Instead the two are bound by a test that reads the cut-offs out of
the migration file, including the asymmetric operators where a boundary value is the
one place they could part. **Verified to bite** — moving one SQL cut-off to 1.6
fails it with *"TS just below 1.6: expected 'Very hard' to be 'Hard'"*. A guard only
ever seen passing is not a guard.

### Verified live, not just typechecked

Tutorial pill reads **Medium / Easy / Hard** from `0.0 / -1.0 / 1.0`; the readiness
report's difficulty axis reads **20 / 55 / 25** and **reconciles exactly** against
banding the raw numbers in SQL; the per-question detail reads "Easy" for an item
frozen at −1.0; the builder renders five bands at **158/152/250/188/191**, identical
to calling the pool helper directly. Ticking Hard gives **268**, checked out as 148
standalone + 120 children of the 20 cases holding a Hard child — a case is
all-or-nothing, so the axis count and the match count legitimately differ
(pre-existing, unchanged). Console clean throughout.

### Also

**Dev backfill, deliberately not a migration** (Sam's call): prod has **zero**
attempt rows, so a migration would be a permanent no-op in the history. 1,636 rows
translated through the seed map; **0 mistranslated**, and every other classification
key **byte-identical to a backup table**; 180 rows had no word and correctly got no
number. Backup `_slice10d_backup` dropped after Sam's sign-off.

**Pre-existing 10a leftover fixed:** the readiness report's per-question map had
**two** hardcoded `['Easy','Medium','Hard']` lists — the grouping and the filter
dropdown — silently dropping every Very easy / Very hard question since five bands
shipped.

⚠ **Not done:** the admin "recalibrate now" button · the calibration history readout
· misfit warnings. ⚠ The editor tick is still a second CAT-reservation door skipping
three of the drawer's guards (latent, 0 rows).

### ⭐ The migration-tracker warning was aimed at the wrong database — RETIRED

Three prior entries carry a ⚠ saying dev's tracker is drifted and must be reconciled
"before the next release". Measured it properly instead of repeating it, and **it is
not a release concern at all**:

| | files on disk | tracker rows | rows with **no** file | files **not** tracked |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **prod** | 144 | 142 | **0** | 2 (today's — correct) |
| **dev** | 144 | 121 | **105** | 128 |

**Prod is clean.** CLAUDE.md's pre-release check is about *prod's* tracker, because
`supabase db push` only ever runs against prod — `migrate-prod.yml` is the sole
migration workflow and there is no dev equivalent. Zero orphaned rows there.

⚠ **A trap worth recording: version *format* is not the test.** Prod has 34
wall-clock-looking versions, which reads as drift — but **34 files on disk don't use
the `120000` convention either** (`20260424130000`, `20260512300000`,
`20260623120100`…) and they match one-for-one. Pattern-matching the stamp gives a
false positive; only a set comparison against the filenames answers the question. I
made this mistake first and caught it on the second pass.

**Dev's tracker isn't drifted, it's fiction** — it agrees with the repo on 16 rows
out of 144, because dev has always been fed through MCP (`apply_migration` stamps a
wall clock; raw `execute_sql`, which is how the recent ones landed, stamps nothing)
while the repo kept a renumbered sequence.

**Left alone deliberately (Sam's call).** Rewriting those 105 rows would assert
"dev has these 144 migrations applied" — very likely true, since dev is where
everything lands first, but not verifiable row-by-row. A broken tracker fails
loudly; a confidently-wrong one silently tells a future `db push` to *skip* a
migration that was never applied. Nothing reads dev's tracker, so the broken state
costs nothing.

---

### Then: the CAT reservation, sealed in the database (migration `20260828120000`)

Closed the long-standing ⚠ that "the editor tick is a second reservation door".
It is a **fourth** door problem, not a second: reserving carries three obligations
beyond setting `cat_pool` — leave the practice builder, stop being a free sample,
don't also be sold in a readiness pack — and all three lived in **one** caller, the
admin drawer's Server Action. The two editor ticks (standalone + case wrapper) write
the flag bare, and **the drawer's own action never re-checks pack membership for
cases**, only for `item` targets.

**⚠ I reported that third one wrong and corrected it before committing.** I said it
was a one-click leak on the sanctioned surface — 10 published cases with pack-member
children, each a click from putting paid questions into the CAT pool. **Not true:**
the drawer's *UI* greys those cases, because `queries.ts` rolls child pack membership
up into the wrapper's `readinessTagged`. I found the missing check in the action and
assumed the UI shared the hole without looking. What is actually missing is narrower
and still real — the **server-side** re-check the item path has, which carries an
explicit comment saying *don't trust what the drawer displayed*. The case path
trusted the page, so a stale tab or a concurrently-edited pack goes through; and the
UI rollup counts only **published** children, so a pack member that is an unpublished
child greys nothing (0 rows today, nothing preventing one). The wrong version had
already gone into the migration header and a code comment; both now state it
correctly *including that it was overstated*, because a migration comment is read
years later by someone deciding whether the trigger still earns its place.

**The fix:** a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger on `nclex_bank_items` and
`nclex_case_studies`. On any write leaving `cat_pool` true it forces
`is_builder_visible` and `is_free_sample` false, and for items refuses the write if
the row is in a pack, naming item and pack. A trigger rather than a fourth TS copy
because four paths set the flag, three skip part of the contract, and the database is
the only layer all four cross.

**Forcing the flags but rejecting on pack membership** is the judgement call: a
curator ticking "Reserve for CAT" has stated an intention the flags merely follow
from, so making them agree *completes* the instruction; pack membership is two
intentions in real conflict and only a human can resolve it. ⚠ `is_free_sample` is
forced on **cases** too, which the drawer never did — same column, same reasoning, a
gap closed rather than a behaviour copied.

**⚠ The trigger name is load-bearing.** Postgres fires same-event row triggers
alphabetically and `nclex_item_cat_pool_inherit_trg` copies a child's flag down from
its parent, so the guard must sort *after* it — `..._seal_trg` is named for that and
nothing else. A tidier name sorting earlier silently disables it on the INSERT path.
Said so in the migration where someone tidying would read it.

**Proven on real rows inside a transaction that rolled back** — five cases, all
passing: reserving an item seals both flags; releasing leaves them alone (the guard
is inert when the flag is false); a pack member is refused; a case whose child is a
pack member is refused **transitively through the cascade**, with the error naming the
offending *child* (`NCLEX_MAT_HS1_Q4` in `NCLEX_PACK_00002`) rather than the case
clicked; a clean case seals itself and all six children. Confirmed afterwards that the
rollback left all 2,833 reserved rows untouched. The drawer also gains the case-level
pre-screen, before the placeability gate so a case failing both is skipped once.

The six-placeable-children rule deliberately stays in TypeScript, where it can say
what is wrong.

### Released to prod the same session

PR [#43](https://github.com/QAcademy-Nurses/mynclex/pull/43), merge commit
`e636665`. Pre-release checks: prod's tracker consistent (145 files, 142 rows, 0
without a matching file, exactly 3 outstanding); **prod has 0 attempt rows**, so
the dev-only backfill has no prod counterpart to worry about and no sitting exists
that could show a blank pill; **0 reserved items and 0 pre-existing guard
violations**, so the new triggers had nothing to conflict with.

Both workflows green — `migrate-prod` applied `20260826120000` / `20260827120000` /
`20260828120000`, `deploy-prod` built and deployed the Worker. **Verified against
prod directly rather than trusting the exit codes:** all six attempt-creators freeze
`difficulty_irt` and none still freezes the word (6 / 0), `nclex_difficulty_band()`
answers correctly live (2.3 → Very hard, −1.0 → Easy), both seal triggers present,
and the Worker serves 200.

**Expected effect on prod: close to none, by design.** No attempt rows, no reserved
stock, and nothing recalibrated. Plumbing laid ahead of the water.

ⓘ This status line was written at release time, not left saying "MERGED to `main`" —
which is the exact failure this session spent time correcting elsewhere in this file.

## Session — 2026-07-29 (later — Slice 10c: difficulty stops being an opinion) — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-e44b13`. **3 commits**, **two migrations**
(`20260824120000`, `20260825120000`), both dev-applied; tsc clean bar the 2 known
`scoring-roundtrip` errors; vitest **565 → 606**; eslint clean; prod still `1a6717d`.
Merged to `main` at `6dfd866` (fast-forward) with Sam's approval, then the workflow was
run for real.

Sam opened with "check a few logics before we build 10c" — which is where most of the
value came from.

### The audit that preceded the build

Traced the whole CAT-pool reservation path and the serving path, then checked every
claim against live dev data. Five findings; the first three mattered.

**⭐ 1. The pool's membership test named the wrong column.** CAT selects on
`difficulty_irt`, the number. The placeability CHECK, the reserve drawer's candidate
query, the audit lens's `unplaceable` warning and the reserve action's case gate all
screened on `difficulty`, the curator's word. Those are written together by the editor
and **not** by bulk SQL: the 622-item gap-fill run set a band on every row and a number
on none — 13 seed files, zero mentions of the column. Bank-wide that left **633 items
with a word and no number**, of which **622 were offerable in the reserve drawer** as
CAT stock the engine could never serve, and **11 were already reserved** — counted in
every Coverage figure, dead to selection. Reserve a few hundred of the rest and the
targets would have read "met" while CAT served none of them.

Sam's read was that this was "just a bulk-seeding artefact, not a problem". Half right —
the cause is benign, the state is not, and nothing stopped it recurring. Measuring the
blast radius (633 bank-wide, not 11) is what settled it.

Fixed by making the number **impossible to omit** — a trigger seeds `difficulty_irt`
from the band on any write that leaves it null, so the editor, bulk SQL, a migration and
any future importer all land in the same state — rather than by tightening the four
tests that missed it. Those follow behind as backstops now that they can't be the only
guard. A planned test turned out unreachable: the taxonomy CHECK already closes the band
to five values, so *band present ⇒ number present* is now total.

**2. The editor tick is a second reservation door** that skips three of the drawer's
guards (doesn't clear `is_builder_visible`, doesn't clear `is_free_sample`, doesn't check
readiness-pack membership). Zero rows affected today, so latent — **still open**, and the
right fix is a database trigger, not a fourth copy of the rules in TypeScript.

**3. CAT ignored a trend dataset's publish state** where practice always required it —
a published trend question under an unpublished dataset would have been hidden from
practice and servable in an exam. Zero rows today; closed anyway.

Plus two content gaps: 32 unpublished items and 1 unpublished case sitting in the pool,
and 1 published reserved case that can't be scheduled (58 of 60 usable).

### Slice 10c — the build

**The design conversation moved three things before any code.**

*The runtime.* §17.6 said Python/`pyirt` in GitHub Actions because "in-Postgres math does
not" do a joint fit. **That reason is wrong** — the calculation is a loop of weighted
sums and Postgres does those. The real deciders: **17.6% of our answers carry partial
credit**, which a SQL version would have to flatten to right/wrong (making every
SATA/matrix/bow-tie read harder than it is); an estimator's bugs are silent rather than
loud; and the iteration shouldn't compete for CPU with the instance serving students.

*The language.* Then a discovery inverted the choice: **there is no Python on the
machine** — the same blind spot that shipped `validate.py` untested — and session
branches never reach the remote, so a GitHub Action couldn't have run one either without
merging blind. A Python job would have been written, merged and first executed in anger.
That loses on **correctness**, which is the entire reason the job left the database. So:
TypeScript, testable in vitest, and — as it turned out — runnable end to end against dev
before merging.

*⭐ Sam's question that reshaped the maths.* "Didn't we already agree how many of five
have to be right for the student to have got it? Shouldn't recalibration use the same
thing?" §4.5 not only settled it (the fraction, never a threshold) but names this exact
consistency as binding. It caught a real flaw in what had been scoped: the "treat a
5-mark question as five mini-questions" framing uses the same fraction but weights big
questions five to thirteen times heavier than the engine does when it serves them —
precisely the drift §4.5 exists to prevent, arriving by the back door. Corrected to
**fraction in, one question counts as one**.

It also made the build smaller. The person half of the joint fit **is `estimateAbility`
from `lib/cat`** — the function the live exam runs, shared not copied, so drift is
impossible rather than forbidden. It anchors ability against the same prior, and zero on
that scale is the passing standard, so calibrated difficulty comes out measured against
pass/fail. It also keeps a perfect scorer finite. Only the item half is new. `lib/cat/`
needed **no edit at all** — `expectedScore` was already public.

*The folder.* Scoped as `lib/cat/calibration`; **Sam corrected it** — calibration covers
the whole bank including practice questions CAT never serves (§5.3.5), so filing it under
`cat/` would misdirect anyone asking why a practice question's difficulty moved. Landed
as `lib/calibration/`, importing from `lib/cat` one way. Splitting the shared half of the
model out of `rasch.ts` is deferred until calibration shows where the line falls.

**A failing test then caught the mirror image.** The weight cancels top-and-bottom in the
item's own Newton step, so a case child calibrates at **full** strength while still
counting **half** toward ability. The test had asserted the opposite. §19.4 had settled
exactly that split, and the maths delivers both from one number with no special case. The
cancellation is exact only at fixed abilities — ~0.006 of a logit leaks through
second-hand, recorded rather than claimed away.

**⚠ The audit table already existed.** `nclex_bank_item_calibration_history` was built in
**Slice 1** (§12.7.5) and had sat empty since August. Arriving with §17.2's column list
(`item_id`, `previous_value`, …) against the built names (`bank_item_id`,
`previous_difficulty_irt`, …) nearly produced a **second table**. §12.7 is canonical on
this, so 10c adapted and added only what was missing: `raw_empirical_value` (the blend can
hide a wild measurement behind a mild-looking move) plus `job_trigger` /
`job_triggered_by`. `new_source` deliberately **not** tightened — narrowing a canonical
CHECK wasn't this slice's call; `rules.ts` enforces §5.2 and is tested for it.

**Writes go through one RPC, one transaction** — the Supabase client can't span
statements, so a loop could stop between changing a difficulty and recording why.

**The switch** follows Sam's own precedent (he supplied it): the nightly enrolment sweep's
`nclex_config` flag, read first, flipped from `/admin/config`. Shipped **ON**, because the
job is self-gating and shipping it off only means remembering to switch it on at launch.

### Proof

- **The maths:** 150 students and 40 questions with chosen difficulties, every seed
  deliberately wrong at 0, answers simulated from the model — recovered to a **mean error
  of 0.25** with **ordering >95% correct** (ordering is what selection actually depends on).
- **The reads:** ran the real job against dev — 678 responses, 2 students, 551 items —
  then reconciled every figure against SQL (1,112 answers → 712 submitted → 688
  bank-sourced → 678 with a usable score). Nothing silently dropped.
- **The off-switch:** flipped to false, re-ran, halted, nothing read or written.
- **The write path,** which dev's data can never reach: drove the RPC under a rollback —
  `0.0 → 1.2345`, `CURATOR_LABEL → EMPIRICAL`, full history row, all undone.
- **In anger:** after merging, [run 30454262945](https://github.com/QAcademy-Nurses/mynclex/actions/runs/30454262945)
  green in 46s, reproducing the local numbers **exactly**. That proved the secrets are
  correct, not merely present.

A typing error caught a real trap on the way: the client types the joined item as an
**array**, and reading the wrong shape would have dropped every response — a green run
reporting zero answers, indistinguishable from a young bank.

### Secrets

Sam set `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY_DEV` / `_PROD`. The two **URLs were dropped from the
plan** — a Supabase project URL ships in the browser bundle and `migrate-prod.yml` already
carries the prod ref as a literal, so making them secrets would have hidden nothing and
added two things to rotate. Four secrets became two.

### The closing stretch — Sam specified 10d and made it smaller twice

Sam asked for the snapshot follow-up "in simple terms", then took it apart. Two
reductions, both his, both checked before agreeing.

**⭐ 1. Drop the source branch.** §5.5.2 said: show the curator's word while
`difficulty_source = 'CURATOR_LABEL'`, the derived band once `EMPIRICAL`. Sam's
observation: `difficulty_irt` always holds a number anyway, and what we display isn't the
number but its word equivalent — so why branch? He is right, and the seeds
**round-trip exactly** (verified by running the built code: −2/−1/0/1/2 each land
dead-centre of their own band, half a logit clear of the nearest cut-off). For an
unmeasured question the two rules return the same word, so the branch protects nothing.

It is also **more faithful to §5.5's stated purpose**. The section exists so the shown
difficulty cannot drift from the number the engine uses — and the branch permitted exactly
that: a row whose number and word disagree while the source still reads `CURATOR_LABEL`
would display the word while the engine used the number. Deriving unconditionally makes
them one fact.

What the branch actually defended was a word with no number — **a live hazard the same
morning, 633 items deep**. Unreachable now that the seeding trigger guarantees a number
wherever there is a band. So the simplification is safe *because of* the earlier fix; it
would not have been safe the day before. ⓘ `difficulty_source` is still needed on the
**curator** side (measured-vs-assumed in the editor readout) — only the student's pill
drops it.

**2. Store the number in place of the word, not alongside it.** Verified nothing else in
the product reads `classification_snapshot.difficulty` — no SQL, no report, no analytics;
the pill is the sole reader. So 10d is **six functions, one substitution each**, no new
column and no table migration.

**Two of my own cautions retired by Sam's reading.** The "two copies could drift" concern
about `cat_item_difficulty` doesn't apply: both are written in the same INSERT from the
same variable, so they *cannot* diverge. And "show then-and-now in review" needs **no**
history table — the snapshot is *then*, the bank row is *now*; the history table answers
the different question of trajectory, which belongs to the admin readout.

Recorded as §5.5.2 **superseded** (not overwritten, so the reasoning survives) + new
§5.5.2b, and as **10d** in BUILD_LIST. ⏭ **Next session builds it.**

### ⚠ Open

- **The maths has never met real data.** Best-covered question: 4 answers, needs 30. 10c
  is built, verified, and correctly doing nothing.
- **The Sunday 02:00 UTC schedule targets prod and is live from now**, not from launch.
- **Not built, on purpose:** the admin "recalibrate now" button · the history readout ·
  **the snapshot plumbing that lights the student difficulty pill** (six attempt-creation
  RPCs) · **misfit warnings** — used as an argument for leaving the database and then not
  built, so recorded rather than dropped.
- **The editor's second reservation door** (finding 2 above).
- **2,284 dev rows still marked EMPIRICAL** are fake — seeded jitter around each curator
  band, so nearly harmless (the fit never reads them, and the job skips them anyway), but
  they make dev useless for telling a working run from a no-op.
- **Dev's migration tracker is still drifted** — `cat_pool_regrain` carries a wall-clock
  version and 10b3's migration isn't recorded at all. Reconcile before the next release.
  **⟶ RETRACTED 2026-07-29 (10d session): not a release concern.** `supabase db push`
  runs against **prod** only, and prod's tracker is clean (0 rows without a file).
  Dev's tracker agrees with the repo on 16 rows out of 144 and is read by nothing;
  left alone on purpose. See the 10d entry for the measurement and the trap
  (version *format* is not the test — 34 files don't use the `120000` convention).

## Session — 2026-07-29 (CAT pool: 10b2 corrections, the regrain, and 10b3 — the reservation is now enforced) — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-bcfc59`. **5 commits**, **two migrations**
(`20260822120000`, `20260823120000`), both dev-applied; tsc clean bar the 2 known
`scoring-roundtrip` errors; vitest **549 → 565**; eslint clean; prod still `1a6717d`
(and prod does **not** carry `cat_pool` at all — 10b1 was never released, so the whole
of §20 is a no-op there until content is reserved).

Started as "verify last session's work". Sam supplied the CD prototype for the CAT-pool
page (⚠ **never saved into the repo** — the other two handoffs live under
`docs/product-plan/design-handoff/`, there is no `cat-pool` folder; worth fixing).

### ⭐ The regrain — two reservation units, not three

Sam's correction, and the code backed it harder than expected: **a trend dataset is not
a selection unit anywhere in the product.** `_nclex_eligible_unit_pool` emits only
`QUESTION` and `CASE`; trend-linked questions travel in the standalone branch on their
own row, and `cat_next_item` picks them individually, capped at one per dataset per exam.
So reserving a dataset reserved something nothing ever draws. The same panel already said
as much about the dataset's builder-visibility flag — *"No effect on trends — each
question sets its own"* — `cat_pool` was the odd one out.

`cat_pool` dropped from `nclex_trend_datasets` + the tutor mirror; each trend question
carries its own flag. A **case** stays a wrapper reservation (it *is* a selection unit)
but its children now hold a **trigger-maintained copy**, so `cat_pool = TRUE` means one
thing on every row. Cascade in the database, not a Server Action: a forgotten join is how
stock leaks, and a cascade in one save path is one a second save path forgets.
**Detach-clear is load-bearing** — without it a detached child becomes a reserved
standalone nobody ticked. All three trigger behaviours proven on real rows in a rollback.

The trend **Coverage tile survives as a variety measure** (Sam's call): *distinct datasets
represented / 60*, with the reserved-question count in its note, worded "reserved" never
"inherited". ⚠ Recorded honestly in the doc that **60 datasets does not derive from the
15-sitting floor** the way 2,400 and 60-cases do — CAT caps trend questions one per dataset
*per exam*, not globally. It is an editorial variety goal.

### Three defects found

1. **The "never tick a child" rule had never been enforced.** The guard tested
   `mode === 'wrapper-child'`, a housekeeping mode nothing has set since the 2026-05-01
   case retrofit — so it silently passed for everything and the tick *was* shown on case
   children, writing a flag the admin page then ignored. Replaced with a React context
   (`CaseChildScope`); the eleven row mappers don't read `parent_case_id`.
   ⚠ **Treat `HousekeepingMode` as unusable for "is this a wrapper child" tests.**
2. **The difficulty-spread marker** was a fifth of the *standalone* target while the bars
   counted the whole pool, so every band read healthier than it was.
3. **⭐ Every Coverage number was truncated by PostgREST's silent 1,000-row cap.** A
   2,833-question pool read as **"1,000 / 2,880"**, standalone 755 instead of 2,393, with a
   plausible-looking spread and blueprint built from whatever the first thousand rows were.
   **Not caused by the regrain** — the standalone read was already 2,393 rows and the
   drawer's candidate read ~1,700, so both have been truncated since 10b2 shipped.
   It survived three rounds of tsc / vitest / SQL because **none of those go through the
   client's row limit**; it was caught the first time a human opened the page (Sam logged
   in). All unbounded reads now page explicitly, ordered by `item_id`. The drawer now
   offers 1,192 free questions where it used to stop at 1,000.

### ⭐ Slice 10b3 — the reservation is enforced

- **Practice stops serving reserved stock** — **one predicate** in
  `_nclex_eligible_unit_pool`. That helper is the single gateway
  (`nclex_create_attempt`, `nclex_count_eligible_items`, `nclex_filter_breakdown` all call
  it), so counts, breakdown and attempt creation follow for free and cannot drift.
- **CAT serves only reserved stock** — both picks in `create_cat_attempt`, the case slot
  plus both adaptive picks in `cat_next_item`. **The mid-case branch is deliberately
  ungated**: a case is vetted when scheduled, and re-checking would abandon a student
  mid-block if a curator released it during the sitting.
- ⚠ **CAT stopped requiring `is_builder_visible`, and had to.** Builder-visible means
  "offer this in the practice builder" — exactly what a reserved question must not be — so
  with enforcement on, the pool would have emptied the moment a curator hid reserved stock.
  Fixing it also closed a **pre-existing drift** where the case pick and the standalone
  picks disagreed about that flag.
- **Two layers on purpose:** reserving also clears `is_builder_visible`, and the Audit
  lens's first card turns from a live-leak counter into a drift detector.
  `is_free_sample` cleared with it — 8 dev rows were both reserved and free samples
  (Sam: unmark; free samples aren't in use. Only reserved rows touched).
- **Mutual exclusivity now binds from both sides** — the three pack-add actions refuse
  CAT-reserved items, closing the direction the drawer never covered.

**How the two big CAT functions were deployed** (worth repeating for any future edit):
`cat_next_item` is the largest function in the system and the one a mistake breaks an exam
with, so neither it nor `create_cat_attempt` was retyped. The live definitions were
**transformed in place under assertions** (raise if an anchor is missing or matched the
wrong number of times), and the migration file's own versions then proven
**byte-identical by md5** (`a12fa7af…`, `8997ca54…`). What ships to prod is exactly what
dev runs.

**Measured on dev:** practice **3,245 → 807** standalone units and **61 → 24** cases; both
leak checks (reserved stock reachable in practice) return zero rows;
reserved-and-builder-visible **2,824 → 0**, reserved-and-free-sample **8 → 0**; CAT retains
**2,382** first-item candidates and **36** schedulable cases; Audit pill 2,824 → 165. The
practice collapse is the seeding working — most of what left is `DEV_CAT_POOL` filler.

⚠ **A live CAT sitting has NOT been played end to end** — the selection queries were
verified by the stock they can see, not by sitting an exam. That is Sam's test.
⚠ Remaining audit numbers are pre-existing content gaps, not enforcement faults: **7
unplaceable** (six children of `NCLEX_CS_00002` + one of `CS_00028`, all missing a
difficulty band) and **159 drafts**.
⚠ **Dev's migration tracker is drifted** — every recent version is stamped with a
wall-clock value instead of its filename version. Dev-only today, but it is the same
failure mode CLAUDE.md flags as a release blocker on prod. Worth a cleanup before the next
release.
**⟶ RETRACTED 2026-07-29 (10d session).** Measured both trackers against the 144
files: **prod has 0 rows without a matching file** — it is not the same failure mode,
because `supabase db push` only runs against prod and prod is clean. Dev's tracker
agrees on 16 rows of 144, is read by nothing, and was left alone deliberately rather
than rewritten into a confidently-wrong state. See the 10d entry.

⏭ **Next = 10c**, the recalibration job (§5.3 / §17) — §20 is now complete.
