## Session — 2026-07-28 (later still — CAT Slice 10b2, the admin CAT-pool page) — MERGED to `main`

Same branch `claude/work-session-22d3b3`, continuing after the reconciliation
entry below. Built **the whole of Slice 10b2** from the v1 Claude Design
prototype — all four sub-slices. **8 commits, MERGED to `main`**; app-layer,
**no migration, no new RLS**; tsc clean bar the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip`
errors; vitest **465 → 549**; eslint clean on new files; prod still `1a6717d`.

⚠ **NOT visually verified by Claude** — the page sits behind an admin login,
so every check was tsc / vitest / eslint / route-compiles. Sam tests next
session and corrections follow then.

### The surface

`/admin/cat-pool` — **top-level, not under `/admin/bank`**. Readiness Packs is
the precedent and matches almost exactly: a top-level route (`/admin/packs`)
whose logic lives under `lib/bank/packs/`, gated on `BANK_CURATE`, with a
composition module and a separate reserved-stock lens. The Bank group's three
children are all **authoring** surfaces; the CAT pool **allocates**
already-authored stock, which is what packs does — §20.2 calls it
*generalising the readiness mechanism*. ⚠ Moving it cost the sidebar: `/admin/layout.tsx`
is a role gate that renders **no chrome**, and every admin sub-folder wraps
itself in `<AdminShell>`; under `/admin/bank` the page inherited that for free.
Fixed with its own `layout.tsx` (Sam caught it).

**One route, three panes** (`?lens=`), not three routes — the v1 design does
panes, and all three read the SAME snapshot the page already loads, so
switching costs nothing. The lens rides the **URL** rather than `useState`
because Load-more and every filter change are soft navigations, which would
otherwise reset the pane mid-way through paging.

- **10b2-a Coverage** — four target cards, the five-band spread with a
  **Set ⟷ Calibrated** toggle, blueprint vs the published NCLEX ranges,
  wrapper supply as *sittings covered*. All arithmetic in a pure `coverage.ts`
  (21 tests) so no number is computed in the view. Reuses
  `CLIENT_NEEDS_BLUEPRINT_RANGES`, `CLIENT_NEEDS_SHORT_LABELS`, `bandForIrt`.
- **10b2-b Reserved stock** — **flat** list (one row per question, wrapper as a
  chip), **50 at a time with Load more** reusing the bank-list convention, and
  the header always reads *"Showing 50 of 2,834"* — the total counts everything
  matching the filters, never the fetched page. **Grouped only when filtered to
  case or trend** (Sam's call — at that point you are looking at a few hundred
  rows belonging to a few dozen wrappers). Release **confirms every time**,
  standalone included (Sam's call); the dialog's job is the cascade — releasing
  one case drops the count by six. Bulk release included: ticking an inherited
  row selects its **wrapper**, so six children collapse to one target.
- **10b2-c Audit** — the three degradation conditions. **Each card deep-links to
  its OWN subset** via a new `wtype` param; the prototype sends all three to a
  generic list, which would land you on ~2,800 rows whichever you clicked. The
  card's count is literally the length of the list its button opens.
- **10b2-d Reserve drawer** — opened via `?reserve=1` so the large candidate set
  is only read when wanted. Published, practice-eligible stock only. The **§20.5
  mutual-exclusivity guard** is implemented here in both halves — blocked rows
  render greyed *with a reason* rather than hidden, and the action re-checks the
  live link table before writing (10b1's boolean means there is no constraint to
  catch a stale page).

### ⚠ Two fidelity corrections from Sam

The first build had the structure right and **skipped the CD visuals**. Reworked:
sticky area head, the dotted "Admin bank" chip, all three header actions, pill
count sub-labels as light text, an h1 that names the lens, the lede back inside
Coverage; and on the list — the section header with its
*"N shown of M reserved · target T"*, publish dot, subcategory, wrapper chip,
`Release` vs **`Release wrapper`** labelling, and search covering the wrapper.
Then the **audit cards** were relaid out: a vertical stack of full-width cards
with a round 34px icon and the count on the title's baseline, not a 3-column
grid with a square icon and the count beneath.

**Deliberately NOT copied:** the design reports results in an inline notice
banner — kept the **toast**, per CLAUDE.md UI #1. And the audit's **"Hide all N"**
bulk fix was left out: clicking it today would clear builder visibility on
**2,825** questions, removing them from student practice in one action, which is
the selection slice's job and needs a decision rather than a button.

### Findings worth keeping

- ⚠ **The reserve drawer takes ~6s to open** on dev's ~1,700-row free pool.
  Acceptable behind an explicit click; the fix would be server-side facets,

- ⚠ **A class collision was introduced and fixed:** `.cp-row` was defined twice,

## Session — 2026-07-28 (later — cloud-branch reconciliation + the marks defect) — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-22d3b3`. Two cloud branches reconciled onto `main`,
then a bank-integrity audit that found half the new 622-item batch unusable.
**10 commits, all MERGED to `main`**; data + docs only, **no app code, no new
migration**; prod still `1a6717d`.

### Reconciling the two cloud branches

`claude/journey-tracker-plan-docs-fs4vjx` (2 commits, docs only) and
`claude/question-difficulty-source-seed-via2ir` (8 commits — coverage gap
analysis, taxonomy normalisation, 622 gap-fill items, CAT-pool triage, 2 skill
additions). **Cherry-picked rather than merged** — both forked well behind
`main`, so merging would have created merge commits and broken the `--ff-only`
push. Both remote branches deleted after verifying no content was lost.

**⚠ The find that mattered: duplicate migration versions.** Both cloud
migrations collided with `main` — `20260818120000` was already
`five_band_difficulty` (10a) and `20260819120000` already `cat_pool_reservation`
(10b1). Because the *filenames* differ, **git merges the collision in without a
single conflict**, leaving two files claiming one version — which breaks
`supabase db push` at the next release, and nothing in the normal merge path
flags it. Renumbered to `20260820120000` (taxonomy) + `20260821120000` (pool
placement CHECK); ordering still correct since the CHECK must run after the
`cat_pool` column exists. The duplicate `db/schema.sql` 5-band fix was dropped
(10a had already committed it, byte-identical).

**A wrong finding corrected on the way in.** The gap analysis §4 called 28 rows
"editor test fixtures" for "carrying raw Tiptap JSON" — that is **our own
rich-content storage format**, not corruption. Only **7** were genuinely not
questions. §10 had already caught this; §4 now points at it instead of
contradicting it. Verified in dev: the 7 are unpublished and out of the pool
(not deleted); pool reads 2,393, all published, all placeable.

Prod pre-checked for the incoming CHECK constraints: exactly one violating row
(`body_system = 'Renal'`), which the migration's own `UPDATE` fixes before the
CHECK runs. Applies cleanly.

### ⭐ The marks defect — half the batch could not be submitted

**312 of the 622 items had `marks = 1`.** Right for MCQ/TF, wrong for every
multi-mark type: SATA 148/148, SELECT_N 55/55, MATRIX 46/46, MATRIX_MR 29/29,
CLOZE 23/24, DRAG_ORDER 11/11. `marks` must equal the item's **maximum
partial-credit score**, because `nclex_submit_answer` snapshots it as
`marks_snapshot` and raises `score_awarded % out of range [0, %]`.

**The failure was score-dependent, which is why it was invisible** — a student
scoring 0 or 1 submitted normally; it only broke for students doing *well*. A
4-correct SATA answered 2-right scored 2 against a ceiling of 1 → Submit threw.

Corrected from each item's own answer key using exactly `computeMarksFromKey`;
`marks` now spans 1–13. All 312 were published and in the free practice pool,
but **no student had reached one**, so there was no attempt data to repair. One
stray BOWTIE outside the batch (`NCLEX_BT_HS1_BT1`, unpublished) had the same
defect and was fixed too — the bank is now at **zero** mismatches.

**The tutor bank was clean (42/42)** — which is the diagnosis: items authored
through the editor derive `marks` automatically, and only bulk SQL bypasses it.

Also fixed: **`instruction` was absent on all 622 rows** (the column wasn't in
the INSERT list). Filled on the 313 multi-select items using the bank's
dominant existing phrasing per type. MCQ/TF deliberately left blank — the stem
asks a single direct question. The 1,307 pre-existing multi-select items
without a cue are a separate open item (Sam's call).

Fixed in **three** places so a re-run can't reintroduce it: dev, the 13 seed
files, and **`validate.py` — which never checked `marks` at all**, the blind
spot that let 312 unusable items load. ⚠ The validator could **not be executed**
(no working Python interpreter in this environment), so it is untested;
equivalent checks were run through a parser over the files and verified against
dev, which now agree across all 27 `(type, marks, instruction)` buckets. The new
column layout was proved to parse in Postgres via a rolled-back temp-table test.

⚠ **`ON CONFLICT (item_id) DO NOTHING` is not present in any seed file**, though
the earlier session log states it was added to make them repeatable. A re-run
fails on the primary key. Recorded in the README, not fixed.

### ⚠ A finding reported and then RETRACTED — option shuffle

A wider audit appeared to show **240 published CLOZE/DRAG_ORDER items whose
answer could be got without reading** (naive strategy scoring 80–91% against an
18–31% guessing baseline). **This was wrong — the runtime option shuffle
already covers those types and has been on prod since 2026-07-17** (slices 3a +
3b, migrations `20260805`/`20260806`, including a DRAG_ORDER anti-giveaway
guard). Verified against the deployed trigger and real attempt rows.

**Cause of the error, worth not repeating:** `SHUFFLE_OPTION_TYPES` in
`lib/practice/runner/option-order.ts` is `{MCQ, SATA, SELECT_N}` and was read as
the *runner's* coverage — it is the **embed player's** (which only renders flat
types). The runner's coverage is the DB trigger. "No shuffle in the renderers"
seemed to confirm it, but reordering happens upstream in
`runner-question-area.tsx`. The memory index line was also stale; both it and
the note have been corrected.

**What remains true but needs no action:** the authored data really does put the
answer first (73.9% of MCQ keys are "A"; 152 CLOZE / 88 DRAG_ORDER / 58
DRAG_CLOZE have it first in every slot). It is a storage artifact, neutralised
at runtime — exactly what the shuffle arc exists for. MATRIX is excluded by
design and measures near chance (51% naive vs 44.9% guessing), so correctly so.

⏭ **Next:** 10b2 (admin CAT-pool page — CD-first). ⚠ Open: the 622 items remain
**unreviewed by a human** (`cat_pool = FALSE`, no `for_prod` tag, so they cannot
reach a student on prod); the 1,307 older items with no `instruction`;
`validate.py` untested; no `ON CONFLICT` in the seed files.

## Session — 2026-07-28 (CAT Slice 10a + the calibration readout + Slice 10b1) — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-92d4a5`. A long build session across the CAT
difficulty arc: fixed the dev server, built **Slice 10a** (five-band difficulty)
+ the **curator calibration readout**, then **Slice 10b1** (the `cat_pool`

`scoring-roundtrip` errors; vitest **465** (+11). Every file announced before
touching; scope discussed before each build.

### Dev-server fix (session-start gotcha)
`next dev` printed "Ready" then crashed on every launch — a stray, **empty**
`node_modules/` in the worktree fooled `next.config.ts`'s `findProjectRoot()`
(it checks a folder EXISTS, not that it holds `next`), pinning Turbopack's root
to the worktree where `next` isn't installed. Removed it → resolution fell
through to the parent repo. Recurs whenever a stray/empty `node_modules` appears
(vitest's `.vite` cache is the usual culprit).

### Slice 10a — five-band difficulty (`a516898` + `b909598` + `3eb02b0`)
Difficulty widened Easy/Medium/Hard → **Very easy … Very hard** (seeds −2 … +2),
band derived from the empirical number.
- Migration `20260818120000` (CHECK 3→5 on both tables, no backfill);
  `DIFFICULTY_LEVELS` → five (cascades to ~9 dropdowns/filters); new
  `lib/bank/difficulty.ts` (label↔IRT seed map + `displayBand()`, 11 tests);
  runner difficulty pill via `displayBand()`.
- **Save rule — settled with Sam, proven live:** last-writer-wins + a
  changed-only guard. Changing the label re-seeds `difficulty_irt` + resets
  `difficulty_source` to `CURATOR_LABEL`; an unrelated edit leaves a calibrated
  number untouched. Replaced the doc's old "empirical locks the field" (§5.2).
  Verified against a real tutor row (first-set writes all three; unrelated edit
  preserved a simulated `EMPIRICAL 0.75`; a label change overwrote it to seed).

### The calibration readout (the old deferred #2)
Editor Classification tab: **Set difficulty** (editable) beside a read-only
**Calibrated difficulty** box (`band (IRT)` + `CURATOR`/`EMPIRICAL` pill +
`≠ label`). Reads the *real* columns — threaded through all 11 row-mappers +
editors — so it shows genuine empirical states now, not waiting for 10c. From a
CD prototype (dropped the "shown to students" bit). 💡 bulb explainer
(`lib/hints/bank/difficulty-calibration-bulb.tsx`). A second agent seeded
**2,315 EMPIRICAL** bank rows; Sam eyeballed agree / diverge / null-label live
and was happy. Fix: suppress `≠ label` when there's no curator label.

### Slice 10b1 — the cat_pool reservation flag
Migration `20260819120000`: boolean **`cat_pool`** on all **six** tables
(standalones + case/trend wrappers, admin + tutor mirrors). **Admin-only
"Reserve for CAT" tick** on three editor surfaces (question Housekeeping +
case-wrapper + trend-wrapper), **never on a child** — reservation lives on the
wrapper, children inherit (mirrors the readiness tag). Admin-only at three
layers: UI (admin surface only), save (writes only when surface = admin), and
inherited RLS (`BANK_CURATE FOR ALL` — no new rule). Shared copy
`lib/bank/atoms/cat-pool.ts`; native control per surface (auth-check vs
VisibilityRow / VisibilityFlag).

  guard becomes an app-layer cross-check, **deferred to 10b3**. (Readiness
  itself uses a tag + link table, not the §20.2 table — so the boolean is more

## Session — 2026-07-28 (bank coverage gap analysis + 622-item gap-fill run) — NOT merged

Branch `claude/question-difficulty-source-seed-via2ir`. Measured the bank
against the NCLEX-RN test plan and against the Mark Klimek lecture notes, fixed
the classification drift that made the measurement unreliable, then authored and
loaded **622 standalone items** to close the gaps. **5 commits, NOT merged** —
awaiting Sam's review.

### The analysis (`docs/product-plan/bank-coverage-gap-analysis.md`)

- **Blueprint:** six of eight client-needs cells were already in range. Two were
  not — Management of Care 14.2% (floor 15%) and Physiological Adaptation 18.0%
  (ceiling 17%). Because the over-weighted cell was over its *ceiling*, the fix
  was purely additive.
- **The real gap was specialty mix**, which body-system commissioning had
  missed: Maternity 3.7% and Pediatrics 3.7%, and both skewed almost entirely to
  health promotion (4 pharmacology items apiece). Klimek spends 2 of 12 lectures
  on maternity alone.
- **Klimek cross-check:** 12 topics at zero coverage, ~17 more in single digits,
  clustering in exactly those under-weighted specialties.
- Corrected the previous session's target arithmetic: a 1,200-item free pool
  costs **622** new items, not 1,042 — the 2,400 CAT reservation is fixed by the
  15-CAT floor and does not grow with the free pool.

### Taxonomy normalisation (`20260820120000`)

Folded 7 stray `nursing_subject` and 12 stray `body_system` values into the
canonical vocabularies and added CHECK constraints so bulk inserts fail loudly.
`lib/bank/classifications.ts` settled two calls the analysis initially got
wrong: canonical is **`Genitourinary`** (the bank's own `Renal/Genitourinary`
was itself drift), and **`Critical Care` is not a subject** — those 45 items are
medical-surgical. Also backfilled 25 NULL subcategories.

### Two bugs found on the way

- **`db/schema.sql` was stale** — still `CHECK (difficulty IN ('Easy','Medium',
  'Hard'))` for both `nclex_bank_items` and `nclex_tutor_questions`, while the
  live DB has carried the five-band constraint since Slice 10a. Rebuilding from
  it would have produced a database rejecting the 560 already-banded items.
  Fixed. (`classifications.ts` still exports only three levels — left alone,
  that is 10a's scope.)
- **48 of 53 unclassified rows were published AND ticked `cat_pool`** — early
  editor fixtures ("The human body is a deligate...", "Haett block") sitting
  inside the reserved CAT pool where a CAT attempt can serve them. **Not acted
  on — needs Sam's call.** Unpublish + clear `cat_pool`, then top the reservation
  back up.

### The run

13 agents against a shared brief (exact JSON per question type, closed
vocabularies, British spelling, no new Physiological Adaptation, explicit
anti-duplication), all items `cat_pool = FALSE`, all `batch_id
'gapfill-20260728'` so the run reverses with one DELETE. SQL + both validators
committed to `db/seed/gapfill-20260728/`.

**Result: all eight blueprint cells now in range.** Management of Care
14.2% → 16.0%; Physiological Adaptation 18.0% → 15.6% with nothing deleted.
Maternity 3.7% → 7.5%, Pediatrics 3.7% → 7.0%. Standalone 2,978 → 3,600; free
pool 578 → **1,200 exactly**. Every zero-coverage topic now has items.

### Lessons worth keeping

- **My verification tooling failed three times; the authored SQL never did.**
  The validator first parsed column lists as data rows, then matched the word
  "values" inside stems and reported 15 phantom duplicate ids, then counted
  semicolons inside `--` comments (which would have corrupted files, not just
  miscounted). Every failure was mine.
- **Cross-file dedup is the thing to guard.** All 13 agents deduplicated within
  their own batch and reported clean; the one real collision was between
  batches. `qa_report.py` exists for that and should be run before loading.
- **Make bulk loads idempotent from the start.** A session limit killed all five
  loaders at 310/622, and partly-loaded multi-row statements cannot be re-run.
  `ON CONFLICT (item_id) DO NOTHING` made resumption trivial.
- Agents correctly declined two jurisdiction-dependent items (adolescent
  disclosure thresholds; LPN catheterisation scope) — right call for candidates
  sitting a US exam from Ghana.

### ⟶ Reconciled 2026-07-28 (later, local session) — corrections to the above

This branch ran in the cloud, forked from `af3a5a4` (before Slice 10a existed),
and was reconciled onto the local session branch afterwards. Four corrections:

- **Migrations renumbered.** Both versions collided with `main`: `20260818120000`
  was already `five_band_difficulty` (10a) and `20260819120000` already
  `cat_pool_reservation` (10b1). Because the *filenames* differ, git merged them
  in without a conflict — two files claiming one version, which would have broken
  `supabase db push` at the next release. Now **`20260820120000`** (taxonomy) and
  **`20260821120000`** (pool placement CHECK). Ordering still correct: the CHECK
  runs after the migration that creates the `cat_pool` column.
- **The `db/schema.sql` difficulty-CHECK fix was dropped** — byte-identical to
  what 10a had already committed to `main`.
- **"48 editor fixtures" was largely a misread.** The `SAMTEST` rows were called
  fixtures for "carrying raw Tiptap JSON" — but that is our own rich-content
  storage format, not corruption. Only **7** rows were genuinely not questions;
  those are unpublished and out of the pool (not deleted). The other 54 were
  completed. **This is no longer open for Sam.** Verified in dev: pool 2,393,
  all published, all placeable, all three CHECK constraints live.
- **Free pool is 1,192 servable, not "1,200 exactly"** — the headline counted
  every non-reserved standalone row regardless of publish state (§9 of the
  analysis carries the honest figure).

Also stale in the heading above: **"NOT merged / 5 commits"** — 7 of its 8
commits are now on the local session branch (the 8th being the dropped schema
duplicate), pending Sam's approval to merge to `main`.

⚠ Still open, deliberately: the **622 items are unreviewed by a human**. They

## Session — 2026-07-26 (later — two fixes + the CAT recalibration design arc) — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-f66147`. A short bug-fix pass, then a long design
conversation that expanded CAT "Slice 10" from one job into a full
three-sub-slice arc. **6 commits + this log, MERGED to `main`** (fast-forward);
app-layer + docs only, **no migration, nothing built beyond the two fixes**;
prod still `1a6717d`. Every file was announced before touching, per Sam's rule.

### Two fixes (built + verified live)

- **`/tutorial` no longer bounces to `/login`** (`d5553f6`). The middleware auth
  guard matched required sections with a raw string prefix (`path.startsWith(p)`);
  `/tutor` is such a section, so the **public** `/tutorial/exam` walkthrough —
  which merely begins with the same letters — was swept into the tutor guard.
  Fix: segment-aware match (`path === p || path.startsWith(p + '/')`), which also
  hardens every other prefix. Verified logged-out: `/tutorial/exam` loads,
  `/tutor/programmes` still redirects. Found by reproducing in the browser once
  truly logged out (a stale session had masked it).
- **Student sidebar labels hide when the rail collapses** (`f38ccbe`). The
  collapsed rail hides labels via `.is-railed .nav-label`, but the student
  sidebar rendered a bare `<span>` with no class (admin/tutor both use
  `nav-label`), so labels spilled out of the 56px rail. Added the class; fixes
  every student surface (`StudentSidebar` is shared). Verified.

### The CAT recalibration design (discussion only — NOTHING built)

A Sam-driven design session on the last substantial CAT slice, all captured in
`bank-consumption-cat.html`:

- **Difficulty scale 3 → 5 bands** (Very easy … Very hard, seeds −2 … +2). The
  §5.1 mapping, the `CHECK`, and the curator editor dropdown all grow to five.
  Widening to ±2 also widens the day-one CAT ladder — the §7.7 "route 2" fix.
- **Student-facing difficulty is DERIVED from `difficulty_irt`** (new §5.5), not
  the frozen curator label. Cut-offs ±0.5 / ±1.5 (midpoints between the five
  seeds), gated on `difficulty_source`; the curator label is a seed only. Keeps
  the shown difficulty honest as recalibration moves the number, and grounds the
  rigorous "% of candidates who get this right".
- **Recalibration runs over the WHOLE bank**, not CAT-only (§5.3.5) — for
  bank-wide insight, the audience-corrected % readout, and pre-calibrated pool
  mobility. Caveats recorded (practice measured under practice conditions; uneven
  velocity).
- **Runtime = a weekly GitHub Actions Python/`pyirt` job** (§17.6) — corrected
  mid-session from the wrong "Cloudflare Worker → Python service" shape (Workers
  don't run scientific Python; pg_cron can schedule but not run the maths).
- **CAT gets a dedicated `cat_pool`** (new §20) — reserved questions that never
  appear in practice, generalising the readiness reserved-stock mechanism.
  Rationale: exposure — the CAT-takers are the heavy practicers, and only a
  dedicated pool gives them a clean, full-quality exam. Fixes two existing leaks
  (452/500 readiness-reserved items are CAT-eligible today; practice-seen items
  reach CATs). Item security deliberately a weak part of the argument (our CAT is
  formative).
- **Reservation is a visible "Reserve for CAT" editor tick** (§20.5), plus an
  admin CAT-pool page (coverage on difficulty AND the 8 blueprint categories).
  Mutual exclusivity enforced at the editor. Readiness retrofit deferred and NOT
  symmetric (readiness carries pack + position; CAT is flat membership).
- **Pool sizing — a 15-CAT non-overlapping FLOOR, not a cap** (§20.4). Allowance
  stays unlimited. Target **2,400 standalone / 60 cases / 60 trends** (~2,850),
  five-band spread (tails empty today). Grounded in the live dev bank (3,086 items
  = 2,418 standalone / 92 cases / 80 trends, all on three rungs — seeded to make
  CAT runnable).
- **Slice 10 restructured** (§19.4): **10a** five-band difficulty · **10b** the
  CAT pool · **10c** the recalibration job (trails after launch).
- **Case-child calibration resolved: full weight** (the §7.4 half-weight is an
  ability formula, not an item-difficulty one; testlet correlation noted as a v1
  simplification). **All response sources** feed calibration (practice + CAT +
  readiness pack).

### Commits

`d5553f6` tutorial guard · `f38ccbe` sidebar · `0a2f061` 5-band + dedicated pool ·
`7384bad` whole-bank + pyirt · `fe0608f` editor-tick reservation · `ffe27e0`
resolve 6 open questions + 10a/10b/10c · (+ this log).

### Next session

**Build starts** — **10a** (five-band difficulty) is the foundation everything
else sits on. ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod (launch-day only).

## Session — 2026-07-26 (the runner tutorial — Slice 3, the entry points — ARC COMPLETE) — session branch, NOT merged

Branch `claude/work-session-4af632`. **Designed then built Slice 3** — the
entry points + the "done/dismiss" memory — which **completes the whole
runner-tutorial arc (Slices 1–3)**. A long design conversation first, then the
build across 3a/3b/3c, all verified live. **8 commits on the session branch;
NOT merged to `main`, NOT on `prod`** (prod still `1a6717d`). One migration
`20260817120000_tutorial_flags.sql` (two per-user tables), applied to **dev**;
app-layer otherwise. tsc clean bar the 2 known `scoring-roundtrip` errors; new
files eslint-clean (runner's 13 + page's 5 `react-hooks` findings pre-existing,
unchanged). Every file list was announced before touching, per Sam's standing
rule.

### Design settled first (with Sam)

- **The tutorial route is now PUBLIC.** The `<Runner>` needs no user (it's
  prop-driven); login was only an artefact of sitting under `(app)/`. Moved to a
  top-level `app/tutorial/` route with its own chrome-less public layout.
  Grounded in a web check — **NCSBN's own** Candidate Tutorial + UWorld's sample
  are public; our sandbox gives away the *demo*, not the paid bank.
- **Two tables, not one column.** "Finished the tutorial" is a fact about a
  *tutorial*; "don't show again" is about a *popup*. So
  `nclex_tutorial_completions (user_id, tutorial_key, completed_at)` and a
  normalized `nclex_dismissed_prompts (user_id, prompt_key, dismissed_at)` —
  both grow to future tutorials/popups with no schema change. Sam's catch: one
  table would assume one tutorial = one popup = one dismissal.
- **Nothing recorded for a logged-out visitor** — by design, guarded in three
  layers (client skip · server `getUser()` no-op · RLS `auth.uid()`).
- **The four doors.** `/help` hub card + `/help/cat` link · a **permanent**
  dashboard "Exam walkthrough" doorway (evergreen copy, no flag) · a global
  **Help** link in the shared chrome (user menu + mobile sheet) · the **pre-exam
  popup** (the only flag-aware surface).
- **The pre-exam popup behaviour.** Fires after Start on the preflight; shows
  **unless `dismissed_at` is set** (completion never suppresses it — Sam's call);
  **no double-ask same-sitting**; **CAT is not special** (watching is free on
  every mode, so one identical neutral popup everywhere).
- **Two of Sam's catches changed the design:** readiness packs **must** get the
  offer too (a first-timer's one paid shot is the worst place to meet the screen
  cold), and once he pushed on it, readiness needed **no special treatment** —
  one neutral popup fits every surface.

### Built — 3a / 3b / 3c

- **3a — the flag tables + helpers** (`31b1a42`). Migration `20260817120000`
  (both tables, own-row RLS + SUPER_ADMIN admin_all), applied to dev + verified.
  `lib/practice/tutorial/completion.ts` (`markTutorialCompleted`,
  `dismissPrompt`, `hasDismissedPrompt` — each no-ops without a user) +
  `keys.ts` (shared key constants, plain module so client + server import).
- **3b — public route + return + completion write** (`89740b2`). Moved
  `/tutorial/exam` to the top-level public `app/tutorial/`; a guarded `?return=`
  (internal-only, closing an open-redirect) threaded into `buildSandboxData` as
  `exitHref`; completion written when the coach reaches its final step.
- **3c-i — the three standing doors** (`17a5d75`). Dashboard doorway, help hub
  card, help/cat link, global Help link (desktop `user-menu` + mobile
  `account-sheet`).
- **3c-ii-a — pre-exam offer on the session preflight** (`c57be08`). New
  `lib/overlays/practice/pre-exam-tutorial-offer.tsx` (calm NEUTRAL modal, its
  own `pre-exam-offer-*` styles — deliberately not the amber auth-discard
  chrome). `LiveData.offerDismissed?` threaded page → runner → preflight; the
  preflight intercepts Start.
- **3c-ii-b — pre-exam offer on the CAT preflight** (`2fe11c6`). Same shared
  offer wired into `cat-home-client.tsx` (CAT's preflight is its own modal).
- **Close button** (`f0929da`). Sam's catch: backdrop-only close is
  undiscoverable, so the two buttons read as forward-or-forward. Added a visible
  **×** (top-right) + **Esc**, both → back to the preflight, starting nothing.
- **Doc marked built** (`d5aa8cb`).

### Key findings while building

- **There is no single shared preflight.** CAT has its **own** preflight modal
  on the CAT home; custom-built + readiness use the `/session` gate (shown while
  `started_at` is NULL). A CAT attempt is created **already-started**
  (`started_at = created_at`, seen in the data), so it never hits the `/session`
  gate — which is why the offer needed wiring into **two** hosts, not one.
- **"Watching is free" holds everywhere with no CAT-clock fix** — the start
  action the popup defers (`markStarted` / `startReadiness` / `startCat`) is
  exactly what stamps the clock / spends the credit / creates the CAT attempt.
- **This-sitting no-double-ask** via a `tut_watched=1` marker on the return
  address — the session preflight reads it; the CAT home derives the modal's
  initial open state from it (a `useState` initializer, avoiding a
  setState-in-effect), reopening the preflight **losslessly** on return.

### Verified live (desktop + mobile)

Full flow end to end: offer appears → **Take the walkthrough** → tutorial with
the right `return` → **lossless return** (session preflight AND CAT modal) →
**no double-ask** → Start goes straight in → **"Don't show again"** writes the
dismissal row + suppresses the offer next time → **× / Esc** back out cleanly.
Completion row lands for a signed-in user; both tables no-op for anonymous. All
test attempts + flag rows cleaned up afterwards (Sam's real in-progress CAT
`5995c77d` left untouched — he confirmed it was his). ⏭ **Awaiting Sam's test +
merge to `main`.** ⏭ Readiness was exercised via the custom-built variant (same
`preflight.tsx` interception), not a live paid pack. ⚠ Deferred (non-blocking,
in the doc): a tutorial-preflight framing screen; a drift-safety tripwire /
typed anchor; the sandbox-as-reusable-primitive public sampler. ⚠
`PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod.

## Session — 2026-07-26 (the runner tutorial — Slices 1 + 2 BUILT) — merged to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-bca7fa`. **Built the runner tutorial** the
previous session had designed — the sandbox that teaches the exam runner
(all question types + tools) in a safe, unscored, unsaved space. **Slices 1
and 2 (2a + 2b + 2c) complete; merged to `main` at wrap-up** (6 commits;
app-layer, **no migration**; tsc clean bar the two pre-existing
`scoring-roundtrip` errors; new files eslint-clean, `runner.tsx` unchanged at
its 13 pre-existing `react-hooks/refs` findings; verified live throughout;
prod still `1a6717d`). Route is **`/tutorial/exam`**. Every step was
discussed and its file list shown before building (Sam's standing rule this
session: announce new + existing files touched, wait for go-ahead).

### The governing principle held: one runner, never a copy

The tutorial mounts the **real `<Runner>`** — same code that runs a live
exam — fed an in-memory, no-writes bundle, never a look-alike. The only
divergence from a real sitting: fake questions and the save-wiring switched
off.

### Slice 1 — the sandbox runner mode (`a1e6b79`, `50df9a4`)

- **Implemented as a flag, not a third mode.** The runner branches on
  `data.mode: 'live' | 'review'` in 30-plus places; rather than add a third
  value and re-audit them all, the sandbox runs as **live `UNTIMED_LEARNING`
  + a `sandbox` flag** — the tutorial's interaction (answer fresh → submit →
  see the rationale) *is* live UL, so the flag only gates the four writes.
- **Submit is the one action that is NOT a pure no-op.** A pure no-op would
  give no feedback and teach nothing; instead the sandbox scores locally with
  the **same pure `scoreAttempt()`** the server uses (against the dummy item's
  baked-in key) and reuses `mergeSubmitResult` unchanged. save-progress /
  complete / per-Q-timer are skipped; Finish leaves via `exitHref`; the clock
  is frozen (also killing a hydration mismatch — an untimed run shouldn't tick).
- **Dummy questions live in code** (`lib/practice/tutorial/questions.ts`),
  never the bank — the 11 stand-alone types + a 6-child **case study** (one
  per CJMM step, History/Vitals chart tabs with progressive disclosure) + a
  **trend** (Nurses' Notes stimulus). `marks` is **derived by the builder via
  `computeMarksFromKey`**, never hand-coded (the invariant that keeps Submit in
  range). Builder in `sandbox-data.ts`; route `app/(app)/(focused)/tutorial/
  exam/page.tsx` mounts the real runner. Additive `LiveData.sandbox?` +
  `sandboxKeys?` on the runner types.
- **⚠ Gotcha (cost a render):** chart tabs MUST be **v2** (`{v:2, entries|
  tables}`) — the runner's `ChartTabBody` only renders `asMergeTab`/
  `asNarrativeTab`; the legacy v1 `{body,section,visible_from}` array (still on
  an old dev-DB case row I first copied) renders "Nothing on this tab yet."
- **✅ Safety invariant proven:** after a full run + submits, the DB had **zero
  new rows** (attempts unchanged at 146). The id columns are UUIDs and the
  sandbox uses plain string ids, so a write is structurally impossible.

### Route made a namespace — `/tutorial` → `/tutorial/exam` (`f09fde1`)

Sam's call: `tutorial` is a **namespace, not a page**, so future walkthroughs
(builder, dashboard, onboarding) get `/tutorial/<slug>` siblings. Done now
because nothing links to it yet (entry points are Slice 3) and it isn't on
prod — a free rename today, a chore later.

### Slice 2 — the coach layer (`8694870`, `f769841`, `6f5ee28`)

The guided coaching overlay, split into three sub-slices (Sam approved).

- **2a — scaffold.** A floating coach card anchors beside the runner control
  the step names, spotlights it (a box-shadow cutout), and walks Back/Next
  through the **31 steps** (CD's 32 **minus the Mark step** — the runner's
  Mark button is a disabled placeholder, BUILD_LIST 4.7; the tutorial must not
  teach a dead control; restore when marking ships). `goto` is a question
  **key**, not an index (CD's order differs from ours). The coach renders
  INSIDE the runner (sandbox only) and drives it (`onGoto`→setCurrent,
  setGridOpen) — one runner, no duplication. Anchoring reads
  `data-coach="<name>"` markers off the live DOM (sandbox-gated on the topbar
  badge/exit/title/counter/clock/calc; inert pure attributes on
  footer/grid/answerarea/case/cjmm/trend). Ring + card positioned
  **imperatively** (element styles in a layout effect, no per-frame setState —
  dodges `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`). New sandbox-only **"Nothing is
  recorded"** topbar badge.
- **2b — learn-by-doing gates.** Next locks until the step's action is done:
  a **calc gate** while the calculator is open, a **submit gate** while the
  current question has a submitted answer (`gateSatisfied` derived, no latch —
  submit resets on the next goto). Amber hint while unmet. **⚠ Found on screen:
  the opened calculator (z 50) buried the coach card** — split the overlay into
  two sibling layers: scrim (dim + ring) at z 40 stays *below* the calc so it
  shows bright, card at z 55 *above* it so Next is never buried.
- **2c — the header controls.** **Hide** collapses coaching to a bottom strip
  ("Coaching hidden — explore freely" · Resume · End), removes the dim, and
  frees the whole runner to explore — the **badge stays up**, so the safety cue
  never disappears; **Resume** restores the card *and* re-anchors the guided
  context (re-applies the step's goto). **Jump-to-section** = a hierarchical
  dropdown (sections + the 11 types indented). **End** reachable from card +
  strip. `COACH_SECTIONS` starts recomputed for the 31-step array.

### Verified live, end to end

Walked all 31 steps: welcome (centred), every chrome anchor + spotlight,
`goto` switching questions (TF, drag-order, **case → Q12**, **trend → Q18**),
the answer/case/trend anchors, both gates locking/unlocking, jump→Bow-tie,
Hide→strip→Resume. Case panel, CJMM strip + "Case 1 of 1 · step N of 6"
topbar, trend "Trend" badge, and local scoring all confirmed on screen.

### ⚠ dev-console gotcha (recorded so we don't chase it again)

A browser tab **accumulates** console errors across soft-navigations + HMR
and they survive a reload **and a full dev-server restart** — a stale
parse-error snippet (from a mid-edit broken window) and a "deps array changed
size" warning (from adding a dep to an effect mid-session) both persisted in
the old tab. Opening a **fresh tab** gave a genuinely clean console (0 logs).
Trust tsc + a clean render + a fresh-tab console — not the old tab's buffer.

### Not built; next

**Slice 3** — the entry points (doorways from `/help`, the dashboard, and an
optional pre-exam "New here? Take the walkthrough") + one per-user
`tutorial_completed_at` flag, which is the **only DB write in the entire
feature**. ⚠ The plan doc `runner-tutorial.md` still reads "build not started"
— a doc catch-up held for Sam's go-ahead. ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on
the prod Worker (launch-day only).

## Session — 2026-07-25 (calculator · help · CAT 5h · tutorial design) — a long build-and-design run

Branch `claude/work-session-4b4c3b`. **A long, multi-thread session**,
merged to `main` at wrap-up. Threads: **(1)** the on-screen calculator
(BUILD_LIST #16); **(2)** the public **Help section** (#11 + a bonus
article); **(3)** the **CAT time limit 4h → 5h** (§9.3, one migration);
**(4)** the **runner-tutorial** designed end-to-end + plan doc + CD handoff
saved (build deferred to next session). tsc clean bar the two pre-existing
`scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint clean on new files; vitest **+42**
(calculator) **+ 103/103** CAT suites after the 5h change. **Released to
`main` at wrap-up** (prod still `1a6717d` — a `main → prod` release is a
separate step).

### 1 · The on-screen calculator (#16)

App-layer, no migration, vitest +42. Built BUILD_LIST **slice #16**, the on-screen calculator — scheduled the
previous session as a proper slice rather than a dead placeholder (a
*missing* calc on a dosage question withholds a tool the student genuinely
needs, unlike the Mark convenience placeholder). Each shape decision was
discussed with Sam before building.

### Why basic, why build our own, what the real NCLEX gives

Opened with a discussion Sam asked for. Grounded the "what does the real
NCLEX calculator look like" question in a **web search + the Pearson VUE
material** rather than memory: it's the **standard (non-scientific)**
calculator, modelled on the classic Windows Standard calc (digits, `.`,
`+ − × ÷`, `=`, `C`/`CE`/`⌫`, `±`, `√`, `%`, `1/x`, and the five memory
keys), opened from a "Calculator" button and movable. Settled three
things:

- **Basic (non-scientific) is the *fidelity* call, not a shortcut.** A
  scientific calc would be a *stronger* tool than the candidate has on the
  day (trains a dependency that isn't there when it counts), and NCLEX math
  is all arithmetic anyway — no trig/log/exponent item exists. Same
  principle as the §16.6 display-leak work: mirror test day, don't improve
  on it.
- **Build our own, don't import.** A basic calc is ~one small state machine
  + a button grid; building it keeps full control over theming / drag /
  mobile / a11y and takes on no dependency, license, or supply-chain weight
  (extraction test, rule #3).
- **The full *standard* set, not the 4-function minimum** — matching the
  real screen *is* the fidelity win, and the extra keys are cheap.

### The build — `lib/calculator/` (Sam's chosen home)

An app-wide cross-cutting widget in the spirit of `lib/overlays/` /
`lib/toast/` / `lib/hints/` (asked before scaffolding the folder, per the
convention). Pure `calculator-logic.ts` reducer (`applyKey`) + a thin
`calculator.tsx` view (draggable panel + button grid + keyboard entry) +
`styles/calculator.css` (`calc-*`), imported in the `(app)` layout so any
surface can mount `<Calculator>`. The runner is the first (only current)
consumer.

- **Logic** — float-noise-free formatting (`0.1 + 0.2` → `0.3` via
  12-sig-fig rounding), an error latch (`÷0` / `√` of a negative → `Error`,
  cleared only by `C`/`CE`), Windows percent semantics. **42 unit tests.**
- **Runner wiring** — `runner-topbar.tsx` gained a **Calc toggle** beside
  Mark / Grid (its own glyph, same stroke family as the clock/grid icons);
  `runner.tsx` holds `calcOpen` and mounts the panel. The toggle is
  **always present** (the calculator is available in **every mode** —
  study + exam, live + review — a real tool, not a §16.6 leak).
- **Draggable** by the header so it never has to cover the question.
- **Keyboard entry** (digits / `+ − * /` / Enter / Backspace / Esc),
  guarded so it never hijacks a focused text field (e.g. a Cloze fill-in).

### The display fix Sam flagged — a running expression line

Sam ("submissions") caught the real UX gap: a single-line calc shows only
one value at a time, so `10 × 2` reads `10` → `2` → `20` with no sign an
operation is happening. Discussed the fix, then built all three signals:

- **An expression line above the big value** — `10 ×` while pending,
  frozen to `10 × 2 =` after equals. Placed **above** (not below, as Sam
  first suggested) — the big number is the live thing and anchors the
  bottom, and every calculator people know puts the expression on top.
  **Reflects the real left-to-right fold** (`2 + 3 × 4` reads `5 × 4 =`,
  since a basic calc has no precedence) — honest over pretty. Unary keys
  describe themselves (`√(144)`).
- **The pending operator stays lit (armed)** until the next operand.
- **A press-flash** (inset accent ring) on each tap — a ring not a fill, so
  it layers over an armed operator.

Rejected a full scrolling "tape" — overkill, no tape on the real NCLEX
calc, adds clutter. Tests **34 → 42** for the expression line.

### Verified live + documented

Verified in the browser on Sam's own Study session (and confirmed the Calc
toggle also renders in an exam context): `1000 ÷ 8 = 125`,
`0.1 + 0.2 = 0.3` (no float noise), `5 ÷ 0 → Error` (latched, digit
ignored, `C` clears), memory store → clear → recall = `42` with the `M`
flag, drag repositions and keeps state (uncovering the grid), and the
`10 × 2` walkthrough showing `10 ×` → armed `×` → `10 × 2 =` / `20`.
Console clean throughout.

**Documented** (Sam's go-ahead): new **`docs/product-plan/calculator.md`**
as the official design home; cross-linked from the runner doc's Related
docs; BUILD_LIST #16 marked built. ⚠ Runner **mobile** still unoptimised
(separate future pass).

### 2 · The public Help section (#11 + bonus)

Built BUILD_LIST **#11** and then a second article Sam asked for. Public,
top-level, outside the auth boundary (`bank-consumption-cat.html` §3.2):
- **`/help`** — a hub that carries short inline help *and* teasers to full
  articles; ships with the topics that exist, built to grow.
- **`/help/cat`** — "How CAT works", the five §3.2 sections in plain English
  (incl. the one-time §3.1 calibration-honesty note).
- **`/help/readiness-packs`** — "How Readiness Packs work" (fixed 100-Q timed
  assessment, 21-day window, one shot, bands, review-inside-window).
- **`styles/help.css`** (`help-*`). **Entry points** (all verified live): CAT
  home, CAT result footer, CAT preflight, dashboard CAT + pack signal "?",
  pack surface header, and a public-footer link to `/help`.
- **URL decision:** flat `/help/[slug]` (not `/help/bank/cat`) — help is
  content not workflow; grouping comes later as index *categories*, not URL
  nesting. Verified desktop + 375px, console clean.

### 3 · CAT time limit 4h → 5h (§9.3)

The first of the two reopened CAT decisions (#13). **Decided: 5 hours**
(§9.2's "match real NCLEX exactly" applies to the clock too; a ceiling only
cuts off a slow candidate). The **passing standard (§9.1.1) deliberately
left at 0.0** — moving it needs a standard-setting exercise, not a guess, and
is cheapest pre-launch; recorded for later. The "one line" turned out to be a
touch more: the real authority is the attempt row's `duration_seconds`
stamped by `create_cat_attempt`, so it needed **migration `20260816120000`**
(redefine the function to stamp 5h; dev-applied) + the TS constant + a rework
of two tests whose 4h-vs-constant contrast collapsed once the constant became
5h. New CATs are 5h; any in-progress CAT keeps its stamped limit. CAT home
now reads "Up to 5 hours"; 103/103 CAT tests pass. ⚠ cat.html's ~8 "4 hours"
mentions **not yet updated** (deferred doc-catchup, with §3.2's status).

### 4 · The runner-tutorial — designed, documented, handoff saved (build deferred)

A sandbox tutorial that **teaches the exam runner** (all types + tools) in a
safe, unscored, unsaved space — surfaced by the 5h work (real NCLEX's 5h
includes its tutorial) but general to every mode. Worked the whole design
with Sam: the **governing principle — one runner, never a copy** (the
tutorial mounts the REAL runner's components in a new sandbox mode + a coach
overlay; a parallel copy is the mock we ruled out); the **sandbox
architecture** (dummy in-code questions, all server actions no-op'd, sign-off
= zero DB writes); and a **3-slice build plan**. Sam had **CD** build the
walkthrough; reviewed v1 then **v2** (adds hide/resume free-explore, End
tutorial, hierarchical jump-to-section) — adopted. Created + iterated
**`docs/product-plan/runner-tutorial.md`** to build-ready, and **saved the CD
handoff in-repo** at `design-handoff/runner-tutorial/` (concept-not-source).
**Build (Slices 1–3) is next session.**

⏭ Open: build the tutorial (Slices 1–3) · #12 Slice 10 recalibration · #13
the passing-standard decision (5h done) · the deferred doc-catchup (cat.html
§9.3 "4 hours" mentions + §3.2 built-status + help-section BUILD_LIST). ⚠
`PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod.

## Session — 2026-07-25 (later) — The practice builder & the runner: three-tab split, exam-mode leaks stripped, grid rules, session title, toggle icons, calculator scheduled

Branch `claude/work-session-bd5542`. **7 commits.** The first 4
(`bea54d5 → b19aa00`) merged to `main` mid-session (FF-push); the last 3
(`cb35dce`, `163d6c4`, `b1a7434`) merged at wrap-up. App-layer only, **no
migration**. tsc clean bar the two pre-existing `scoring-roundtrip` errors.
**NOT prod** (prod still `1a6717d`).

An iterative, Sam-driven session — a chain of small, well-scoped UI
decisions on the practice builder and the attempt runner, each discussed
before building.

### The three-tab practice builder (BUILD_LIST #2 — closed)

Started by examining the Builder's filter accordion on a phone (#2). Sam
looked on a real phone and decided the **accordion stays as-is** —
collapsing the default-open axes isn't a UX win, it trades away
discoverability + the live per-row counts to shorten a scroll that read
fine, and the 5-open/4-closed split is already a considered call. Also
walked the two nested axes (Client Needs → Subcategory, Subject → Body
System): the `indent` is cosmetic, the `linkedTo` smart-link is the real
mechanism, and they're independent (Client Needs → Subcategory is a real
taxonomy; Subject → Body System is an invented v1 association). Left them.

The real outcome of the review: the Builder's crowded two-tab layout (the
"Filters" tab stacked the Question-pool chips above the nine content axes)
was **split into three tabs — Intent & Mode · Question pool · Content
filters** (`bea54d5`), each pane one job. Pure UI reshaping, no
logic/state/payload change; tab-key type `'mode' | 'filters'` →
`'mode' | 'pool' | 'content'`. The **axis-sub-panes idea was considered and
rejected** (duplicates what the accordion already does, hides the
whole-filter-state view). Verified live: clean split, metas track state,
console clean, desktop untouched, three even tab columns at 375px with zero
horizontal overflow.

### Exam-mode display leaks stripped (BUILD_LIST #10 — built)

The main build (`7e845fb`). During a **live exam** the runner no longer
reveals anything that shows the engine's opinion of the candidate or
pre-announces the exam's structure: the **difficulty pill** (in a CAT it's
a live ability readout — the worst leak), the **subject chip** (Sam chose
to strip this too — "an exam is an exam"), the **`Case N of M` counter**,
the **`CJMM step` label**, and the **six-pill CJMM strip**. Kept: the
question-type pill + Trend badge (they describe the item's format, not the
candidate), the case content panel, and the grid (its cell labels carry no
difficulty/subject — verified). One flag drives it all —
`hideExamScaffold = intent === 'EXAM' && isLive` — so **review restores
everything for free** (a reviewed exam is educational, not a live leak).
Applied to ALL exam modes, not just CAT. ~5 shallow edits + one `examLive`
prop on `RunnerQuestionArea`; the topbar needed no change (just pass
`caseMeta` as `undefined`). Verified live across MCQ/Cloze/Drag-order
standalones + a Sequential case ("Postoperative Atelectasis", Sam's own
session `7d8fbe63`) — DOM-confirmed the case-child strips difficulty /
subject / counter / CJMM while keeping the case panel; study control shows
all of it. Console clean. (Noted, unrelated, pre-existing: a hydration
mismatch in `ClockGroup` — server vs client clock differs by a second on
any timed session; left alone.)

### Intent-based session title (`b19aa00`)

The runner topbar hardcoded "Practice session" for every attempt. Now that
intent drives behaviour, that's the odd one out — the title is now the
**intent frame: "Study session" / "Exam session"** (singular, matching the
2026-07-24 mode-label cleanup that dropped "Exams"). Reads e.g. "Exam
session · Sequential · 25 questions". Applies in review too. Computed in
`runner.tsx`, passed as `sessionTitle`; topbar stays presentational.
Verified live on both.

### Grid rules + topbar grid toggle (`cb35dce`)

Two coupled changes. **(1) Full-hide the grid in live Sequential + CAT.**
There it can't navigate — `onPickGuarded` already made clicks a no-op, so
it was tappable-looking dead furniture (a nervous test-taker taps "1" to go
back → silence). Now it isn't rendered at all (neither the grid nor the
collapsed handle); the question reclaims the space (`.rn-main` is flex:1,
the grid a fixed sibling, so no gap). Gated on
`archetype === 'SEQUENTIAL' && isLive` — the SEQUENTIAL archetype covers
CAT (it dispatches as SEQUENTIAL), and `isLive` keeps the grid in **review**
(there you genuinely can navigate). Matches the real NCLEX (no navigator).
**(2) A topbar grid show/hide toggle** after Mark, mirroring the clock's
eye toggle so both panels have an obvious control (the old collapse chevron
+ thin handle were easy to miss). Per Sam: **additive — nothing retired**;
all controls drive the same `gridOpen` state. The toggle only renders where
the grid exists (null in live Sequential/CAT), so grid + toggle come and go
together. DOM-confirmed: live Sequential has no grid, no handle, no toggle;
Study/Free-Nav has the grid + a working toggle. (Runner mobile is still
unoptimised — a separate future pass, confirmed by Sam; the crowded 375px
topbar is pre-existing, not this change. The toggle's label-hide is
pre-wired via `.rn-grid-toggle-label`.)

### Real toggle icons (`163d6c4`)

The clock visibility toggle rendered `◉`/`◌` glyphs that read as neither a
clock nor an eye. Sam tried an eye (rejected — a bare eye signals
"visibility" without naming *what*) and chose a **clock face** — plain when
showing, slashed when hidden. The grid toggle got the **matching
treatment** (plain grid / slashed grid) so the two adjacent topbar toggles
read the same way. Inline SVGs. tsc verified clean (via the main checkout's
`node_modules`, resolved upward from the worktree — the worktree has none).

### The calculator — scheduled as slice #16 (`b1a7434`)

Sam's second ask: the runner needs a calculator (the real NCLEX gives one
for dosage / med-math / fill-in items). Discussed placeholder-vs-build: a
**disabled** calc on a dosage question withholds a tool the student
*needs* (worse than the Mark placeholder, a mere convenience), and the real
thing is small — so a dead placeholder was the wrong middle ground. Sam
chose to **schedule it as a proper slice** rather than build now. Captured
on BUILD_LIST as **#16**: basic 4-function, draggable pop-up, available in
every runner mode (a real exam tool, not a leak — it *raises* fidelity),
topbar entry next to Mark/Grid, and — per Sam — built once as an
**app-wide reusable widget** (spirit of `overlays`/`toast`/`hints`), not
runner-only. Home folder TBD at build (ask-before-scaffold).

### Dev cleanup

Deleted the **7 test attempts** created this session on dev (all
`CUSTOM_BUILT`, `IN_PROGRESS`, zero readiness-credit refs → safe; children
cascaded). 0 today remaining, 138 older preserved, 0 orphans. Dev only.

### Open / next

Still open on the list: **#11** (`/help/cat` page + route), **#12**
(Slice 10 — weekly recalibration, the substantial one), **#13** (the two
reopened CAT decisions), and now **#16** (the calculator). Prod is behind
`main`; next release ships migration `20260814120000` (no-op on prod data)
+ `20260815120000` (engagement clock). ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on
prod (launch-day only).

## Session — 2026-07-25 — The engagement clock, the small fixes, the mode-name decision, and the resume rule

Branch `claude/work-session-4fe7ee`. **9 commits; the first 6 merged to
`main` mid-session (`6287478 → 76fe846`, FF-push), the last 3 on the branch
awaiting merge.** App-layer + **one migration** `20260815120000`
(dev-applied). tsc clean bar the two pre-existing `scoring-roundtrip`
errors; eslint adds nothing new (the runner/page.tsx findings are the
pre-existing baseline, confirmed by stashing). **NOT prod.**

### The small fixes (BUILD_LIST #3, #4, #5)

Cleared the whole "Small fixes" group. **#3** (`f0b5901`) — the public
`/bank-access` builder demo's CAT button said "Start Exam — CAT", a button
the real Builder no longer has (§10.7.1 made CAT a doorway); now "Go to
CAT", verified live. **#4** (`0266425`) — five stale slice-number build
notes on the tutor library, all describing features that have since
shipped: the embedded-questions picker, note→unit-activity attachment,
draft publishing, and the all-notes "filters and search land in a future
slice" line (search + lenses already exist). Also de-jargoned the dormant
`comingIn` template + its pills ("coming soon" / "Soon") so a future
disabled block type can't leak a slice number. Verified all four live
strings on the tutor library. **#5 — closed as NOT broken.** The note
claimed "every curator bulb's `<ul>` loses its markers, runs together as
one block." Opened a live curator bulb (case-study editor): it renders as a
clean, spaced, indented list — the note had over-generalised the student
dashboard's *already-fixed* squashing onto the curator bulbs, which use
`.auth-help-bulb-list` (styled) and were never broken. The only residue is
latent fragility (the shared `<Bulb>` ships no default list style), left
for a rainy day. Left the "Recent" view's *"ships with a later slice"*
tooltip alone on purpose — genuinely unbuilt, no jargon, an honest "not
yet".

### The mode-name decision (BUILD_LIST #1)

Walked the §15.1 naming proposal (Study: Tutor·Untimed·Timed / Exam: Timed
exam·Exam simulation·CAT) against the interim labels adopted 2026-07-24.
**Sam chose to keep the interim labels for now**; §15.1 retained as a future
alternative. **Flagged against it, specific to *our* product:** the
proposal borrows **"Tutor"** for the first study mode (from UWorld, which
has no in-product human tutors) — but MyNclex has real tutors (Tutored
Programmes), so a "Tutor" mode would collide with an actual person; the
§15.1 avoid-list didn't catch it. Recorded in BUILD_LIST + §15.1 status
line (`6f98526`). My analysis on record: keep them through launch but treat
naming as genuinely unfinished (not "settled") — the weak spots are "Free
Navigation" (jargon) and "practice" doubling with the tutor PRACTICE
quiz-kind; the winning future move is probably the proposal's structure
with "Tutor"→"Learn". (Sam asked me not to record that last analysis.)

### ⭐ The engagement clock (BUILD_LIST #6) — the main build (`da850cc`)

Makes `(STUDY, TIMED_FREE_NAV)` — "Timed practice" — honour the promise its
config always made (`clock: 'engagement'`, "pauses if you step away,
resumable"). Until now the runner derived the countdown from `started_at +
duration` with no intent branch, so it drained while the student was away,
identical to the EXAM wall clock.

**The whole design was settled with Sam step by step before any code:**
- **Which mode:** only Study→Timed practice. Every EXAM mode keeps the wall
  clock *on purpose* (an exam should run whether you're there or not); the
  untimed Study modes have no clock. The discriminator already existed — the
  `clock` field ('engagement' vs 'wall'), which is a function of (intent,
  mode). The same mode id `TIMED_FREE_NAV` under EXAM stays wall-clock.
- **What "away" means:** page hidden — tab-switch, screen-off, backgrounded,
  closed — reusing the per-question time engine's own Page-Visibility signal
  (which already treats tab-switch as away). For a *study* clock, letting a
  student freeze the clock to look something up is legitimate.
- **How much away-time is forgiven:** all of it, even across a full close and
  a return days later (Sam's call).
- **Durability:** because away-time is forgiven across a full *close* (which
  wipes the browser's memory), the engaged total must be **persisted
  server-side**; the client resumes from it. This is the shift from
  "wall time since start" to "engaged time used".
- **How often to save:** Sam pushed on the heartbeat and we **dropped it** —
  saves on submit / navigation / page-hide only. A device dying mid-question
  forgives that one question's engaged time, which lands in the student's
  favour; no background timer. (Walked through the "phone dies" case in
  detail with a worked example until it clicked.)

**Built in three layers.** Migration `20260815120000`: nullable
`engaged_seconds_used` column on `nclex_attempts` + monotonic,
**mode-guarded** `nclex_record_engaged_time` RPC (guards owner + IN_PROGRESS
+ (STUDY, TIMED_FREE_NAV), so a stray client call can never freeze an EXAM
clock). **The load-bearing fix:** `page.tsx` lazy expiry now tests engaged
≥ duration for engagement mode, NOT wall `now ≥ started_at + duration` —
otherwise a reopened engagement attempt (wall-expired but barely
engaged-used) would be force-killed on return. Server action
`recordEngagedTimeAction` + client hook `use-engagement-clock.ts` (own
visibility listener, separate accumulator from the per-question timer —
copy the idea, not the machinery; `performance.now()` makes it
clock-tamper-proof). Runner branches `remaining` off engaged vs wall.

**Verified live on dev** (attempt `69462afe`): clock froze at 14:17 through
8s hidden; DB `engaged_seconds_used`=42 matched the screen while wall was 61
then 242; **reload resumed at ~14:03 (900−42), NOT 10:58 (900−242)** — all
away-time forgiven. **Control** (attempt `0e4a0541`, EXAM *same mode id*):
clock drained 14:46→14:14 while hidden, its `engaged_seconds_used` stayed
NULL — proving the intent split works and the RPC guard holds. Console
clean. **This build also closed #7** (Study-timed and Exam-timed were
mechanically identical) **and #9** (intent drove no behaviour — it now
selects the clock). **Sam tested it on a real device and approved; merged.**

**⚠ Known minor:** an engagement *timeout* (rare — students usually finish
early) still back-dates `ended_at` via `nclex_expire_attempt`. A direct
correction was attempted and dropped — the owner has no direct UPDATE on
`nclex_attempts` under RLS (SUPER_ADMIN only), so fixing it means an RPC.
Cosmetic, deferred.

### The resume rule (BUILD_LIST #8) — `1a64969`, not yet merged

Sam's own insight reframed it: the behaviour was never broken — an Exam **is**
resumable while its wall clock still runs (`page.tsx` finalises it only once
the clock drains); the **copy** was lying. Three docs disagreed (parent §15
"no timed resume" / §6.1.2 "STUDY only" / the code resuming everything
non-CAT). **Settled in favour of the code's coherent behaviour:** every
non-CAT attempt is resumable; the only difference is whether the clock
waited (Study froze, Exam kept draining). Fixed the Exam start-note
("cannot be resumed" → "the clock keeps running if you leave") on the real
builder + public demo, **split CAT out** (it genuinely can't resume, keeps
"cannot be resumed"), corrected the stale resume-fetcher comment, and
reconciled both plan docs. No behaviour change, no migration. Verified live.
**Honesty catch:** softened an over-asserted readiness-pack resume line (that
path has its own credit + review-window rules I hadn't traced).

### State at close

  `20260815120000` (both no-ops on empty prod data).

## Session — 2026-07-24 (later) — The modes: investigated, two cut, renamed, and the stale copy behind them

Same branch `claude/work-session-fad16e`, continuing after the mobile work
above. **6 commits, merged to `main`**; app-layer + **one migration**
`20260814120000` (dev-applied). tsc clean bar the two pre-existing
`scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint clean on new files (the 13 in
`runner.tsx` are pre-existing, confirmed by stashing). **NOT prod.**

### It began as an investigation, not a build

Sam asked for a report on the quiz **modes** — how intent and mode relate,
and what the runner actually does with them. Reading the plan against the
code produced **four findings**, and they shaped everything after.

**The mode framework lives in three documents, and the one meant to unify
them was never written.** `bank-consumption.html` §15 has the framework,
attempt-creation §6.1.2 the intent×mode tuple, runner §8/§10.1/§15 the
timer, submit model and navigation. Runner **§4 — "Per-mode behaviour
matrix"** lists exactly the axes Sam asked about and its entire body reads
*"To be filled in."* That empty section is the root cause of the drift
below.

**Finding 1 — the engagement clock does not exist.** The builder tells
STUDY students *"Engagement-clock — pauses if you step away"*, and §6.1.2
says STUDY uses an engagement-clock where EXAM uses a wall-clock. But
runner §8 — **settled later, 2026-05-09** — defines the countdown as
running toward `started_at + duration_seconds` with **no intent branch**,
and the code matches §8: `elapsedSec = (nowMs - startedAtMs) / 1000`. No
pause logic; no pause columns on `nclex_attempts`. A STUDY timed attempt
burns its clock while the student is away. The word "engagement" does have
a real implementation — the per-question time engine — but §8 explicitly
says that is a different clock that does not sum with this one.

**Finding 2 — STUDY-timed and EXAM-timed are mechanically identical.**
Same duration (90s × count, from a trigger branching on **mode only**),
same wall clock, same nav, feedback and submit model. Label and copy are
the only difference.

**Finding 3 — resume is a three-way contradiction.** Parent §15: "impossible
for any timed mode". §6.1.2: rewrites to "STUDY intent only". The code
(slice 4.5a): resumes **everything except CAT** — `mode ≠ 'CAT'`, no intent
condition, at either call site. Meanwhile the Builder tells EXAM students
*"single sitting · cannot be resumed"* and an abandoned EXAM attempt **does**
reappear in the Resume banner.

**Finding 4 — `intent` drives no behaviour at all.** Across the entire
session route it is read in **exactly one place** — `preflight.tsx`, to
print the word "Study" or "Exam". Every mechanical difference comes from
`mode` + `duration_seconds`.

### The two cuts — and the mirror nobody would have looked for

Sam's read was that some modes conflict. The investigation supported it more
sharply than "conflicting": **each removed tuple was mechanically identical
to its twin under the other intent**, so each was a duplicate card
distinguished only by a label making a promise the runner does not keep.

- **(EXAM, Untimed Test)** — untimed means `duration_seconds = NULL`, so
  there was no clock either way. "Exam framing without time pressure" is
  precisely what the STUDY version already is.
- **(STUDY, Timed Sequential)** — forward-only reproduces exam mechanics
  (NCSBN: answer in order, no going back, no skipping) and teaches nothing
  on its own. An exam constraint wearing a study label.

**The blast radius was bigger than the student builder.** `nclex_tutor_quizzes`
has **no intent column** — intent is DERIVED at launch (MOCK→EXAM,
PRACTICE→STUDY) — so its `kind_mode_tuple` CHECK is a mirror of the attempts
one. Left alone, a tutor could author a quiz that exploded on launch against
the tightened constraint. MOCK lost Untimed Test, PRACTICE lost Timed
Sequential: **7 pairs → 5**. Verified first that no existing quiz used
either pairing; the migration **refuses to run** if one turns up rather than
silently rewriting a tutor's authored mode.

**Three dev attempt rows converted, not deleted** (Sam's call — one is a
COMPLETED attempt, and deleting it would punch a hole in a student's history
for a naming decision they never made). This is also **load-bearing for
display**: four read paths resolve a mode's label via
`(intent === 'STUDY' ? MODES_STUDY : MODES_EXAM).find(...)` and fall back to
the raw id, so a row left on a removed tuple would print `UNTIMED_TEST` in
History. Order matters — the conversion runs **before** the constraint
rebuild, since adding a CHECK validates against existing rows.

**Verified on dev, not assumed:** conversions landed exactly as predicted
(STUDY/Untimed Test 3→5, STUDY/Timed Free Nav 0→1, both removed tuples at
zero); constraint definitions read 6 and 5; and **both removed tuples were
proven actively REJECTED** by a live rollback-only UPDATE rather than read
off the definition. In-browser: Study 3 modes, Exam 3, tutor form 3 for
PRACTICE and 2 for MOCK.

Landing shape — **STUDY**: Untimed Learning · Untimed Test · Timed Free Nav.
**EXAM**: Timed Free Nav · Timed Sequential · CAT.

### The naming arc — and the four competing vocabularies under it

Sam opened the naming question. The structural observation that unlocked it:
**"Untimed Learning" and "Untimed Test" are named after the axis they SHARE,
not the one that separates them** — both lead with the clock, when the real
difference is *when the truth arrives*. The names point at the wrong
variable, which is why the structure was hard to see.

A **web search** grounded the options rather than inventing them: AMBOSS
ships "Study Mode"/"Exam Mode" as its two frames (validating our *intent*
names, and ruling those words out one level down); **"Tutor" is the
NCLEX-native term** for immediate rationale across UWorld, Archer and
Bootcamp; UWorld's "Untimed" means exactly our Untimed Test; and NCSBN's own
rules make forward-only the *authentic* mode, not an arbitrary one. Recorded
as `bank-consumption.html` **§15.1 — PROPOSED, NOT APPLIED**, together with
the reasoning for **rejecting numbered mode names** ("Study Mode 1/2/3" — the
number becomes the headline while meaning drops to subtitle; position-based
numbers break exactly when the list changes, as it just did; higher numbers
imply a ranking that isn't real) and a **naming avoid-list**
(Practice/Mock are tutor quiz *kinds*; Study/Exam are the *intent* names).

Sam then set **interim** labels — STUDY: Learning · Untimed practice · Timed
practice; EXAM: Free Navigation · Sequential · Computer Adaptive Testing
(CAT). The shape has its own logic: one "Learning" mode plus two "practice"
modes separated only by the clock, which is their only real difference — and
"Free Nav" could be dropped from Sequential's sibling precisely *because*
Timed Sequential had just left STUDY, leaving nothing to contrast against.
"Exams" was dropped from the EXAM pair after it read twice over on the
surfaces that prefix with the intent themselves ("Start Exam · Sequential
Exams").

**⚠ The rename exposed FOUR competing label lists.** `filter-config`'s two
arrays, plus one each hardcoded in `runner.tsx` and `preflight.tsx` **keyed
on mode alone**, plus a fourth in `lib/tutor-quiz/format.ts`. The two
mode-keyed ones cannot express a per-intent name, so renaming would have left
the runner topbar and preflight still saying "Untimed Test" for the same
attempt the builder called "Untimed practice" — and they had already drifted
into **three spellings of one mode** ("Timed · Free Nav" / "Timed · free
navigation" / "Timed · free nav"). All four now resolve through one
`modeLabelFor(intent, mode)`.

The tutor side is the sharpest case: `formatQuizMode` now **requires** the
quiz kind, because TIMED_FREE_NAV is the one mode allowed under both and is
named differently under each. **Proven on real rows** — the tutor quiz list
renders the *same mode id* as "Timed practice" for the PRACTICE quiz and
"Free Navigation" for the MOCK. Nine call sites updated; every one already
carried `quiz_kind`. A tutor was previously configuring quizzes in words no
student ever reads.

The public `/bank-access` demo was resynced and **stays hardcoded** (Sam's
call — it must render for a signed-out visitor with no data behind it); the
file now names the source of truth and the cost of that choice, since it had
already drifted a full naming generation.

### The intent card

Sam's eye: the icon sat on a line of its own above "Study"/"Exam". Icon and
name now share the top row, description spanning beneath. **159px → 128px**,
and it lands **twice on mobile** where the cards stack — both intent cards
plus the first mode card now fit on one 375px screen, where the Mode section
had been entirely below the fold. CSS-only; the card's `position: relative`
turned out to be vestigial.

### ⚠ The stale copy underneath it all — Sam spotted two, looking found three

- **The preflight showed a build note to students:** *"In 4.1 every mode runs
  with per-question submit and free navigation (Untimed-Learning behaviour).
  Timer, sequential lock, and batched submit land with slice 4.5."* Our slice
  vocabulary on a student screen, and false since 4.5 shipped.
- **The footer described the wrong mode — the serious one.** `statusMessage()`
  returned Untimed-Learning copy for **every** non-CAT mode. A student in a
  timed sequential exam — on a screen where Prev is disabled and no rationale
  appears until the end — was told to expect a rationale after each submit and
  a free Next. It also named "Untimed Learning", a label dropped hours earlier.
- **"Next → →".** Three of five `primaryLabel`s baked in an arrow while
  `RunnerFooter` appends one, so the **most common button in the runner**
  rendered a double arrow on every question but the last, in every mode.
- The Mark button's tooltip read *"Mark-for-review · slice 4.7"*.
- `RunnerFooter`'s fallback hint was *"MCQ wiring lands in slice 4.1.4"*.

New `lib/practice/runner/mode-brief.ts` holds `footerBrief()` +
`preflightBrief()` **and the archetype mapping**, so the copy sits beside the
rule it must agree with; `runner.tsx` imports `archetypeFor()` rather than
keeping its own. The footer prefixes the mode's **live** label, so a rename
can never again leave it naming a mode that doesn't exist.

**A deliberate omission:** `preflightBrief` states feedback timing,
navigation and the length of the clock — but **not** the mode card's "pauses
if you step away" claim, because that clock isn't built. Stating the minutes
is true under either design; promising it pauses is not, and the preflight is
the last screen a student reads before committing to a sitting.

Verified live on real attempts, one per archetype — preflight UL and
FREE_BATCHED (timed, *"You have about 38 minutes"*), footer SEQUENTIAL
(matching its actual controls: Prev disabled, "Submit & continue"),
FREE_BATCHED and UL.

### The open list is now in BUILD_LIST

All 15 flagged items captured at the top of `BUILD_LIST.md` as **"⏭ THE OPEN
LIST"**, grouped by what each needs — Sam's decision, small ready-to-run
fixes, the four findings, bigger CAT work, operational. **Sam's pick for next
session is recorded there: the engagement clock**, with its two design
questions (what counts as "away"; how much time is credited when a student
vanishes) captured so they don't have to be rediscovered.

### ⏭ Open

- **Prod is behind** — `origin/prod` at `1a6717d`. The next release ships
  migration `20260814120000`; a no-op on prod data (no attempts, no quizzes).
- Everything else: see the BUILD_LIST open list rather than duplicating it
  here.
- ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` (live key) still not on the prod Worker.
