## Session — 2026-07-24 — The Custom Quiz Builder made mobile-compatible

Branch `claude/work-session-fad16e`. **1 commit (`08eba9d`), NOT merged to
`main`**; app-layer, **no migration**. tsc clean bar the two pre-existing
`scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint findings on `practice-builder.tsx` are
pre-existing and unchanged (verified by stashing). **NOT prod.**

### Session start — a correction to the record

Every SESSIONS entry through 2026-07-23 says the CAT arc and the Bank
Dashboard are "NOT prod". They are. `origin/prod` sits at `1a6717d`,
*"Release main → prod — the CAT engine end to end, plus the student Bank
Dashboard"* (2026-07-23 12:30), so the whole arc shipped. Nothing was
queued for release at session start.

Sam confirmed he had looked at the Bank Dashboard and was happy — which
closes the one item the previous session left open (it had been merged on
his instruction without a test pass, then released).

### What was NOT worked on

**§16.6 (exam-mode display leaks) was proposed and parked by Sam** — it
forms part of a wider runner pass he wants to do later, so the difficulty
chip stays for now. The analysis is not wasted; it is recorded here
because the five leaks each need a decision and four of the five sites are
now located: the difficulty pill at `runner-question-area.tsx:176`,
`Case N of M` at `runner-topbar.tsx:95`, the CJMM strip in
`lib/practice/runner/case/cjmm-strip.tsx`.

My recommendation on the record: hide the difficulty pill, the case
counter and the CJMM strip in exam mode; **keep** the subject chip and the
question grid (neither leaks the ability estimate — the grid cannot even
navigate, since a CAT has no back nav).

### A slice-status audit, and one item that was already done

Asked what remained on the CAT slices. Checking the plan against the code
rather than trusting the doc found:

- **Slices 1–8: built, and now on prod.**
- **Slice 9's "Builder EXAM-intent CAT option" is ALREADY BUILT** — it is
  the "Go to CAT" handoff at `summary-panel.tsx:76`, done 2026-07-21 when
  the button that claimed to start a CAT but started an ordinary attempt
  was fixed. The doc was never marked; the 07-21 session even listed
  "marking §10.7.1/Slice 9 built" as a follow-up held for Sam.
  **Paperwork, not work.**
- **`/help/cat` (§3.2) is genuinely unbuilt — and so is the route to it.**
  Grepping for `/help/cat` returns **zero references anywhere in the app**.
  §10.7.2 assigned "What CAT is + link to /help/cat" to Slice 6a, and the
  CAT home carries no help link at all. So a student meeting an adaptive
  exam for the first time has no explanation outside the preflight. This
  is the last real build item before Slice 10.
- **Slice 10 (weekly recalibration)** remains the only substantial slice
  left, and its priority is still raised for the §7.7 reason: on the
  three-rung difficulty ladder the exam cannot visibly track a strong
  student and the readiness probability saturates near 100%.

### The session's work — the builder on a phone

Sam supplied a Claude Design zip ("NCLEX Quiz Builder Redesign") and
scoped it tightly: **the current build only, mobile compatibility only,
discuss before code.** Of the five files in the zip only
`Current Build + Mobile` was in scope; the two `current.png` screenshots
are blank and `1a.png` is from the wizard variant.

**What CD proposed was less than it looked.** Its "mobile version" is the
same component with four switches (tighter padding, full width, stacked
columns, rail → sticky bottom bar). Everything else is left to reflow.

**What the current build actually was.** `builder.css` had **no media
query at all** across 986 lines. `.bk-grid`'s second track is a fixed
340px, wider than a 375px phone's ~319px content box, so the grid allotted
the *first* track — the column holding every control in the builder —
**0px**, and the rail overflowed 31px. Measured in-browser: page
`scrollWidth` stayed at exactly 375, so **the clipped strip was
unreachable rather than merely off-screen** — the `+` stepper and the
recap values were gone, not just cut off.

### The finding CD could not have known

`/student/bank/practice` is **one of the four student mobile tabs**
(`lib/nav/student.ts:30`). At ≤768px the page already carries a fixed 56px
bottom tab bar with 64px reserved in the content padding. **CD's sticky
action bar lands exactly there.** A design partner working from a brief
cannot see the nav system — the same class of gap as the dashboard's six
data mismatches, and the reason concept-not-source keeps earning its keep.

### The proposal Sam approved

The principle: on desktop the builder is a two-pane workbench; on a phone
there is no "beside", so it becomes **a form filled top-to-bottom**.

1. **Stack `.bk-grid`.** The whole structural fix in one media query.
2. **The summary moves to the end of the form — and NOTHING is dropped.**
   `<SummaryPanel>` is already the last child, so one column lands it
   there for free. CD shed the recap, the breakdown, the custom stepper
   and the start note *because it chose a bar*; in flow there is room for
   all four. The recap arguably matters **more** on a phone, where the
   filters have scrolled far out of sight.
3. **A thin fixed launch strip** carrying only the live count and Start.
   It earns its space by doing two jobs: the count is the builder's whole
   feel (tick a filter, watch it move), and the Filters tab measures
   **~3,000px** on a phone, so without it a filter change means scrolling
   three screens to launch.
4. **Collapse the fixed column counts** — mode and intent cards to one
   across, pool chips 3 → 2. `.bk-checks` needed nothing; its
   `auto-fill/minmax` already collapses on its own.
5. **Leave the accordion alone this pass** — five of the nine axes default
   open. Making that viewport-dependent is not CSS (the open state is
   React state, so it needs client detection and risks a hydration
   mismatch), and the clean version changes desktop behaviour Sam didn't
   ask about. Judge it on a real phone first.

### Built

`styles/builder.css` (+~170), new
`app/(app)/student/bank/practice/mobile-launch-bar.tsx`, plus edits to
`summary-panel.tsx` and `practice-builder.tsx`.

- **The strip is 61px**, pinned flush on the 56px tab bar (117px of
  permanent furniture — more than the ~48px estimated, because a 44px tap
  target is the floor). **z-index 19**, deliberately under `.m-tabbar`
  (20) and far under the drawer (45) and account sheet (55), so a launch
  button can never float over an open menu.
- **One shared `startLabelFor()`** so the strip and the panel cannot drift
  on the CTA. The strip takes the short form but **keeps Quiz/Exam** —
  that word is the difference between a session you can pause and one you
  cannot, too consequential to lose on a one-tap surface.
- **CAT reads `MIN_ITEMS`/`MAX_ITEMS`.** The handoff still carries the
  pre-NGN **"75–145"** that §9 corrected to 85–150, and its CAT button
  still *starts* a CAT, which §10.7.1 forbids. Both would have sailed
  through if the prototype had been followed literally.
- Rendered on every viewport and hidden above 768px **by CSS, not a JS
  width check** — the open/closed state would otherwise have to be known
  at render time on the server, which it cannot be.

### Three defects found by LOOKING AT THE SCREEN

None were visible in the stylesheet; all three appeared only once the
layout was fixed and the page could actually be read.

- **The resume banner rendered ONE WORD PER LINE.** Its icon and its
  button pair are both `flex-shrink:0`, so the text column absorbed the
  entire squeeze down to ~50px. Actions now drop to their own full-width
  row and split evenly, which also yields two proper tap targets.
- **The tab strip broke into four ragged fragments** — each tab is a label
  plus an inline meta note, and side by side at 375px they interleaved.
  Label and meta now stack; the two tabs split the width evenly.
- **The header pointed at nothing.** "The count on the **right** updates
  as you go" was true of the desktop rail only. Restated without a
  direction, it is true of both layouts.

### Verified live

Work column **0px → 315px**; **zero horizontal overflow at 375px AND
320px** (no element past the viewport at either); strip flush on the tab
bar with no gap or overlap; the strip's count tracked a real filter change
(**2,024 → 2,361**) and **agreed with the panel exactly**; CAT showed
**"85–150 / Go to CAT"** on both surfaces; console clean. **Desktop
confirmed untouched** — two columns, sticky rail, strip `display:none`,
and the header copy is the only shared change.

**Not run: vitest.** No test imports any of the touched files, and this
worktree has no local `node_modules` — precisely the condition in CLAUDE.md
where running vitest leaves a stray directory that breaks Turbopack's root
resolution. Zero coverage to gain against a live dev server to lose.

- **The commit is NOT merged to `main`** — awaiting Sam's test + approval.
- **Docs not updated** (held for Sam's go-ahead): `mobile-responsive.md`

- **The accordion question** — five axes open, ~3,000px of Filters tab.

## Session — 2026-07-23 — CAT Slice 9: the student Bank Dashboard, built in four stages

Branch `claude/work-session-ad4124`. **7 commits, merged to `main`**;
app-layer throughout, **no migration**. tsc clean bar the two pre-existing
`scoring-roundtrip` errors; eslint clean on touched files; vitest
**393 → 412** (the suite was 341 at session start — +71 across four new
pure modules). **NOT prod.**

### Where it started — the concept changed before any code

The previous session left Slice 9 planned with cards 1–7 settled and cards
8 (the readiness ring) and 9 (the doorways) unresolved, because both needed
data we didn't have: the ring needed a formula nobody had designed, the
doorways needed a grand-total loader and a Journey Tracker that isn't built.

Sam reshaped the concept around data we can actually read, took it back to
Claude Design, and returned with a new canvas (turns 1–6, variants
1a → 2d). I read the whole CD thread in-browser plus the exported file, and
**2d was chosen** — readiness as its own full-width panel, each signal
carrying the real signature graph from its own page.

### Six things the prototype said that our data doesn't

Checked before building, against the live schema and real rows rather than
against the design's story:

1. **⚠ The readiness pack has no pass/fail.** The card showed a green
   "Passed" pill and 2c drew a pass tick at 65%. Our packs return one of
   **four band words** (Building / Approaching / **Ready 75+** / Excelling).
   "Passed" appears nowhere in the product, and 65 is not our number.
2. **⚠ There is no "At standard" for a CAT.** `CatVerdict` is binary. The
   three-step meter invented a middle rung that can never light.
3. **⚠⚠ The design could punish a student for what she hadn't bought.**
   Two of the four signals are separate purchases, and ○ meant both "you
   fell short" and "you never attempted this". A bank-only student with
   2,000 answers at 78% would read *"Not yet ready — 1 of 4 signals met"*,
   with no route to the top band that doesn't involve spending more money.
   **This was the finding that mattered most.**
4. **The 1,500 target was invented** — the same class of thing as the old
   68% ring, smaller. Sam reframed it as an *evidence threshold*, which
   changed what it is rather than papering over it (see below).
5. **A red 55% pill on a practice quiz** — a practice set has no pass mark,
   so red invents a verdict.
6. **The CAT mini-chart didn't read at 120px** — a green smudge with the
   dashed passing line, the entire point of the chart, invisible.

Plus two reverts of decisions already settled in the cards 1–7 walk: the
resume banner and recent rows had regained an invented subject
("Pharmacology · Untimed practice"), which a filter-built quiz cannot have.

### The four decisions that replaced the design's readiness card

Settled with Sam, then built into one pure file
(`lib/home/student/bank/readiness.ts`, 27 tests) so none of them can erode
into the view:

1. **Three signal states** — met / not met / **not yet attempted**. The band
   counts only what was **attempted**, and an unattempted signal renders as
   a doorway ("Sit a pack →"), never as a mark against the student.
2. **The band reuses the packs' vocabulary** — Building / Approaching /
   Ready. Without this the page carried *three* vocabularies for one idea.
   **Excelling excluded** (a pack earns it with a real score out of 100;
   three yes/no signals can't claim it), and **Ready needs ≥2 attempted
   signals** — clearing accuracy alone is real but thin ground for telling
   someone they're ready for an exam.
3. **Volume is the GATE, not a signal.** Results say how well you did;
   volume says how much we know. As a fourth signal, 1,600 answers at 45%
   would tick a box and read as progress. Below the gate the band is
   withheld — **which also supplies the first-week empty state the handoff
   README explicitly left undesigned**.
4. **⚠ The copy never promises odds.** Sam's framing was "at about 1,500
   answers we could do an estimate of your chances" — but a real pass
   probability needs outcome data from nurses who have sat the NCLEX. The
   threshold buys **reliability** and says so; a test asserts the words
   "chance" and "probability" never appear.

**The accuracy bar is 75, not the design's 65** — 75 is the boundary our own
packs call Ready, the one number documented as pass-associated. A lower
figure would put two bars on "good enough" and only one would trace to
anything. ⬜ Open whether it's too strict; it's one constant.

### A level correction the plan had wrong

§19.4's walk called the mastery bars "the 8 **L1 category** bars". There are
only **four** L1 client-needs categories; the **eight** the design draws are
the **L2 subcategories**. Bars and nudge therefore work at L2 — which is
also the level `weakestSubcategory()` and the builder's `?subcat=` deep link
already speak, so **"Drill it" reuses the link the readiness report already
uses** rather than inventing one.

### The streak — shared, then deliberately un-shared

Stage 1 extracted the programme home's streak maths into a shared pure
module. **Sam reversed it**: a programme streak and a bank streak measure
different achievements from different tables and should be free to define a
"study day" differently — **which they immediately do**. The bank's now
requires an **answered question** (SUBMITTED / AUTO_SUBMITTED), not merely
an attempt row: a streak you can hold by building a quiz, looking at
question 1 and closing the tab isn't measuring study. Verified against SQL
before looking at the page — the strict rule removes exactly two days on the
test account (20 July, 28 June), both days an attempt was opened and nothing
answered, taking the longest run 6 → 5. The page then showed exactly that.
**CAT counts toward the streak** (it is study) but never toward **mastery**
(§13.2). `submitted_at` is coalesced to `updated_at`/`created_at` — ~10
SUBMITTED rows carry none, and filtering on it alone would punch silent
holes in the streak.

### ⚠ Two cap bugs, one already live

`getHistoryAttempts()` limits to 50 rows, so counting its length reported
**"50 past sessions"** — the true figure was **51**, and it would have said
50 for ever after. Both the session count and the CAT count now come from
real count queries; a CAT sat more than fifty sessions ago would otherwise
have silently stopped being counted.

### Found by looking, not by tests

- **The shared 💡 bulb opened off-screen.** It anchors `right: 0` — right for
  the curator toolbars it was built for, where the bulb *ends* a row — but
  beside a card title on the left it was clipped; at 375px it then
  overflowed the other way. Both fixed page-scoped.
- **The bulb body has no stylesheet anywhere in the repo**, so the global
  reset strips a `<ul>`'s indent and markers and every bulb's list runs
  together as one block. Scoped fix here; **⬜ still true of the curator
  bulbs**.
- **Sam caught the 💡 glyph used as decoration** on the lowest-scoring card:
  it's the app's help affordance everywhere else, and a bulb that does
  nothing when clicked costs us the real ones. Now a flag.
- **Sam caught the missing doorway icons** — restored from the app's own
  `<NavIcon>` set. CAT takes `chart` rather than the sidebar's `target`,
  which the sidebar shares with Readiness Packs; survivable in a long nav
  list, reads as a mistake when the rows are adjacent.
- **Thin-slice bars.** "Basic Care and Comfort 100%" sat at the top of the
  list off **10** questions, above an 88-question bar at 59% — the
  least-tested area reading as the strongest. Each bar now shows its count
  ("100% · 10").
- The date line lost its comma to `en-GB`; the streak divider dangled on
  mobile; long blueprint names were ellipsised (now wrap — there's no
  tooltip on a phone).

### Verification

Every figure checked against SQL rather than eyeballed: accuracy **55%**
(159 of 287), all eight category bars, **51** sessions, **3** CATs, **3**
unclaimed credits, and the off-canonical subcategory correctly absent
(233 classified − 2 = the 231 shown). **Two panel states no account could
show were exercised deliberately and reverted** — lowering the gate rendered
the band ("Approaching · 1 of 3 signals met"), and nulling the pack + CAT
rendered the invitations ("Building · 0 of 1 signal met", singular handled).
Desktop + 375px throughout, no horizontal overflow, console clean.

**⚠ NOT tested by Sam.** He was tired and instructed the merge without a
test or audit — so the first real user-eyes pass on this dashboard is still
outstanding, and the four open items below have had no review.

### ⏭ Open

- **⬜ Sam's own test/audit of the dashboard** — the merge went in without it.
- **⬜ The accuracy bar at 75** — traceable but strict; one constant.
- **⬜ "Ready" requiring two attempted signals** — my call, unreviewed.
- **⬜ Recent activity still uses emoji** (📝 🎯 🧠) under a column of proper
  icons; the handoff itself calls them placeholders.
- **⬜ The curator bulbs' unstyled `<ul>`** — same defect, unfixed there.
- **⬜ Slice 9's other two pieces**: the Builder's EXAM-intent CAT option
  (§2.3) and public `/help/cat` (§3.2).
- Recurring: vitest before `next dev` in a fresh worktree leaves a stray
  `node_modules/.vite` that breaks Turbopack root resolution — hit again at
  session start (third time). Remove `node_modules/`.
- ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod; the `main → prod` release
  backlog keeps growing.

## Session — 2026-07-22 (later) — CAT Slice 8, and the CAT allowance wired end to end

Branch `claude/session-7f5864`. **6 commits, all merged to `main`** (FF
`3b4d60a → cbcfeb7`); app-layer throughout, **no migration** (every column
already existed). tsc clean bar the two pre-existing `scoring-roundtrip`
errors; eslint clean on touched files; vitest **341** (unchanged — the work
was UI + data plumbing).

### 1. Slice 8 — CAT review wiring, and the frozen-nav bug under it — `6b2e28f`

The Slice 8 entry points (summary → Review, History → summary, exit) were
already wired by Slice 7 + the 2026-07-21 resolver fix. **Verifying that
wiring end to end surfaced the real work:** a finished CAT could not be
navigated in review — the pane was pinned to the last question, Prev/Next/grid
all inert. Root cause `runner.tsx`: `current` was derived to the newest item
for **all** CAT modes; that pinning is right for the LIVE exam (grows one item
per turn, no going back) but froze review. Scoped to `isCat && mode === 'live'`;
a finished CAT now navigates like any other review (proved live — a grid jump
rendered the case panel + CJMM strip + HIGHLIGHT per-element feedback). Also:
"Q N / 85" now shows in review (was "Adaptive length"); Exit from a CAT review
returns to its **summary page** (not CAT home); the CAT-home history gained a
direct **"Review"** shortcut beside "View report", guarded to rows with
reviewable items. **Bonus fix the extra column exposed:** the CAT home scrolled
the whole content area sideways on a phone (a column-flex item whose
`margin:0 auto` cancelled the stretch → sized to the table's min-width) — fixed
with `width:100%` on `.cat-home` so the table scrolls in its own container
(CLAUDE.md UI #3).

### 2. CAT allowance — Phase 1: the admin field (§15.5) — `1ec1198`

The `cat_allowance` columns and the `create_cat_attempt` enforcement guard
already existed (Slice 1 + Slice 3), built inert. Added the first deferred

admin product form (a deliberate act, not a bare number box). Bank-family only
— hidden for READINESS SKUs, and the server forces NULL there regardless of
what's posted. The catalogue list shows the allowance in each bank pass's

### 3. CAT allowance — Phase 2: the student side — `f638003`

**The one missing wire: the snapshot.** At activation, `activate.ts` now copies
the product's `cat_allowance` onto the `nclex_subscriptions` row it inserts —
which switches the whole feature on (enforcement, the CAT-home count, the

activation). Plus the exhausted-state **upsell** ("Get more bank access →" to
`/bank-access`, replacing a dead disabled button), and the allowance line is
now a **banner** in both states (neutral for "N of M remaining", amber for

`cat_allowance = 6` onto the new subscription (matches the product);
most-generous-wins (§15.5.2) demonstrated — an account holding a legacy-NULL
(unlimited) pass alongside a capped one correctly reads unlimited; the count +

`/bank-access` cards now show the allowance ("3 CAT exams included" /

The bank checkout showed only "Full question bank" + a price; the specifics the

## Session — 2026-07-22 — CAT: the timeout defect fixed, and the second defect it was hiding

Branch `claude/work-session-ba4acf`. Two build commits + docs.
App-layer plus **one additive migration** (`20260813120000_cat_expire_with_verdict.sql`,
dev-applied). tsc clean bar the two pre-existing `scoring-roundtrip.test.ts`
errors; eslint clean on touched files; vitest **306 → 328**.

Took the top carried-forward item from §19.4.6. It turned into two fixes,
because the first one uncovered a defect it had been hiding.

### 1. A timed-out CAT now gets a verdict — `2088352`

`checkTermination`'s `TIME_LIMIT_HIT` branch was correct and tested, but
reachable only from `playTurn` — and a turn needs an answer to score. A
time-out is exactly the case with no answer. Meanwhile the runner's generic
auto-expire fires the instant the countdown hits zero, is mode-blind, and
writes none of the `cat_*` columns. It won every race, so the branch was dead
code and every CAT timeout produced no verdict.

**The fix is a sibling of `playTurn` minus its scoring step** —
`lib/practice/cat/expire.ts`: load history, re-estimate, apply **the same
`checkTermination`**, persist. Reusing the rule rather than reimplementing it
is what stops a time-out *between* turns resolving differently from one
*inside* a turn — the property that made a separate SQL implementation the
wrong shape.

The branch lives in `expireAttemptAction` because **both** triggers (the
countdown in `runner.tsx`, the lazy catch-up in `page.tsx`) call it. Elapsed
time is derived from the row **server-side**: this call decides a verdict with
no answer behind it, so unlike the turn path it must not be influenceable from
the browser.

Two deliberate differences from `nclex_expire_attempt`: **no `final_score`**
(the engine writes none either; §13.5 forbids showing a CAT a raw score, and a
number no surface may display is one that eventually leaks onto one), and
**`ended_at` back-dated** to the true deadline rather than the detection
moment, which on the lazy path can be days later. *Planned as "back-date
both"; corrected while building* — the engine's `NOW()` is right, because that
branch is reached because an answer just landed, and back-dating there would
put `ended_at` before that answer's `submitted_at`.

**The duplicated 4-hour constant, collapsed.** Defined twice with nothing
keeping the copies in step (`TIME_LIMIT_SECONDS` vs `C_DURATION_SECONDS`)
while the runner expired on the row and the engine judged against its own.
`checkTermination` now takes `timeLimitSeconds` from the **attempt row**,
required and undefaulted so no call site can silently inherit the constant;
a test reads the migration file to keep the creation default honest. **This is
what makes §9.3 a genuine one-constant change** — a move to 5 hours applies to
new exams while an in-progress exam keeps the limit it started under.

**A guard the tests corrected.** The first version asserted the rule must
return `TIME_LIMIT_HIT`. It does not — confidence is checked first by design,
so a strong history returned `CONFIDENCE_REACHED` and the guard rejected a
legitimate timeout. The precondition is *the clock*, checked directly.

**Verified against the real path**, not a simulation: a cloned live CAT
back-dated past its limit, then the actual lazy expiry fired in the browser.
`TIMED_OUT` · `TIME_LIMIT_HIT` · `BELOW_STANDARD` · **48** items · `ended_at`
back-dated exactly · `final_score` NULL · one `SKIPPED` row. **48 and not 49**
is the detail worth keeping: 49 were served, the 49th was on screen unanswered
— counting it would inflate "You answered N questions" *and* could tip a
student over the 85-item line on a question they never saw.

### 2. ⚠ The second defect — the unmeasured ending — `108bf97`

With a timeout finally reaching the report, the page read **"Below standard"**
directly above **"We're ~93% confident you're above our standard."** Seen on
screen, on real data.

Not an implementation slip: `report-derive` was quoting **§13.1 verbatim**.
§13.1 defines copy for two outcomes, both reading the verdict as though it
*follows* the estimate — true for `CONFIDENCE_REACHED`, `MAX_ITEMS_HIT`, and a
time-out with 85+ answered. It breaks for a time-out **under** the minimum,
where §9.3 forces `BELOW_STANDARD` on insufficient evidence whatever the
estimate says: the verdict stops following the estimate while the probability
carries on being computed from it.

§13.1 *half*-anticipated it with the near-boundary variant it calls "typically
a max-length or time-out finish" — but that sorts by how **confident** we are
(40–60%), and this case is about how much **evidence** we have. They come
apart exactly here: a student can run out of time at question 48 while sitting
at 93%. **Latent since §13.1 was written, invisible only because the first
defect stopped anything reaching the page. One bug was hiding the other.**

**Settled with Sam and built the same session.** Two claims, stated
separately: the **result is a fail** (real NCLEX counts running out of time as
one) and the **measurement is declined**. The fail leads in the headline —
*"not enough information"* as a headline reads as "this one doesn't count",
i.e. a free do-over. **No probability appears anywhere on the variant**; the
ring shows the evidence gap (*"48 / of 85 needed"*) instead, and a test
asserts no `%` and no "confident" reaches this copy. The body advises on
**pace, not content**: every other variant sends the student to the category
breakdown, which here would send them back to do the same thing again. All
figures derived, so §9.3 moving to 5 hours needs no copy edit.

**Display-only — `cat_verdict` stays `BELOW_STANDARD`**, so nothing needs an
override. But one shared `isUnmeasured()` keeps three surfaces in step: the
report, the CAT home history row (*"Not passed — ran out of time"*), and the
compare-to-previous strip (**"Timed out"**, not "Below" — the dot still plots
since the estimate is real, but labelling it Below beside measured sittings
would read as a decline in ability that never happened). Category breakdown
kept (Sam's call). Known and accepted: the **85-item cliff** — answer 84 and
you get this page, 85 and you get a full verdict.

**Two things fixed by looking:** the full-length pace read *"closer to 1.6
minutes"* like a spreadsheet next to the student's own "about 5 minutes each"
(now **95 seconds**, still derived), and the compare strip's 52px result
column wrapped "Timed out" onto two lines beside single-line rows.

Verified desktop + 375px, console clean.

### 3. Slice 6c — the transition escalation, on a now-real idempotency guard — `<pending>`

The last piece of Slice 6, taken after the two fixes above. Two parts — the
UI was the slice, the guard fix was the prerequisite it turned out to need,
found while checking that Retry would be safe. App-layer, no migration; vitest
328 → 341. cat.html §19.4.8.

**Part 1 — the idempotency guard was inert.** §19.4.4's `p_expected_item_id`
was meant to catch the lost-response retry (RPC replays instead of recording
the old answer against the new question). But `turn.ts` set that id from its
OWN `loadCurrent` (= the server's newest item), computed in the same request —
so it always equalled newest and the replay branch could never fire. Built,
tested at the wrong altitude (the test only asserted the value passed
through), unreachable — **the same shape as the §19.4.7 time-limit dead
code**. It never bit because a lost-response retry was a rare manual act;
Slice 6c's Retry button makes it a routine one-tap action on flaky mobile
data, so it was fixed first. `expectedItemId` now comes from the CLIENT (the
question it displayed, captured before submit, reused on Retry); `turn.ts`
also honours a replay (a replay means the turn already landed, so the
locally-computed score AND decision are stale and discarded — without which a
replay whose stale decision was `stop` would falsely complete a still-live
attempt). The real replay test §19.4.4 lacked now exists.

**Part 2 — the escalation (the slice).** A CAT turn is a server round-trip:
fast almost always, but on mobile data it can take seconds or drop. The
0–300ms dim + `inert` shipped in 6a/6b; 6c adds the timed climb — dim (no
spinner) → spinner (300ms) → "Still loading…" (3s) → Retry (10s), plus an
error phase (message + Retry, no spinner) on failure. Pure state machine
(`lib/practice/cat/turn-transition.ts`, 16 tests) + thin timer hook
(`use-turn-transition.ts`) + overlay (`cat-transition.tsx`, OUTSIDE the inert
wrapper so Retry is reachable, in a contained panel, `role="status"` +
`aria-live`). Footer primary dead whenever blocking (incl. error, where
`submitting` has gone false) so the overlay's Retry is the single way on.

**Verified live (desktop)** by injecting artificial latency / a forced
failure (reverted after): stuck phase, error phase (footer confirmed
disabled), Retry recovering and advancing, slow turn → reset → advance.
**Mobile taken structurally** — the overlay is a `max-width:300px` centred
card with no responsive branches, can't overflow 375px; escalation not driven
on mobile (Select-N/Matrix touch input resisted scripting). "No spinner under
300ms" is unit-tested, not eyeballed.

**Seen on screen, not 6c:** a live CAT question shows a **"Difficulty ·
Medium"** chip — the engine broadcasting its own opinion of the item to the
student mid-exam. That is the **§16.6** leak, the next carried-forward item.
Noted, not touched.

### Notes

- **Session-start gotcha, recurring:** running vitest before the dev server in
  a fresh worktree creates a stray `node_modules/.vite`, which breaks
  Turbopack's root resolution and kills `next dev` (`We couldn't find the
  Next.js package … from the project directory`). Same as 2026-07-13. Remove
  `node_modules/` and it serves clean.
- **Dev fixture** `aaaa1111-0000-4000-8000-000000000001` (cloned from
  `4113c191`, 49 items / 48 answered, back-dated) left in place for Sam to
  view — **cleanup candidate**.
- ⏭ NEXT = **§16.6 exam-mode display leaks** (the difficulty chip, seen above) ·
  Slices 8–9. Open: whether `4113c191` (pre-fix, no verdict) is backfilled or
  left as a historical artifact; dev fixture `aaaa1111` (the unmeasured-ending
  demo) is a cleanup candidate once Sam has viewed it. ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY`
  still not on prod.

## Session — 2026-07-21 — CAT: re-pointing the termination popup, and the timeout defect underneath it

Branch `claude/work-session-093b0c`. Four commits, **not merged to `main`**.
App-layer throughout — **no migration**. tsc clean (bar the two pre-existing
`scoring-roundtrip.test.ts` errors); eslint unchanged from baseline on touched
files; vitest **306 pass**.

Started as the small loose end from Slice 7 — the report page existed but
nothing at termination pointed at it. Two rounds of Sam's pushback turned it
into three defects, one of them on the engine.

### 1. The termination popup — `09975c0`

A finished CAT was falling into the bank-Builder variant of the shared results
popup: *"Session complete"*, a raw **"X of N correct"**, and buttons for
*Review attempt* / *Build another* / *Exit to bank* — with the Slice 7 report
reachable only by going back to CAT home and finding the row in the history
table.

The score is the specific harm, and §19.4.5's finding is why: raw correctness
in a CAT converges toward half for everyone, so the first real CAT read
**41.6%** under a 98%-confident pass. As the headline number, that tells a
passing student they failed.

Three decisions worth keeping (all in cat.html §19.4.6):

- **Branch on `mode`, not `source`.** A CAT is stored as
  `source = 'CUSTOM_BUILT'` (§19.4.2 — there is no `'CAT'` source value), so
  source cannot distinguish it. `mode` is written on the same insert.
- **`isCat` is a prop, not part of the fetched context** — via the context
  there would be one frame where the popup did not yet know it was a CAT, and
  it would render the forbidden score in it.
- **The reason sentence reuses the report's `itemsAdministeredLine()`**, so
  popup and page cannot describe the same ending two different ways.

**Sam's `source = 'CAT'` proposal — agreed in principle, deliberately not
done.** Semantically right (a student controls no filters; it is not
"custom-built"), but three gates currently apply to CATs *only* because they
look CUSTOM_BUILT — one of them the bank subscription check in
`session/page.tsx` — plus two coupled CHECK constraints. Worth its own slice
with those audited; not worth coupling to a popup fix.

### 2. "Are you sure bug 1 was fixed?" — it was not — `296e789`

It was fixed **in the popup only**. `resolveAttemptExitHref` is also called in
`session/page.tsx`, feeding four *other* exits (runner topbar, the CAT
preflight's Back, two runner pushes) — all still landing on
`/student/bank/practice`. The hardcoded popup href was papering over the cause.
The **resolver** is now CAT-aware and `mode` is **required** on its input type,
so a new call site cannot silently inherit the Builder destination.

**Checking that turned up a real bug.** Loading the other dev CAT, the popup
rendered *"You answered **0** questions. The engine reached confidence at
question **0**."* — with 49 in the grid behind it. Both halves false.

`itemsAdministeredLine()`'s fall-through asserts confidence was reached. That
is safe on the report, which only calls it once a verdict exists; it was reused
here **outside that precondition**. The verdict/no-verdict split is now
explicit and travels as one object so the two cases cannot drift.

### 3. ⚠ THE FINDING — a CAT that times out is never reported

Sam: *"so if someone is sitting the exam and at 70 and timed out it won't be
reported?"* **Correct — and it is the normal timeout path, not an edge case.**

`checkTermination` case 3 handles the clock exactly as §9.3/§9.4 specify — at
or above `MIN_ITEMS` resolve by side, below it `BELOW_STANDARD`, returning
`TIME_LIMIT_HIT` **with a verdict**. But it only runs **inside a turn**, and a
student cannot take a turn after the limit: the runner's **mode-blind
auto-expire** fires `expireAttemptAction` the instant the countdown hits zero.
That path writes none of the CAT columns.

**So the auto-expire always wins the race and §9.3's `TIME_LIMIT_HIT` branch is
effectively dead code.** Every CAT timeout produces no verdict; a student who
answers 149 questions and times out on the last is told the exam "ended early"
and shown nothing, while the ability estimate sits in `cat_theta_before` on
every item row. Confirmed on dev `4113c191`: `TIMED_OUT`, 49 items, `ended_at`
exactly `started_at + 4:00:00`, all three CAT columns NULL.

**A correction worth keeping.** This was first written up as *"a CAT shows no
clock at all"*, off an "Untimed" pill — which is `statusLabel`, the
**review-mode** label on a finished attempt. Sam pushed back; live CATs do show
a countdown (`isTimed` is just `duration_seconds !== null`, and a CAT's is
14400). A label had been read off a completed attempt and generalised to the
live exam. Correcting it made the defect **worse**: with a clock visibly
running to zero, the silent auto-expire is simply what a timeout *is*.

Written up as cat.html **§19.4.6**, and listed at the **top of the
carried-forward list** marked as a defect rather than an open decision,
sequenced *with* the reopened §9.3 — if the limit moves, this is the code that
has to honour it.

### 4. The Builder's CAT card — `d8b11c3`

Spotted in passing, then Sam asked for both halves fixed.

- **"75–145 questions" in three places** (mode card, summary rail, public
  `/bank-access` demo) — the **pre-NGN pair**, left behind when §9 corrected
  the range to 85–150 on 2026-07-14. CAT home was right, so two surfaces
  disagreed about the exam's length. All three now interpolate
  `MIN_ITEMS`/`MAX_ITEMS`.
- **"Start Exam — CAT" actually started something, and it was not a CAT.** It
  called `createAttemptAction` with `mode='CAT'` and a hardcoded count of 75 —
  the **Builder's** RPC — producing an ordinary attempt with 75 pre-snapshotted
  items and no engine state, which the runner would then play adaptive turns
  against.

**§10.7.1 settled this on 2026-07-19 and it was never built**: the Builder is a
**doorway**, not a launcher — exactly one place may spend a CAT, because two
paths that spend an entitlement drift and the failure mode is a student burning
a 4-hour one-shot through whichever forgot a gate. Its explicit instruction was
"do not wire it to `create_cat_attempt`"; it had been wired to the *wrong* RPC
instead. CTA is now **"Go to CAT"**, routes to `/student/bank/cat`, creates
nothing, with a note saying so.

### Verified live

Both popup endings on dev, desktop + 375px: `5b97141d` (85 items,
CONFIDENCE_REACHED) reads the confidence sentence and its CTA lands on the
report, whose own items line matches the popup's **verbatim**; `4113c191`
(TIMED_OUT, 49, no verdict) reads "ran out of time after 49 questions", claims
no result, and lands on the abandoned view which independently says "question
49". Topbar Exit lands on CAT home. Builder reads 85–150 in both places, "Go to
CAT" navigates, and the CAT attempt count + newest timestamp are **unchanged**
after the click. Console clean throughout.

The popup was exercised by temporarily forcing `showResults` open against real
attempts (reverted; confirmed absent) — a CAT has no score pill to reopen it
from. That drives the real component and the real Server Action, but **not** the
trigger line itself (`setShowResults(true)` in `onCatTurn`, unchanged); seeing
that fire needs a CAT sat to termination.

**Not verified on screen:** the public `/bank-access` demo takes the same
constants and type-checks, but its CAT toggle could not be driven from the
browser console.

## Session — 2026-07-20 — CAT Slice 7: the results page (built from CD) + two reopened decisions

Branch `claude/work-session-dfba40`. Three commits, **merged to `main`**. App-layer
throughout — **no migration**. tsc + eslint clean on touched files; vitest
**280 → 306**.

### What was built — Slice 7, the CAT results page (§13) — `7462682`

The surface a student lands on when a CAT ends, and re-opens from the history
list. One page, both contexts. All seven §13 regions in the plan's priority
order: verdict → readiness probability → items administered → trajectory graph
→ Client Needs breakdown → clinical judgment → compare-to-previous → CTAs. The
abandoned CAT is a **separate surface**, not the report with its regions
blanked.

Structure: pure `lib/practice/cat/report-derive.ts` (+26 tests) · server loader
`lib/practice/cat/report.ts` · route
`app/(app)/student/bank/cat/result/[attemptId]/` (page, view, chart, abandoned
view) · `.catr-*` in `styles/cat.css`, mobile-first at 768px. The CAT home's
history rows now link in.

**Two values are derived on read, never stored** (§12.7.12): the readiness
probability, via the same `readinessProbability()` the stopping rule calls at a
different threshold — so page and engine cannot disagree — and the
clinical-judgment read from the stored case answers.

**The trajectory shift** is the subtle bit. The engine stores theta *before*
each question (that is what it knew when it chose the item); the graph plots the
estimate *after* each answer. Each point therefore takes the NEXT item's
before-value, and the last takes the attempt's final theta. Getting it wrong
draws a plausible graph that lags the truth by one question and nothing else
would catch it — three tests guard the shift alone. The shaded band is the real
95% interval drawn from the engine's own `CONFIDENCE_Z`, which is what makes the
§9.5 ceiling case legible: a student who ran to 150 can *see* the band still
straddling zero.

### The design round-trip

Two rounds with Claude Design (concept-not-source). The brief was written to
stand alone rather than lean on the repo — schema gives column names, not why
the ordering is what it is. Round 1 came back faithful to §13 with two good
unprompted additions (the confidence band; a termination-aware items line) and
four things needing a decision. Sam's calls: **cut the raw θ / standard-error
tiles** ("a student can't even understand what that is"), cut duration
(a §13.6 v2 item my brief leaked in), **remove the per-result calibration note**
(§3 settles that the caveat is disclosed once on a help page, not per attempt),
keep the chip grid. Round 2 applied all four and added the "take another CAT"
secondary CTA — a gap in §13 itself, which promises a next sitting in the
Below-standard copy and offered no route to one.

### ⚠ What the real data revealed — the category percentages

Verifying against the real run, the page showed **19–48% per category beside
"Above standard — 98% confident."** Checked against the database: **not a bug.**
Mean item difficulty was **0.765** against an ability of 0.48, so Rasch predicts
~43% and the student scored **41.6%**. The engine is exactly right.

The consequence is the finding: **a CAT serves questions at the edge of a
student's ability, so the raw success rate converges toward roughly half for
everyone, at every level.** Absolute category percentages are near-uninformative
in a CAT, and shown bare the page reads as contradicting its own verdict — with
the student likely to trust the small number over the headline. §13.2's mandated
footnote only anticipated the *partial-credit* effect, much the smaller of the
two. The shipped footnote names both and tells the student to read the
categories against each other, not out of 100; rows are ordered weakest-first.

**This strengthens Sam's earlier critique** that the page is thin on "what did I
get wrong, what next": the region §13.2 calls the most actionable on the page is
weaker than it looks. §13.6's drill-down + a remediation path (the readiness
report's "next moves" cards are the working precedent) is now the strongest
remaining gap on this surface.

### Two decisions reopened — noted, nothing changed in code

**§9.3 — the 4-hour time limit** (`beeea85`). Sam: real NCLEX gives 5. The 4 was
deliberate, but three things argue against it — it contradicts §9.2's "match real
NCLEX exactly" principle applied one section earlier; a time limit is a *ceiling*,
so shortening it can only cut off a slow student, making the "less punishing"
rationale inverted; and the first real CAT's pace (1.4 min/item) puts a 150-item
run at ~3h35m, close enough that a 2-min/item student times out near Q120.
Against a straight 5: real NCLEX's 5 hours includes tutorial and breaks, so 5
hours of pure answering is *more* generous than the real exam. Open: 5h / 4.5h /
keep 4h.

**§9.1.1 — the passing standard** (`7976528`). Raised by Sam on seeing the report.
The finding that opens it: **the standard was never independently chosen.** Three
things in the engine are zero for unrelated reasons — `PRIOR_MEAN`, the Medium
difficulty rung, and `PASSING_THETA`. The first two are principled; the third
inherited zero because zero was already the centre of a scale we invented. So
"Above standard" means "better than the middle of our own difficulty labelling."
Sam's argument: for an exam this consequential, passing should not sit at the
midpoint by construction. Not cosmetic — the real run cleared 0.0 by **0.025**
(band [0.025, 0.935]), so a bar even 0.1 higher means that exam does not stop at
85. Probabilities at candidate bars recorded (0.0 → 98.1%, 0.25 → 83.9%, 0.5 →
46.5%, 0.75 → 12.2%). **⚠ Recorded trap:** a 0.5 bar would dissolve §7.7's
saturation — a consequence, *not* a reason. Sam's root-cause read, recorded as
his: the engine works, the **three-rung ladder** is the problem, so widen the
spread first; setting a precise bar on a blunt scale is false precision. Route
out is **standard setting** (NCSBN uses expert panels, not pass statistics), so
outcome data is not the only path. ⚠ Unlike §9.3, changing this **re-interprets
every CAT already sat** — verdicts are computed live from stored theta — so
decide whether history is re-read or pinned.

### Verification

Driven in the browser against the real attempt `5b97141d` at desktop and 375px:
all regions render off live data, **18 case chips** = the 3 scheduled cases × 6
children (§7.4 confirmed on screen), tap-to-place moves the marker, no
horizontal overflow, console clean on a fresh tab. Two layout bugs found and
fixed by looking: the desktop grid auto-placed every card, giving the items line
a row as tall as the verdict card beside it and stranding it above a large gap
(fixed with explicit rail/main wrappers); and the chart's "passing standard"
label collided with the trace, with no safe side to move it to since a passing
line runs above zero and a failing one below (fixed by reserving padding outside
the plot). The abandoned surface was exercised with a **temporary dev attempt
row**, since nothing in dev reaches that state — **row deleted, dev back to one
CAT attempt.**

### Outstanding

- The generic results popup still fires at termination — §19.4.4 flagged Slice 7
  would replace it; the report exists but the popup is not yet re-pointed.
- Clinical-judgment thresholds are provisional (§13 defines the region, never the
  bands).
- Slice 6c, §16.6 exam-mode leaks, Slices 8–9.
- ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod; the `main → prod` release backlog
  keeps growing.

## Session — 2026-07-19 — THE CAT BUILD: Slices 1–5 (engine complete) + 6a/6b (first CAT sat end to end)

Branch `claude/work-session-5c5c81`. The CAT engine went from nothing to a human
sitting a complete adaptive exam. Fourteen commits; **280 tests**, tsc clean,
five migrations dev-applied.

**Decisions taken before building** (all in `bank-consumption-cat.html`).
**§15.5 CAT allowance** — a nullable `cat_allowance` on products, snapshotted
onto subscriptions, enforced by one guard; a *ceiling*, not a balance, so
consumption is derived and the double-spend bug class never arises;
most-generous-wins across stacked rows; an abandoned CAT burns the attempt and
the preflight says so up front. **§10.6 engine runtime** — the §4.2-vs-§10.5
"conflict" was a misreading; the deciding fact is that `lib/scoring` is pure
TypeScript and §10.2 requires it reused unchanged, so **TypeScript computes and
the PL/pgSQL RPC persists and selects**. **§10.7** — creating a CAT is ONE atomic
act, deliberately NOT mirroring the readiness create/start split (an unstarted
row would need a `started_at` filter in every attempt query, and missing it in
§15.2's exposure check would silently withhold questions from a student's next
three exams). **§10.7.1** — the Builder hands off rather than launching, so
exactly one place can spend a CAT.

**§9.1 corrected to the 95% rule** (Sam spotted it). §9.1 defined termination
only as "the SE band doesn't cross zero" and illustrated it with a **±1 SE**
band ≈ 68% confidence — apparently never chosen, just a by-product of writing the
example with one SE. It would have shipped "you're ready" verdicts at ~84%
confidence, wrong about **one time in six**. Later confirmed verbatim against the
**2026 NCSBN test plan**: "95% certain that the candidate's ability is either
clearly above or clearly below the passing standard."

**Slices built.** **1** schema (`20260808120000`) — additive; a doc error caught
while building it (§12.7.5 specified the audit FK as UUID → `nclex_bank_items(id)`;
the real key is `item_id TEXT`, so the migration would have failed as written).
**2** `lib/cat/` — the Rasch maths, 56 tests. Estimator is **EAP with a
Normal(0,1) prior**, because maximum likelihood diverges when a student is
all-right or all-wrong — the state after question 1 of every CAT — and the
prior's spread answers §6's unquantified "high SE" (1.0). The §7.4 half-weight is
applied to the **log-likelihood**, so the level-move and the certainty-gain halve
together structurally rather than as two rules that could drift. **3**
`create_cat_attempt` (`20260809120000`). **4** the core loop (`20260810120000` +
`lib/practice/cat/`). **5** cases + trends (`20260811120000`) — 3 cases per exam at
positions **randomised within three windows** and derived from `attempt_id`, so
the schedule is replay-stable without needing a column; children snapshotted
**one per turn**, because `cat_theta_before` records the estimate each question
was actually faced with. **6a** the CAT home + one-shot preflight. **6b** the turn
loop.

**§7.1 changed before Slice 4**: selection is **nearest-neighbour**, not a ±0.1
window. Every `difficulty_irt` is seeded from Easy/Medium/Hard, so all 2,353
eligible items sit on three values — a ±0.1 window would have matched *nothing*
at theta 0.35 and fired the §7.5 fallback on nearly every pick, destroying the
one signal that tells us the bank is too thin.

**THE FIRST COMPLETE CAT, sat by Sam in the browser.** 85 items,
CONFIDENCE_REACHED, ABOVE_STANDARD, theta 0.480 / SE 0.232, 121 minutes. **It
terminated by 0.025** — one more wrong answer and the band would not have
cleared. §9.5's equivalence held on live data (P(above) 98.06% against the 97.5%
bar). 3 cases at the scheduled 8/35/59, evidence weight **76.0 across 85 items**
(the 18 case items correctly counting as 9), blueprint balance 22/21/21/21.

**Four bugs found by LOOKING AT THE SCREEN** — every one passed tsc, eslint and
the test suite first. The preflight put the irreversible confirm **above** the
cancel on mobile, exactly where the thumb lands. Backdrop click did nothing,
though CLAUDE.md UI #2 requires it. **"No questions in this attempt." flashed
between questions** — the stub for a *broken* attempt, shown mid-exam, because a
stored index advanced before the new question arrived; now **derived**, which
also delivers §10.1's "stays visible, dimmed" for free. And the footer told CAT
students to "Submit to see the rationale, then Next" — controls that do not
exist in a CAT. Separately, **`cat_next_item` gained an idempotency guard**
(`20260812120000`): it had assumed the newest item is always the one being
answered, which is false on a retry and would mark a student on a question they
never saw — and adding a DEFAULTed parameter silently **created an overload**,
briefly leaving three signatures in the database.

**Open, recorded not fixed.** **§7.6** — trend frequency is emergent and **not
guaranteed** (~1–2 per exam; zero is realistic) while the NCSBN plan targets ~10%
stand-alone clinical-judgment items; the real gap is that we have never defined
*which* of our formats count as clinical-judgment. **§7.7** — the three-rung
ladder means the exam stops visibly responding to a strong student, and the §9.5
readiness probability **saturates at ~100%**; theta-capping was considered and
rejected (it would poison Slice 10's recalibration, which infers item difficulty
from student ability), and 1.0 is a *seeding artifact*, not a design ceiling.
**Slice 10's priority is raised** accordingly. **§16.6** — what the runner must
HIDE in exam mode, chiefly the **difficulty chip**, which is a live readout of the
engine's opinion of the candidate; deferred as one coherent pass (Sam).
**⚠ §8 is built on the superseded 2023 test plan** — the 2026 plan renames a
subcategory and shifts the Client Needs percentages; flagged, not patched.

**⏭ NEXT:** Slice 7 (the results page — students still hit the generic "30 of 85"
popup), then 6c (the spinner/Retry escalation), the §16.6 exam-mode pass, and
Slices 8–9. ⚠ `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on the prod Worker; the
`main → prod` release backlog now also carries five CAT migrations.

## Session — 2026-07-18 — CAT plan deep-read + the case-scoring HYBRID decision + worked-examples recovery + build slice plan (docs-only; build starts next session)

Branch `claude/work-session-f532b2`. **No product code** — a planning/decisions
session ahead of starting the CAT build next session. Four docs-only commits on
the branch, **none on `main`** (await Sam's approval as usual). We read the CAT
plan (`bank-consumption-cat.html`) together in the browser and worked the one
genuinely-open area (case/wrapper scoring) to a firmer landing.

**1. Walked the whole CAT plan with Sam** (browser, `file://` render). Covered
the concept, the honesty constraints (§3 — verdict against OUR standard, never a
real-NCLEX pass claim until outcome data), the Rasch engine (§4, incl. §4.5
partial-credit-as-fraction), calibration (§5), selection (§7), and the testlet
case model (§7.4). Confirmed the CAT design is settled and build-ready.

**2. Recovered the testlet worked examples that were referenced but never
committed.** §7.4 pointed at "the 2026-07-16 planning session notes" for two
worked examples — but no such notes were in the repo (no 2026-07-16 session
entry; the cloud branch `remote-session-zo5yrp` was deleted). Traced it: the
testlet *decisions* DID reconcile to `main` correctly (commits `32d7271` /
`93ba9ff` / `f32b251` confirmed on `origin/main`); only the illustrative
*worked examples* were the gap — they lived only in a claude.ai cloud session
(`session_016jHF…`). Read that session via the in-app Browser and recovered
**both** examples (I had wrongly said the calc was never worked and that the
"three cases" example didn't exist — both were wrong; Sam was right). Folded
them in-doc as **§7.4.1** (`5ef92f2`): the **Kojo** example (a case mid-exam,
0.70→0.75), the **Kofi** example (three cases across a full exam — early case
*sets* the level, late case only *nudges* it → the "never at position 1" rule),
plus an explicit statement of the merge method (confidence-weighted average +
inflate the case's uncertainty so it counts as ~one). The dangling pointer is
retired; §7.4 is now self-contained.

**3. New design decision — the HYBRID is the chosen case-scoring build; the
testlet is retained as the alternative** (`0e52661`). Sam proposed serving a
case's 6 children in fixed order but nudging the **main** level directly; I
flagged that pure form re-introduces the over-confidence / local-independence
problem the testlet solved. We converged on the **hybrid**: each case child
updates the main level directly but **counts as HALF a normal question** (halves
BOTH the level-move and the certainty-gain), so a 6-child case ≈ **3 questions'
worth**, not 6. Advantages over the testlet: one ability track (no separate
estimate, no merge), no noisy fresh-0.0 start, and the certainty folds in
automatically (the testlet's most error-prone step disappears). The "half" is a
starting default = an assumption about how much a case's children overlap
(`effective ≈ 6 ÷ (1 + 5·overlap)`; half ≈ ~3), tuned from data via the §5/§17
recalibration pipeline. Clinical-judgment read preserved as a **display-only**
calc from the 6 answers. Updated §7.4 (mechanism, "two shapes" line,
clinical-judgment + schema notes), §7.4.1 (3-way method comparison on Kojo:
naive-6 vs testlet vs hybrid), doc-meta, and the §19 settled entry.

**4. Recorded the ordered build slice plan — §19.4** (`b7b675b`). 10 slices / 4
phases: **A** schema migration (§12.7, the entry point, dev-only, additive) →
**B** engine (Rasch math pure+tested → `create_cat_attempt` →
`cat_next_item` standalone → case/trend hybrid) → **C** student experience
(runner → results/summary → review wiring → dashboard/entry points) → **D**
recalibration job (trails; not needed to launch). Flagged the two build
decisions to lock at the engine phase (engine runtime: PL/pgSQL RPC vs TS edge,
§10.5; confirm the hybrid "half" default) and the back-end-vs-browser
verification split. Dev CAT test pool (2,087 `CATPREP` items) + the runtime
option shuffle are already done, so the build opens at Slice 1.

**⏭ NEXT SESSION = start the CAT build at Slice 1 (the §12.7 schema migration,
dev only).** ⚠ These four doc commits still need a `main` merge (Sam's
approval). `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` (live key) still not on prod — launch-day only.

commit flagged the **runtime option shuffle as an ASAP prerequisite** before any
authored bank content goes student-facing — which became the session's main build.

**2. Runtime option shuffle — the CAT §19 prerequisite — BUILT across EVERY
shuffleable type.** The problem: authored content (esp. the machine-authored CAT
pool) clusters correct answers at the front (A/B/C…), and nothing shuffled at
runtime — `option_order_json`/`shuffle_seed` were stamped `'{}'`/NULL and unused.
Design invariant throughout: `content_snapshot_json` stays the honest authored
record; `option_order_json` holds a **display permutation**; the runner relabels
letters **positionally** (1st shown = A) while the option/choice/token **id** stays
the value for picks, feedback, and scoring — so **scoring is never touched** (it
already keys on id). Shipped in slices, each dev-applied + verified live in Sam's
session then merged:
  - **Slice 1 — generation** (`a851ce4`, mig `20260804120000`): a pure
    `nclex_build_option_order()` (md5-seeded permutation) + a **BEFORE INSERT
    trigger** `nclex_attempt_items_shuffle_trg` — one trigger covers ALL five
    attempt-create paths (bank / readiness / tutor-quiz ×2 / note-embed) with **no
    edits to the big create RPCs**. Honours per-item `shuffle_options` (default
    TRUE). MCQ/SATA/SELECT_N only. DB-verified (distinct per-item permutations;
    TF/MATRIX/HIGHLIGHT correctly skipped).
  - **Slice 2 — runner consumes it** (`9d9d566`): pure `lib/practice/runner/
    option-order.ts` (`orderedOptions` + `optionLetter`, +tests); dispatcher
    `runner-question-area.tsx` reorders MCQ/SATA/SELECT_N; the 3 renderers show a
    positional letter. Verified live: shuffled order matched the DB, scored 3/3,
    review consistent, order frozen across reload.
  - **Slice 3a — drag pools** (`448981e`, mig `20260805120000`): DRAG_CLOZE +
    DRAG_ORDER shuffle `content.tokens`; **DRAG_ORDER anti-giveaway guard** (Sam's
    option a) re-seeds until the pool's answer-token subsequence ≠ the canonical
    rank order (bounded, exception-safe). Reused `orderedOptions` on tokens; no
    renderer edits. Verified live: leaky no-distractor CAT item protected;
    placement + 5/5 scoring.
  - **Slice 3b — multi-pool** (`32c4945`, mig `20260806120000`): CLOZE + BOWTIE
    hold several pools, so `option_order_json` goes **object-keyed** (CLOZE
    `{blankId:[…]}`, BOWTIE `{left/centre/right:[…]}`); builder emits the object,
    trigger stamp accepts a non-empty object; dispatcher reorders each pool. No
    renderer edits, no guard. Verified live: CLOZE per-blank dropdowns matched DB
    + scored 2/2; BOWTIE all three wings matched DB.
  - **CAT §19 doc** marked BUILT (`9ed262f`).

**3. Embed player option shuffle — option B (persist)** (`4995b14`, mig
`20260807120000`). Discussed at length *why* the attempt-side fix didn't reach the
in-note player: **both halves are bolted to the attempt pipeline** (trigger on
`nclex_attempt_items` + the attempt dispatcher), while the embed player is a
separate path (reads bank content live, logs to `nclex_library_embed_answers`).
The shared runner components don't shuffle — the caller's prep step does, and the
embed player has its own. **Decision (Sam): keep it off the attempts table
(deliberate) and give it its own shuffle, persisting the order (option B) for
replay-exactness + future types.** Added `option_order_json` to the embed table;
shared `seededOptionOrder`/`embedOptionOrder`/`withOptionOrder` helpers; the order
is **deterministic on `play_id`+`item_id`** — the client computes it to render, the
server RE-DERIVES the same order and persists it (no wire transfer, no client
trust). Honours `shuffle_options`; scoring by id. MCQ/SATA/SELECT_N; NULL =
authored order. Verified live on the real "Normal Sinus Rhythm" note embed: display
== persisted `["D","A","B","C"]` == server-derived, differed from authored order,
answer stored by id + scored correct, past-sitting review replayed the stored
order. Doc note added to `tutor-library.md`.

**The ENTIRE option-shuffle arc is complete** — every shuffleable type on the
attempt runner + the embed player. Excluded by design: TF (2 fixed options),
MATRIX/MATRIX_MR (shared-header grid), HIGHLIGHT (inline prose).

## Session — 2026-07-13 — READINESS LAZY-EXPIRY — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-5efb6c`. Commit `6b9d15d`. **App-layer, NO
migration** (the `expired_at` column + the one-live-claim unique index already
existed — only the *writer* of the stamp changed). tsc + eslint clean; vitest
169 → **176**. On `main`, **not prod**.

**The last designed readiness slice.** A readiness credit's 21-day window has a
frozen deadline (`expires_at`, stamped at activation) but nothing wrote the lapse
*event* (`expired_at`). The retired plan was a nightly cron; the settled design
(2026-07-11) is to stamp **lazily, on the next touch** of the row — a packs-page
read, and (correctness-critical) at re-claim time — mirroring the `/session`
runner's existing lazy-expiry.

**Why it's correctness-critical, not cosmetic.** The
`idx_nclex_readiness_credits_one_live_claim_per_pack` unique index counts a claim
as live unless `expired_at` (or `revoked_at`) is set. So a lapsed-but-unstamped
credit still occupies the `(user, pack)` slot, and re-claiming that pack with a
fresh credit `23505`'d — wrongly refused as "you already hold this pack" — until
the old row is stamped. The claim actions now stamp first, freeing the slot.

**Built (all app-layer):**
- `lib/payments/readiness-credits.ts` — new pure `isLapsedLive(credit, nowMs)`
  (activated · not terminal · `expires_at` in the past); corrected the stale
  "nightly sweep" comments to lazy-stamp-on-touch. +7 unit tests.
- `lib/payments/readiness-expiry.ts` **(new)** —
  `sweepLapsedReadinessCredits(userId, packId?)`, a service-role write (credits
  have no student-write RLS). Idempotent + race-safe by its WHERE clause
  (re-checks activated / not-terminal / deadline-past, satisfying the
  expiry-requires-activation + one-ending CHECKs); best-effort (returns 0 on
  error, caller carries on).
- `lib/payments/readiness-packs-view.ts` — sweeps before the read (self-heals
  the row) **and** uses `isLapsedLive` in the stage derivation so the card flips
  to lapsed/claimable even in the rare render where the best-effort write didn't
  land. One clock read (`now`) reused throughout.
- `lib/payments/readiness-claim.ts` — folds the stamp into both actions:
  `claimReadinessPack` scoped to the target pack (after pack validation, before
  finding the fresh credit), `claimAllReadiness` before its read.

**Scope call (with Sam, pre-build):** the abandoned-sitting finalisation (a
sitting whose 3h20m clock ran out while the student was away) is **left to the
existing `/session` lazy-expiry**, not folded into the packs page — the packs
page has no need to finalise attempts, and the SITTING→USED flip already
self-heals on the next session load. `getMyCredits` stays a pure reader (one
write path per surface).

**Verification.** tsc + eslint clean; full vitest 176 pass. No student was
logged into the dev browser and I can't enter a password, so I proved the
mechanism at the DB level against a real backdated ACTIVE credit: (A) the exact
sweep predicate stamps **only** the lapsed row — USED / unactivated credits
untouched, no CHECK violation; (B) idempotent (0 rows on a second run); (C)
re-claim **succeeds** once the stamp lands; (D) the same claim onto a still-live
pack **collides (23505)** — the exact bug the stamp prevents. Then **Sam's
browser test** exercised the real code path: logging in as the tutor account and
opening `/student/bank/packs` fired the sweep and stamped `expired_at` on his
lapsed Pack-2 credit (confirmed in the DB immediately after). All dev test data
was set up and **fully restored** afterwards (tutor: 3 unclaimed + 2 USED;
superadmin: 5 unclaimed; zero residue).

**Session-start gotcha (fixed):** the worktree's dev server failed to boot —
Turbopack `couldn't find next/package.json`. Cause: a stale empty `node_modules/`
(just a `.vite` vitest cache) in the worktree made `next.config.ts`
`findProjectRoot()` stop there instead of walking up to the parent repo's
packages. Removed the stray dir → dev serves cleanly on `:3000`.

**State.** The **whole designed readiness build is now complete on `main`** —
buy → claim → activate 21-day window → one-shot sit → permanent report → honest
lapse handling. Nothing designed remains. §12 markers reconciled (five stale
`🔨`/`⬜` flags for already-shipped slices flipped to ✅). **⏭ NEXT = prod-release
work only:** a `main→prod` release (ships the whole readiness + time-engine
backlog that's accumulated on `main`) + `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` on the prod Worker
to un-dormant readiness. The only genuinely-unbuilt §12 item is the *parked*
products-catalogue promotions columns (`compare_at_price` / coupon codes) — not
a readiness slice, and its strike-through "was price" capability already shipped
as `full_price_minor_*`.

## Session — 2026-07-12 (later) — READINESS REPORT ⑦ (Polish + QA) — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/priceless-cori-3a6ca1`. Commit `824231c`. App-layer, **no
migration**. tsc clean; `report-view.tsx` eslint-clean. On `main`, **not prod**.

**Context.** Report slices ①–⑥ shipped 2026-07-11 with two visible
placeholders (the Pacing card, the per-question map's time bits). The
per-question time engine (this morning's arc) filled them, so — per Sam's
2026-07-11 sequencing call — ⑦ polishes the **complete** report, not one built
around placeholders. Sam scoped ⑦ to **all four areas** (mobile reflow +
open-anchor settle + copy sync + animation), judgment on the animation (no CD
prototype re-share needed).

**QA sweep first.** Walked the report on the real sitting
`38d3c02c-0220-4d4d-aea9-791d5e0aa9de` (Pack-1 51.8%, 69 answers carrying real
`time_spent_sec`) at desktop + 375px, all six accordion sections, drilling the
focus tree (L1→L2→L3 topic checklist) and tapping map cells. Confirmed the
time-engine surfaces are genuinely live: the Trend & pacing card ("2m 7s avg
per answered", You-vs-exam-pace bars), the map's **Rushed · 11** filter, and
per-cell **Time spent** (e.g. Q11 "1m 47s"; "—" on untimed questions).

**Five fixes** — all in `report-view.tsx` + `styles/readiness-student.css`:

1. **Open-anchor settle.** `openSection` scrolled `window` / read
   `window.scrollY`, but the app shell scrolls an inner `.product-content`
   container (its own sticky-mini-verdict code even says so) — so the
   scroll-into-view was a **silent no-op**, and opening a lower section left its
   header scrolled off the top (you landed mid-section, worst on mobile). Fixed
   by finding `el.closest('.product-content')` and scrolling that; settles the
   section under the sticky mini-verdict (mobile, offset 64) / near the top
   (desktop, offset 16), with a small guard against re-scroll jitter.

2. **Mobile top-gap.** `.rs-rep-mini` (the sticky mini-verdict) is
   `display:flex; position:sticky` on mobile even when hidden — a sticky
   **in-flow** element still reserves its full height, leaving ~50–80px of dead
   space at the top of the report. Collapsed the hidden state to `height:0;
   overflow:hidden` (padding/border/shadow zeroed), restored on `.is-shown`.
   The slide-in still animates via opacity + translateY; the height snap
   happens off-screen (anchored at the scrolled-away top).

3. **Mobile header-crush.** `.rs-rep-sec-head` is a nowrap flex row (caret +
   title/sub + right-summary); a long summary ("Weakest: Reduction of Risk
   Potential") squeezed the title into a ~40%-width column, wrapping "Where to
   focus" to three lines. Added `flex-wrap:wrap` + made the summary
   `flex-basis:100%; text-align:left; margin-left:22px` so it drops to its own
   full-width line, indented under the title. Title stays on one line.

4. **Latent reveal-clip.** `@keyframes rr-open` animated `max-height: 0 →
   2600px` while `.rs-rep-sec` is `overflow:hidden` — so any section taller
   than the 2600px cap (a fully-drilled focus tree, the mobile per-question
   map) would be **permanently clipped**. Replaced the height animation with
   opacity + a 6px slide — no ceiling, and a cleaner reveal.

5. **Entrance choreography.** New `rr-rise` keyframe: rail cards
   (`.rs-rep-rail > *`) then accordion sections (`.rs-rep-main > .rs-rep-sec`)
   rise in on load with staggered `animation-delay`. Pure CSS (fires on first
   paint, no invisible-until-hydrate risk); disabled in the reduced-motion
   block.

**Verified live** on `38d3c02c`: desktop open-anchor (clicked rail nav
"Per-question map" → section scrolls to top), mobile top-gap closed ("Back to
packs" flush under the top bar), mobile header no longer crushed, mobile
open-anchor settles, sections render fully (no clip). No console errors.

**Copy-sync — analysed, mostly left alone.** Band + % are already consistent
across the USED card (`ResultBlock`) and the report — both go through
`scoreToBand`. The one real inconsistency is the **shared universal results
popup** (`lib/practice/runner/results-popup.tsx`): its readiness variant shows
`%` + `finalScore × totalQ` ("51.8 of 100 correct") + **no band**, and that
fractional number matches *no* number on the report ("49 fully correct" /
"153 of 284 answer-points"). **Parked as a design decision** — the popup is
universal across all quiz sources, so changing it (e.g. show band + % like the
card/report) is Sam's call. **Days-left** (`Math.ceil`, `report-view.ts:48`):
first read as a cross-surface mismatch, but `readiness-packs-view.ts:168` and
`entitlements.ts:38` use the **same** formula — so all surfaces agree at any
instant; the 21↔20 I saw across two loads was `ceil` ticking as the window
crosses its activation-time-of-day. Left as-is.

**Lint note.** Repo-wide `npm run lint` is currently red on **pre-existing**
errors in files this session didn't touch (the `.claude/skills` transcribe
scripts; `session/[attempt_id]/page.tsx`, checkout pages, `sidebar-frame`,
`programme-switcher-overlay`, `activity-modal` — React-Compiler-style
`Date.now`/`set-state-in-effect`/`prefer-const` rules). None are ours
(`report-view.tsx` lints clean); flagged to Sam for a separate cleanup pass.

**Merge.** Sam approved → FF-merged into `main` from the primary worktree
(`git -C <primary> merge --ff-only`, then `push origin main`);
`71328e1..824231c`. Local + origin `main` in sync (no staleness).

## Session — 2026-07-12 — PER-QUESTION TIME ENGINE (Slices 1–3) + readiness report improvements — MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-8404b4`. Two arcs, both on `main` at wrap (not yet prod).

**Design-first.** Sam reframed the "time engine" correctly: it's **not** a
readiness feature but a **runner / attempt-level** capability — the readiness
report is only its first *reader*. Spec written into
**bank-consumption-attempt-creation.html §6.3.2** (deliberately NOT the readiness
doc) BEFORE any code: per-question **engaged** time (pauses on screen-away via the
Page-Visibility signal; wrapper-tab switches / scroll do NOT pause — only
whole-screen departures), **independent of the attempt clock** (two clocks, they
don't sum), monotonic-duration trust (send deltas not timestamps), additive
persistence, and skipped-but-read capture. **Settled after the Slice-1 test** (a
sub-thread Sam drove): the number means "total engaged time while the attempt is
LIVE" (stops dead at completion; UL mid-sitting rationale/revisit counting is by
design, not a bug — untimed study, no pacing run on it); storage is the single
`time_spent_sec` integer, **not** a per-visit segment log (considered + rejected;
accepted trade-off — strict *decision-time* pacing, if ever built, is a separate
future capture).

**Time engine — 3 slices:**
- **Slice 1 — capture (`ad493cf`, mig `20260803120000`).** Pure engaged-segment
  accumulator `lib/practice/runner/time-tracker.ts` (sibling to `clock.ts`; 11
  unit tests) + a React hook `use-question-timer` wiring it to the runner
  (visibility pause/resume, flush on nav / page-hide / unmount, `flushActive()`
  awaited before finish). Persist via new additive RPC `nclex_add_answer_time`
  (COALESCE+delta, clamped to the attempt budget, **silent** no-op once terminal
  so a late flush racing finish is quiet). Read-then-skip captured via a
  DRAFT+NULL-answer placeholder that `_flushDrafts` skips and
  `nclex_complete_attempt`/`nclex_expire_attempt` (both recreated) convert to
  SKIPPED preserving the time. Sam-tested live (10-Q UL — all types captured,
  additive revisit accumulation confirmed, the one "null" was a live flush not
  yet landed). I also drove a 5-Q Untimed-Test sitting in the browser myself:
  read-then-skip Q4 → SKIPPED with **31s kept**, last-Q flush landed on Finish,
  scoring unchanged (**60% verified**), no lingering DRAFT rows.
- **Slice 2 — readiness report reads real time (`471d524`).** New pure
  `lib/payments/readiness-pacing.ts` (budget = duration ÷ count, rushed =
  <0.6×budget ≈ the CD prototype's 70s, avg over answered, wall-clock "to spare";
  6 tests). `readiness-report.ts` adds `timeSpentSec`+`rushed` to `ReportItem` +
  a `PacingStats`. Per-question map: real "Time spent" (+ "· rushed" tag) + the
  disabled "Rushed · soon" chip became a real **Rushed · N** filter. Trend &
  pacing: the placeholder replaced by the designed **Pacing card** (avg per
  answered Q + You-vs-exam-pace bars + "finished with N to spare · M answered"),
  graceful fallback for pre-engine sittings. Built from the "report page final
  design" CD prototype (Sam re-supplied it). Verified on dev with a seeded time
  spread (avg 2m 7s, 11 rushed, all rendered).
- **Slice 3 — in-note embed player parity (`bd60fb8`).** Reuses the pure
  time-tracker; **single-shot** (the embed write is append-only → no RPC, no
  migration) — measures engaged time per embedded question and passes it to the
  existing (previously null-fed) `timeSpentSec` slot. Feeds the deferred tutor
  "time-on-block" analytics metric.

**Then report improvements** (Sam driving the app on-screen; all app-layer, on `main`):
- **Beat + Pace in the rail glance card** (`c7d7c0e`) — restored two CD glance
  rows now that the data exists (peer percentile + avg pace); each hides
  gracefully (Beat below peer min-N, Pace on pre-engine sittings). "Since Pack 1"
  **dropped** (the Trend card already shows that delta).
- **Review surfaced** (`bf27fd0`) — the Review-window card moved to the **top** of
  the rail + a primary "Review your answers →" button (launch was buried in the
  map cell detail / a collapsed accordion / a next-move link). The independent
  Review-window accordion section left untouched.
- **Page top bar** (`f3f7551`) — back link + "Pack N · date" title lifted out of
  the rail/verdict into a full-width bar → **the two panes' first cards now align**
  (the back link had offset only the rail) + the verdict card opens clean on the
  ring. Verified aligned (both first cards at the same Y) desktop + 375px.
- **Peer histogram Y-axis** (`2a3c88a` + `c4b4e4f`) — the distribution had no
  Y-axis (bars normalised, counts only on hover). Added a **nurse-count Y-axis**
  (0 / mid / nice-top ticks) + the **count printed on each bar**; foot reworded;
  the "N nurses have sat this pack" **cohort-size line dropped** (Sam). Grounded
  in real data (Pack-1 = 32 nurses, bell peaking at 11).
- **Shell scrollbar-gutter fix** (`bd55f90`) — `.product-content` (the shell's
  shared scroller) used `scrollbar-gutter: auto`, so content width jumped **15px**
  when the scrollbar appeared/disappeared (most visible expanding/collapsing the
  report accordions). Set `scrollbar-gutter: stable` **app-wide**. Investigated +
  reproduced in-browser with a fixed edge-marker (edge 1368→1375 on `auto`, vs
  1368→1368 on `stable`); note it only manifests when the *collapsed* page fits
  the viewport but an *expanded* accordion overflows.

tsc + eslint clean throughout (runner.tsx's 13 lint errors pre-existing); vitest
**152 → 169** (+11 tracker, +6 pacing). ⚠ **dev cleanup candidate:** a synthetic
`time_spent_sec` spread was seeded onto attempt `38d3c02c` (Pack-1 52%) so the
report showed real pace/rushed/peer while reviewing — revert to null when done.
Peer `_peertest` rows still present. `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` still not on prod.
**NOT released to prod** — the time-engine migration `20260803120000` + all these
app changes ride the next `main → prod`. ⏭ NEXT = Report ⑦ polish/QA; the
per-question time now also unblocks CAT.

## Session — 2026-07-11 — READINESS RESULTS REPORT (step 3): slices ①–⑥ BUILT + Sam-tested + MERGED to `main`

Branch `claude/work-session-fbecf8`. Built the permanent per-sitting **readiness
results report** (§11.5) — the destination behind "See your full report" that
2b-iv left as a placeholder. **Design → plan → build in one session**, from the
CD **v3 Responsive** prototype (concept-not-source, rebuilt in app tokens). All
app-layer bar the peer slice's ONE migration; **tsc + eslint + 152 vitest clean
throughout**; each slice Sam-tested on `localhost` desktop **and 375px** before
the next.

**Design pass first.** Sam had CD generate 4 layout variants (v1 story-scroll ·
v2 tabs · v3 rail+collapsible-sections · v4 rail+panes). Viewed all four rendered
(local static server); **chose v3** — the full story reorganised into a scannable
accordion whose *collapsed* summaries keep everything visible (not hidden behind
clicks like v2/v4). Reviewed CD's **responsive** pass at 375px (sticky
mini-verdict, single-column accordion, under-row map detail, two bug fixes) — I'd
initially under-rated v3 vs v4 and corrected on Sam's push. §12 slice plan
recorded before building.

**The build (7 slices; ①–⑥ done):**
